# Corner-Case Catalog

> Design document (2026-07-22). Every case has an ID (`CC-<area>-<n>`), the
> expected server behavior, and a **When** column tying it to the roadmap phase
> whose exit gate must cover it (test, design decision, or live probe).
> [roadmap.md](roadmap.md) references these IDs; a phase does not exit while one
> of its cases is unhandled. A `[verify — needs a live call: …]` marker means Meta's
> public docs were checked and do **not** answer it; the marker must always name the
> exact call that would settle it, so it can be discharged mechanically. A marker
> that does not say how to discharge it is a bug in this document. Facts settled
> from official docs carry *[verified &lt;date&gt; — source, url]* instead.

Legend for **How covered**: `unit` = deterministic test with mocked fetch/clock ·
`design` = must be decided in the design, then unit-tested · `live` = live-API
probe protocol (junk account) · `docs` = documented behavior/limitation.

A **When** of `post-M5` marks a case that was not foreseen in the original design
but found afterwards by the mutation audit of 2026-08-26 (633 mutants across
`core/log.ts`, `core/host.ts`, `core/auth.ts`, `mcp/define.ts`, `tools/index.ts`).
These are recorded here for the same reason as the rest — so the behaviour is
decided on purpose rather than inherited — and each one names whether it is fixed,
pending a decision, or accepted.

**Where a row lives — and why that is not where you would look.** §1–§7 group the
originally designed cases by area, and each heading names the prefix that dominates
it. Those sections are **not exhaustive for their prefix.** Every case found after the
sectioning was set is appended at the *end of §8*, in discovery order, carrying
whatever prefix its subject belongs to — so §8 today holds 503 of the 618 rows the
catalogue defines, and 293 of those carry a prefix other than the `CC-PROC` its heading
advertises: 106 `CC-DATA`, 53 `CC-AUTH`, 61 `CC-CFG`, 35 `CC-PUB`, 19 `CC-INS`,
11 `CC-COM` and 8 `CC-RATE`. That is deliberate on both counts: chronology is the more
useful index for what is really an audit log, and moving a row would break the
line-anchored references that source comments and the other documents point at.
**To find every row of one prefix, grep the ID — do not read a section.**
Nothing downstream resolves a case by section number; 1605 citation sites across the
repo name rows by ID alone, and `test/docs-catalog.test.ts` pins that every one of
them still resolves.

## 1. Auth & tokens (CC-AUTH)

| ID | Scenario | Expected behavior | When / how |
|---|---|---|---|
| CC-AUTH-1 | Token expires mid-session (day 60 passes while the server is running) | Next call maps 190 → `kind: auth` with remediation ("run `refresh` / `login`"); no retry storm | M1 · unit |
| CC-AUTH-2 | Path-A refresh called on a token < 24 h old | Meta rejects it; server knows the obtained-at timestamp and refuses locally with a clear "too new to refresh" message instead of burning a call | M2 · unit |
| CC-AUTH-3 | Path-A refresh attempted on an already-expired token | Refresh cannot resurrect it; error says a full `login` is required | M2 · unit |
| CC-AUTH-4 | Token supplied via client `env`, auto-refresh persists to XDG — refreshed token never takes effect (the refresh-persistence trap) | **Design gate D2** (see roadmap): resolved before M2; until then auto-refresh is disabled for client-env tokens and `token_status` warns | M2 · design |
| CC-AUTH-5 | ~~Both `IG_ACCESS_TOKEN` and `IG_FB_ACCESS_TOKEN` set, no `IG_AUTH_MODE`~~ **RETIRED 2026-07-29** — designed out. There is one token var for both paths (`IG_ACCESS_TOKEN`), so the ambiguity cannot arise. What replaces it: an unset token is a hard startup error, and an `IG_AUTH_MODE` value that is neither `ig-login` nor `fb-login` is rejected by name rather than guessed | Retired (see workplan §3) | — |
| CC-AUTH-6 | `IG_ACCOUNT_ID` does not match the account the token resolves to (`/me`) | `doctor` flags the mismatch; tools fail fast with both IDs in the message | M1 · unit |
| CC-AUTH-7 | Token pasted manually (never went through `login`) — no exchange metadata, and Path A has no `debug_token` to recover expiry | `token_status` reports expiry **unknown** honestly, recommends re-acquiring via `login`; never invents a date | M1 · unit |
| CC-AUTH-8 | IG account converted professional → personal while the server holds a token | Graph starts erroring (100/190-class); mapped error explains the account-type requirement, points at the conversion doc | M1 · docs + unit |
| CC-AUTH-9 | Path-A user changes Instagram password / revokes the app | Token dies before expiry date; same handling as CC-AUTH-1 — metadata-based expiry is a hint, not a guarantee | M1 · docs |
| CC-AUTH-10 | Token granted before a scope was added to the app (scope drift) | 10/200-series → `kind: permission` naming the missing scope **and** the fix (re-run `login` to re-consent) | M1 · unit |
| CC-AUTH-11 | Path-B system-user token whose Business assets don't include the target Page/IG account | Permission error names the asset-assignment step, not just the scope | M1 · docs + unit |
| CC-AUTH-12 | Path B: `data_access_expires_at` (90-day data-access window) expires independently of token validity | `doctor`/`token_status` surface it separately; nearing expiry produces its own warning | M1 · unit |
| CC-AUTH-13 | Local clock wrong / DST shift → "days left" and refresh-threshold math skewed | All time math goes through the injectable clock; warnings state the absolute expiry timestamp, not only "N days left" | M1 · unit |
| CC-AUTH-14 | Two server instances (e.g. two Claude windows) both trigger Path-A auto-refresh concurrently | Refresh is guarded: re-read the env file before refreshing (another instance may have already rotated it); atomic write prevents interleaved corruption. Whether Meta invalidates the old token on refresh: `[verify — needs a live call: refresh once via GET https://graph.instagram.com/refresh_access_token?grant_type=ig_refresh_token, then replay a cheap read (GET /me?fields=user_id) with the OLD token and record whether it still succeeds or maps to 190]`. Meta's `refresh_access_token` reference documents the preconditions and the new token but is **silent on the old token's fate** *[checked 2026-07-30 — https://developers.facebook.com/docs/instagram-platform/reference/refresh_access_token]* | M2 · design + live |

| CC-AUTH-15 | Path-B `appsecret_proof` is computed once at construction and carries no `appsecret_time` | The proof is therefore **static for the token's lifetime**: anyone who observes one outgoing query string holds a proof that replays indefinitely. Graph supports the time-bound form (`appsecret_proof` + `appsecret_time`); adopting it costs one extra parameter and bounds the replay window. **Open — owner's decision, found post-release** | post-M5 · design |
| CC-AUTH-16 | Path-B app secret configured as whitespace only (`'   '`, or a value with a stray trailing newline) | `if (!appSecret)` catches `undefined` and `''` but not `'   '`, so a valid-looking but wrong proof is computed and **every** Graph call fails with an opaque server-side error instead of a clear local one. Expected: reject a secret that is blank after trimming, and name the env var that fixes it | post-M5 · unit |
| CC-AUTH-17 | Path-A token endpoints are addressed inconsistently: `cli/login.ts` calls `https://graph.instagram.com/access_token` — the only **unversioned** Graph URL in the project — while `core/refresh.ts` goes through `buildUrl` and gets `https://graph.instagram.com/v25.0/refresh_access_token` | One of the two is not honouring the contract `GRAPH_VERSION` claims to pin. An unversioned endpoint silently follows Meta's default version, so the login exchange can change shape under the server without a single line of the repo changing. Expected: decide which form Meta actually documents for each endpoint and make both deliberate. **Open — needs the live probe (T-E1) to confirm** | post-M5 · live |
| CC-AUTH-18 | A profile name containing a character that is legal in a name but not in a POSIX identifier (`Second-Studio`) | `envVarFor` yields `IG_PROFILE_SECOND-STUDIO_APP_SECRET`; a `.env` file accepts that key but `export` refuses it, so an error message telling the operator to set the variable names something they cannot set the normal way. Pinned as-is by test rather than silently renamed — changing the scheme would break every existing named profile | post-M5 · unit |
| CC-AUTH-19 | `debug_token` returns an `expires_at` no `Date` can represent | `summarizeTokenExpiry` multiplied the wire value by 1000 and handed it straight to `new Date(...).toISOString()`, which raises a **bare `RangeError: Invalid time value`** for `NaN`, `±Infinity`, or any magnitude past ±8.64e15 ms. Reachability was proven, not argued: `JSON.parse('{"expires_at":1e400}')` yields `Infinity` — `1e400` is valid JSON and there is nowhere else for it to go — so one truncated numeric literal upstream is enough; `NaN` arrives instead from a corrupt or hand-edited credential record. A `RangeError` is not an `InstagramError`, so no layer above classifies it, and it escaped a **pure** function into `doctor` and `token_status` — the two diagnostics an operator runs *because* they already suspect the token. Fixed as `state: 'unknown'` with a warning naming the offending value, guarded **before** the state decision rather than merely around `toISOString`: had only the throw been silenced, `NaN <= nowMs` and `NaN <= refreshAfterDays` are both false, so the value would have fallen through to a plainly `valid` verdict — a worse outcome than the crash. Both guard clauses are load-bearing (`Math.abs` catches the magnitudes including `±Infinity`, but never `NaN`). Neither `ExpiryState` nor any output field changed. 23 → 35 tests, including an end-to-end pass through the real `debugToken` with `1e400` on the wire and a 500-run `fast-check` property proving totality over arbitrary doubles. **The same unguarded expression survived at three other call sites** — `src/tools/account.ts` (`data_access_expires_at`, the same handler, so `token_status` still fell over after the api-layer fix), `expiryLabel` in `src/index.ts` and `expiryLine` in `src/cli/login.ts`; closed separately, see CC-AUTH-20 | post-M5 · unit |
| CC-AUTH-20 | The same unrepresentable-timestamp expression at the three call sites CC-AUTH-19 left open | `new Date(sec * 1000).toISOString()` survived in `src/tools/account.ts` (`data_access_expires_at`), `expiryLabel` in `src/index.ts` and `expiryLine` in `src/cli/login.ts` — so the api-layer fix moved the crash rather than closing it, and `token_status` still fell over on the very field the same handler returns. **Closed 2026-09-02** by extracting the guard into a Layer-0 helper, `src/core/time.ts`, reachable from `tools/`, `index.ts` and `cli/` under the layering rules; `src/api/account.ts` keeps its own copy because Layer 1 must not depend on Layer 0's, and the duplication is documented in the module header rather than hidden. The contract was decided per site rather than uniformly, on one rule: **a guard that turns unusable data into a plausible timestamp is worse than the `RangeError` it replaced** — an operator reading `expires at 1970-01-01` believes it. The tool result therefore omits `dataAccessExpiresAt` entirely and appends a `warning` naming the offending value — never `null`, never an `Invalid Date` placeholder — because omission alone would make "Meta said nothing" and "Meta said nonsense" identical to the model, and the frozen tool surface has no room for a new field. Both CLI renders say `unknown` **and** why, and `login` still exits 0: the old throw turned a fully successful login into `login failed: Invalid time value` after the credential was already on disk. Mutation found two survivors that mattered. The magnitude boundary had only ever been probed a whole *second* past the limit, which overshoots by 1000 and cannot separate `MAX + 1`; it is now pinned at millisecond granularity via `MAX_TIMESTAMP_SEC + 0.001`, asserted to multiply out to exactly `8_640_000_000_000_001` with no float slop. And `expiresAtSec === 0` versus `<= 0` survived because nothing drove a **negative** absolute expiry through `runLogin` — reachable on a host whose clock predates 1970 (a container with no RTC), where an ordinary 60-day lifetime computes negative and the mutant reports an expiring credential as `never`, silencing every later expiry warning. 119 → 121 tests. A fourth render, `new Date(ctx.clock.now()).toISOString()` in `src/mcp/write-mode.ts`, is **not** this class — an in-process injected millisecond clock, not upstream data — and is left alone | post-M5 · unit |
| CC-AUTH-21 | A `client_secret` whose bytes are not URL-safe is logged **in the clear** | `createRedactor` masks the exact substrings it was handed, while `buildUrl` form-encodes what actually goes on the wire — so a secret containing `+`, `/` or `=` reaches the request line as `%2B`, `%2F` and `%3D`, a spelling that was never registered and that the redactor therefore finds nothing to mask. Measured, not argued: a probe with `client_secret=aa+bb/cc=dd` came back with the encoded value intact in the error message. Every existing leak test in the module used a fixture drawn from `[A-Za-z0-9]`, where the two spellings coincide — so the whole set **passed for the wrong reason** and would have gone on passing while a real secret leaked. Meta's app secrets are hex today, which is why this had never been seen; nothing in the contract promises they stay hex, and `IG_APP_SECRET` is whatever the operator pastes. Closed 2026-09-02 by registering both surface forms of every secret. A neighbouring mutant is recorded here because it is the inverse failure: relaxing the `value !== undefined` filter to `!== null` registers the **string** `"undefined"` as a secret — nine characters, past the eight-character minimum — and then masks the commonest word in upstream diagnostics, turning unrelated log lines into `[REDACTED]` noise | post-M5 · unit |
| CC-AUTH-22 | Two different `unknown` verdicts, one untested message, and the guard between them can be deleted | `summarizeTokenExpiry` answers `state: 'unknown'` twice, for two unrelated reasons: Path A, where the local credential record simply has no `expires_at` in it (CC-AUTH-7), and the unrepresentable-timestamp guard added for CC-AUTH-19. Both messages end in the same `login` CLI advice, and the only Path-A assertion was `warning.includes('login')` — satisfied by either. So deleting the `expiresAtSec === undefined` branch outright **survived the whole suite**: `undefined * 1000` is `NaN`, the `NaN` clause catches it, and the operator is told *upstream reported an `expires_at` of `undefined`* — a wire value invented for a field that was never on the wire, pointing the investigation at Meta instead of at the local record with no expiry metadata in it. Closed 2026-09-02 by asserting the Path-A sentence **exactly** rather than by substring. The general lesson is the reason this row exists: where two branches agree on the enum they return, the enum is not what a test may assert — the message is the only thing that separates them, and a substring match on the half they share asserts nothing. The neighbouring mutants were re-run at the same time and all die — `>` \| `>=` on the magnitude bound (the millisecond-granularity boundary test from CC-AUTH-20 catches it), dropping `Math.abs`, and dropping the `Number.isNaN` clause — so the guard itself needs nothing further | post-M5 · unit |
| CC-AUTH-23 | A dead token that arrives without a recognised code is retried three times and reported as `upstream` | `deriveKind` in `src/core/errors.ts` classifies by subcode, then by code, then by HTTP status. Meta marks every authentication failure with `error.type: 'OAuthException'` — but that field, declared as `GraphErrorBody.error.type` in `src/core/types.ts`, is read by **no source module**; only tests set it. So an `OAuthException` arriving at HTTP 400 with an absent or unrecognised `code` falls past both earlier rules into the status fallback, where 400 answers `upstream`. `isRetryableKind` in `src/core/http.ts` returns `idempotent` for `upstream`, so on any GET the request is then retried the full three times against a credential that will never work again, and the operator is handed a generic upstream failure with no instruction to re-authenticate — the one diagnosis that would end the outage. Found on 2026-09-02 during the module's mutation pass, not by a failing test: every existing `OAuthException` fixture carries a code the code rule already recognises. Recommendation on the table: consult `type === 'OAuthException'` as a tie-breaker immediately before the status fallback and map it to `auth`. It adds a rule, changes no exported type, and cannot disturb the numbered OAuthException codes (4, 17, 32 and friends) because those are decided earlier. Not applied — a change to the retry taxonomy is an owner call. **Fixed 2026-09-19.** Applied as recommended. `mapGraphError` reads `error.type` through the same `nonEmptyString` filter as the message fields and threads it into `deriveKind`, which takes it as rule 3 — after the subcode ladder, after the code ladder, before the HTTP-status fallback — mapping `type === 'OAuthException'` to `auth`. The placement is the whole design: Meta stamps `OAuthException` on throttles (4, 17, 32) and on permission failures (10, 200–299) as well, and those numbered codes are the more specific signal — a throttled token is not a dead one, and re-authenticating would not clear it — so the tie-breaker can only ever classify an envelope the code ladder declined. The match is exact and case-sensitive: `type` is Meta's own enumerated class name, spelled one way, and this envelope shape cannot rule out a proxy's or a gateway's own `type` field, so a lower-cased or padded near-miss is not promoted to "re-login"; a non-string `type` arrives as `undefined` through that same filter and falls through untouched, so the comparison needs no typeof guard of its own. Measured before the rule: four fetches, three backoffs, kind `upstream`; after: one fetch, kind `auth`, and `isRetryableKind` answers `never`. Six tests in `test/core/errors.test.ts` pin it — `an OAuthException with no code at HTTP 400 classifies as auth, not upstream`, `an OAuthException with an unrecognised code still classifies as auth`, `a recognised code outranks the OAuthException type: code 4 stays rate_limit`, `a non-OAuthException type with no code at HTTP 400 still falls through to upstream`, `the OAuthException match is exact: a case or whitespace near-miss is not auth`, `a non-string type is treated as absent and falls through to the status rules` — and the rung is now in the docs/operations.md §3 taxonomy table. The retry half is pinned one layer up, in `test/core/http.test.ts` — `a GET that meets an OAuthException with no recognised code is auth and is NOT retried (CC-AUTH-23)` — so the rule and the consequence that made it worth adding are held by different files. Mutation-verified 2026-09-22 with six hand mutants over `deriveKind`: neutering the tie-breaker, returning `permission` from it, comparing case-insensitively, trimming `type` first, and hoisting it above the code/subcode ladder are all killed by the tests named above; the sixth — dropping the `nonEmptyString` filter on `type` — survives and is equivalent, because `type` is consumed by exactly one strict `===` against a string literal and every value that filter would replace with `undefined` already compares false against it. That proof is written at the site rather than tested against | post-M5 · design |
| CC-AUTH-24 | The F-4 comment claimed `login` registers its minted token too — it does not, and must not | `src/index.ts` registers the freshly minted long-lived token with `registerSecret` the instant the exchange returns, and its comment closed with *“this is the same instant `login` mints its own token at (`cli/login.ts`)”*. A reader takes that as “`login` does the same thing”. It does not: `grep -rn registerSecret src/` finds call sites only in `src/index.ts` and the definition in `src/core/redact.ts`. Adding one to `runLogin` was the obvious repair and is the **wrong** one — `runLogin` is dispatched at the `subcommand === 'login'` branch and `process.exit`s from there, *before* `createLogger` / `createRedactor` are ever constructed, so the login process never builds a redactor for a registration to reach. The call would be inert by construction: unobservable code in a module held at 100% coverage, and an unkillable mutant by design. What makes `login` safe is stronger and simpler — it never prints the value. Its only unbounded output is `login failed: <message>` in the catch arm, and that message can only be a Graph `error.message` (via `exchangeError`) or one of the module's own fixed strings; `readJsonOrThrow` deliberately keeps the request URL and the raw body out of it, because the query string carries the app secret and the token. **Closed 2026-09-02** by correcting the comment in `src/index.ts` to say why `login` does not register, rather than by adding a call that does nothing. Recorded because the tempting fix here is the one that makes the codebase worse. **Amended 2026-09-23:** `login` now also redacts its failure line through a redactor local to the run (CC-AUTH-55); it still registers nothing | post-M5 · doc |

## 2. Publishing & container state machine (CC-PUB)

| ID | Scenario | Expected behavior | When / how |
|---|---|---|---|
| CC-PUB-1 | Container reaches `FINISHED`, but `media_publish` fails (quota, 5xx, integrity) | Error carries the container ID; composite returns a **resumable** result; `media_publish` is never auto-retried. **Re-measured and closed 2026-09-01.** The promise held only on the paths that *return*; on the paths that *throw* it did not. Measured before the fix: a 429 on the status read after the container existed produced `Application request limit reached` with the container id nowhere in it, and a 500 on `media_publish` produced the same shape after the recorded sequence create-container, read-status, publish — so the post could already be live while the only handle to it died in a local variable, and the natural recovery ("run it again") is exactly the duplicate this module exists to prevent. The composite now captures the id as the flow produces it and re-wraps a throw into an `InstagramError` carrying the original message, `kind`, `status`, `code`, `subcode`, `fbtrace_id` and `cause`, plus the instruction to read the state with `instagram_get_container_status` instead of publishing again. Deliberately **not** re-wrapped, both measured: a failure before any container exists (nothing to resume), and a message that already names the id (the carousel abort). `ERROR` and `EXPIRED` containers are also left untouched — a container in those states is not resumable and must not be advertised as if it were. The half of the promise that was already true stays true: publish is issued exactly once per flow, only after a read `FINISHED`, and nothing in the tool layer repeats it. | M2 · unit; re-measured post-M5 |
| CC-PUB-2 | Composite hits the 60 s poll cap while the container is still `IN_PROGRESS` | Returns in-progress result + container ID + instruction to resume; composites accept `resume_container_id` to continue without re-creating | M2 · design + unit |
| CC-PUB-3 | Container expired (code 24 / subcode 2207008 — 24 h passed unpublished) | Actionable error: re-create; the write journal shows the original creation for reference | M2 · unit |
| CC-PUB-4 | `media_publish` called twice for the same container (model retry) | Second call fails Meta-side; server detects the already-`PUBLISHED` status and reports success-idempotently with the existing media ID rather than a raw error `[verify — needs a live call: POST /{ig-id}/media_publish twice with the same creation_id and capture code / error_subcode / error_user_msg]`. The official Instagram error-code reference lists **no already-published subcode** *[checked 2026-07-30 — https://developers.facebook.com/docs/instagram-api/reference/error-codes/]*, so the shape cannot be derived from docs. **Resolved differently 2026-08-29:** the media id does not exist anywhere on this path — the container-status edge returns only `id`, `status_code` and `status` — so "report the existing media ID" was never implementable without guessing. The hole is now explicit instead of hidden: the result carries `media_id: null` plus a note saying the post was **not** published a second time and pointing at `instagram_list_media` to locate the existing one. Returning a success-shaped object with a missing key was the worse option | M2 · unit + live |
| CC-PUB-5 | Carousel: one child container fails / times out while siblings finish | Composite reports which child failed and returns all sibling container IDs; no parent container is created on partial failure. **Second half implemented 2026-08-29.** Facts established first: an unpublished container **expires on its own within 24 h** (CC-PUB-3), it costs **no publishing quota** (the quota is charged at `media_publish`, CC-PUB-12), and **Graph exposes no endpoint to delete a container** — the only `DELETE` in the whole api layer is `DELETE /{comment-id}`. So "clean up on failure" was never available, and inventing such a call would have been worse than the leak. Chosen instead: the abort error names the failing step by index (`child 2 of 3` / `the album container`) and lists the orphaned container ids. The original error is a **literal prefix** — the first failure stays the one the caller sees — and `kind`, `status`, `code`, `subcode`, `fbtraceId` and `cause` are carried through. The ids go in `message`, not in `cause`, because `errorResult` renders only `Instagram error (kind): message`. The failing **URL is deliberately not echoed** — pre-signed URLs carry credentials in the query string (CC-PUB-8), and a test pins that the secret does not appear | M2 · unit |
| CC-PUB-6 | Carousel bounds: < 2 or > 10 children; mixing image and video children | **Mixing is confirmed allowed — do not over-restrict.** "Carousels can have up to 10 total images, videos, or a mix of the two"; `children` is "an array of up to 10 container IDs" *[verified 2026-07-30 — IG User `/media` reference, https://developers.facebook.com/docs/instagram-platform/instagram-graph-api/reference/ig-user/media/ ; content-publishing guide, https://developers.facebook.com/docs/instagram-platform/content-publishing/]*. zod enforces the **max of 10** from the docs; the **minimum of 2** is an Instagram product rule that Meta's API docs never state, so it is enforced client-side as our own bound and Meta's rejection (if any) is mapped rather than predicted | M2 · unit |
| CC-PUB-7 | `image_url` unreachable by Meta (404, auth-walled, redirects, slow origin) | Container goes `ERROR`; server enriches with the container `status` detail and reminds that the URL must be publicly fetchable by Meta's crawlers | M2 · unit |
| CC-PUB-8 | Pre-signed URL (S3-style) expires between container creation and Meta's fetch/retry | Documented pitfall: recommend ≥ 1 h validity; error path same as CC-PUB-7 | M2 · docs |
| CC-PUB-9 | PNG/WebP/HEIC supplied (Meta accepts JPEG only for feed images) | zod/extension+content-type heuristic warns in preview; Meta's rejection mapped to `kind: validation` naming the JPEG-only rule | M2 · unit |
| CC-PUB-10 | Aspect ratio outside 0.8–1.91, image > 8 MB, reel > 300 MB, story video > 60 s | Client-side validation refuses **before** any container is created (no quota burn); message states the exact limit violated | M2 · unit |
| CC-PUB-11 | Caption edge: exactly 2,200 chars with emoji (UTF-16 surrogates), > 30 hashtags, > 20 @tags | Meta documents the caps — "Maximum 2200 characters, 30 hashtags, and 20 @ tags" — but **never defines the counting unit** *[checked 2026-07-30 — IG User `/media` reference, https://developers.facebook.com/docs/instagram-platform/instagram-graph-api/reference/ig-user/media/]*. We count in **code points**, not UTF-16 units, and validate client-side with precise counts in the error. `[verify — needs a live call: POST /{ig-id}/media with a caption of exactly 2,200 code points that includes non-BMP emoji (each 2 UTF-16 units); acceptance ⇒ code points, rejection ⇒ UTF-16 units or bytes]` | M2 · unit + live |
| CC-PUB-12 | Publishing-quota race: `quota_usage` shows 1 slot left, two posts run concurrently | Second gets code 9 / subcode 2207042 → refuse with reset info. Composites do not re-check `quota_usage` before publishing; Meta's code 9 / subcode 2207042 is the guard (see CC-PROC-201) | M2 · unit |
| CC-PUB-13 | Story specifics: no like/comment counts, self-expires in 24 h | `get_media` on an expired story returns a Graph error → mapped with "stories expire after 24 h" context; insights on expired stories degrade gracefully **Amended 2026-09-26:** `core/errors.ts` has no story-specific mapping — the Graph error (typically 100 / subcode 33) is classified `validation` with Meta's own message; the 24 h context is given in the `instagram_get_media` description. Whether to add a subcode-33 hint is an owner decision. | M2/M4 · docs + unit |
| CC-PUB-14 | Integrity restriction (subcode 2207051) on publish or comment | Never auto-retried; Meta's `error_user_msg` surfaced verbatim; journal records the refusal | M2 · unit |
| CC-PUB-15 | `location_id` invalid or the place was deleted | Graph 100-class → `kind: validation`; documented that location tagging is best-effort | M2 · docs |
| CC-PUB-16 | Client disconnects / process killed between container creation and publish | The write journal records a composite post (`post_image`, `post_reel`, `post_story`, `post_carousel`) once the whole flow returns — `published`, `in_progress` or `already_published` — not when the container is created, so a crash between create and publish leaves no journal line. The container id is journaled at creation only by the granular `instagram_create_media_container`, which is the path to use when a resumable handle matters (corrected 2026-09-23; the earlier wording promised a creation-time record the composite flow never wrote) | M2 · design + unit |
| CC-PUB-17 | User tags supplied for a **carousel** post | **Closed 2026-09-01.** Measured: for a two-image carousel not one request carried `user_tags` — the field appeared in no body on the whole flow — while the same tags on a single image were sent. Graph accepts user tags on a single feed image only, so the carousel builder sets them neither on the children nor on the album, and the input was being dropped in silence. The field description already said "(not carousels)", but a description is advice and the **preview** is what the operator approves: they approved a post carrying tags and got a post without them, and tags cannot be added to a carousel after it is published. It now warns through the existing `details.warnings` channel instead of refusing — the album is exactly what was asked for — and the input schema is unchanged. | post-M5 · unit |
| CC-PUB-18 | The container status endpoint answers with a `status_code` this build does not know — a new Meta value, an empty string, `null`, or no field at all | The poll loop's fall-through comment said "IN_PROGRESS (or an unknown/empty code) — keep polling", and that is exactly what it did: **measured**, an unrecognised code behaves byte-for-byte like IN_PROGRESS — 21 polls, 60 000 ms, 20 sleeps, then `{status: 'in_progress'}` and zero publications. The tool layer renders that as "re-run with `resumeContainerId`", and the resume re-enters the same loop, so an operator (or an agent acting on the hint) can be walked through an unbounded chain of 21-read budgets that can never succeed — and each poll is itself retried up to 4 times by `core/http`, so one 60 s budget is ~3 min of wall clock. Fixed by remembering whether a **recognised** `IN_PROGRESS` was ever seen: the unknown code is only indistinguishable from IN_PROGRESS *at a single reading*, not across the run. Budget exhausted having seen one → `in_progress` as before; budget exhausted having seen none → an `upstream` `InstagramError` that names the container, quotes the last answer (`JSON.stringify`, capped at 40 characters, so upstream free text cannot restructure a message an agent reads), lists the codes this build knows, and says **"Do NOT post it again"** — it tells the caller to read the container directly and publish *this same id* if it reports FINISHED, never to re-create it. No fourth `PublishFlowResult` variant, no second `publishMedia` call site: the duplicate-post guard is that `publishMedia` appears exactly once in the module, right after the FINISHED `break`, and the new path only throws. 41 → 50 tests; 59 mutants, 51 killed | post-M5 · unit |
| CC-PUB-19 | An operator times the publish flow and finds it far outside the 60 s poll budget | **Not a defect — documented, not fixed.** The budget is a **floor, not a ceiling**, and the docstring now says so. Two separate reasons, both measured. (a) The deadline is only tested between polls, so overshoot is bounded by one interval plus one in-flight request: at 0/100/700/2999 ms of upstream latency the run takes 60 000/62 100/63 600/62 989 ms. (b) Each individual poll is itself retried up to four times by `core/http` (backoff plus per-attempt timeout), so a 60 s budget is roughly **three minutes** of wall clock in the worst case. There is also no cap on the *number* of requests, only on elapsed time — with a finite interval and a monotonic clock the deadline bounds both, and only a non-monotonic injected `Clock` breaks that, which is not production-reachable. Related and equally deliberate: `pollDurationMs` normalises a `NaN`/`Infinity`/negative budget at the seam only. `PublishFlowOptions` is internal — `src/index.ts` does not export it and all three composite post tools call `runPublishFlow(args, ctx)` with `{}`, so only tests supply it. If it ever becomes a tool input it needs zod validation there, not a silent fallback here | post-M5 · observed |
| CC-PUB-20 | `assertCarouselSize` is handed a count that is not a whole number — `NaN`, `2.5`, `Infinity` | **Closed 2026-09-01.** The guard read `count < CAROUSEL_MIN \|\| count > CAROUSEL_MAX`, and `NaN` compares **false against both bounds**, so it returned normally and the caller went on to build a carousel out of a child count that is not a number; `2.5` slipped through the same gap while naming a carousel that cannot exist. The range test and the integrality test are not redundant — neither subsumes the other — so `!Number.isInteger(count)` is now the first disjunct. Every **production** call site passes an array `.length`, so only a direct caller of this exported, pure function reaches the new branch today; it is written for the general case for the same reason `imageUrlFormatWarning`'s two dot guards are, and the suite exercises it the way a future caller could. | post-M5 · unit |
| CC-PUB-21 | A feed image is served with a modern still-image extension an image CDN emits today — `.avif`, `.jxl`, `.apng`, `.ico` | **Closed 2026-09-01.** Format is unverifiable before Meta fetches the URL, so `imageUrlFormatWarning`'s extension set is the **only** warning an operator gets; four current formats were missing from it and passed as silently fine. `jxl` is the sharpest of them — it reads as a JPEG in its own name while being a format Instagram does not accept. Video extensions (`mp4`, `mov`) are deliberately **still absent**: the message says "suggests a non-JPEG image", which is wrong prose for a video, and the same extension is exactly right on `instagram_post_reel` — covering them needs a second message shape and a per-field notion of expected media kind, i.e. a signature change (owner decision, open). The JPEG spellings `jpg`/`jpeg`/`jfif`/`jpe`/`pjpeg` must never enter the set: warning on one would send the operator off to "convert" a file already in the only format Instagram takes. Mutation-verified 2026-09-22 with ten hand mutants on the set, ten killed: removing `avif`, `jxl`, `apng`, `ico` and — as a control on a pre-existing entry — `heic`, each caught by `every extension in the clearly-not-JPEG list produces the exact warning`; and adding each JPEG spelling (`jpg`, `jpeg`, `jfif`, `jpe`, `pjpeg`), each caught by `no spelling of JPEG is ever flagged as non-JPEG`, with `jpg` taking ten more tests down with it across the tool layer. | post-M5 · unit |
| CC-PUB-22 | The same `.png` is handed to a feed image, to a story image, and to a reel `coverUrl` | **Closed 2026-09-01.** The feed image got the non-JPEG format hint; the other two got **silence** — and silence is what an operator reads as "this one is fine". The hint was wired only to `instagram_create_media_container`'s `imageUrl` and to the `instagram_post_image` loop; `coverUrl` (both call sites) and `instagram_post_story`'s `imageUrl` were `assertHttpsUrl`-checked and nothing more. An inconsistent hint is worse than no hint, because it teaches a false rule. All three now warn, on the channel each handler already had: `details.warnings` reaches the **preview**, the refusal payload and the confirm-prompt elicitation, so the operator sees it *before* approving rather than after the post went out. The container site collects over `[imageUrl, coverUrl]` in argument order, so two offenders produce two entries rather than the first one only. A resume path warns about nothing, because resume ignores the media inputs. `videoUrl` is deliberately still unhinted — see CC-PUB-21. | post-M5 · unit |
| CC-PUB-23 | An `.mp4` is handed to an image field, and nothing warns | `CLEARLY_NOT_JPEG` holds still-image formats only — no video extension is in it — so `imageUrlFormatWarning` is silent on a video URL given to `imageUrl` or `coverUrl`. Deliberate, and the reason is the sentence the warning would have to say: it reads "suggests a non-JPEG **image**", which describes an `.mp4` wrongly, and the very same extension is exactly right one argument away on `instagram_post_reel`. A warning that names the wrong problem is worse than none, because the operator goes looking for a converter instead of for the field they crossed. The honest fix is a second message ("that extension is a video, and this field takes an image"), which is a change to `src/api/media-spec.ts` and a new sentence on the published surface. **Owner decision** — the silence is a choice about wording, not an oversight, and it is recorded here so it stops being rediscovered | post-M5 · unit |
| CC-PUB-24 | A dot in a directory segment makes the extension check read the wrong segment | `imageUrlFormatWarning` takes `pathname.lastIndexOf('.')` over the whole path, so `https://cdn.example.com/v1.2/photos/` yields the "extension" `2/photos/` and `https://cdn.example.com/img.v2/cat` yields `v2/cat`. Neither is in `CLEARLY_NOT_JPEG`, so the observable behaviour is silence — which is the documented default for an absent or ambiguous extension, and correct here by accident rather than by construction. It matters only if the set ever gains a short entry that a junk tail could collide with. Reading the LAST path segment before taking its dot is a two-line change and strictly more accurate; it is not made yet because this is explicitly a hint and never proof, and a stricter reader invites exactly the "no warning means it is a JPEG" reading the docstring refuses. **Owner decision**, since taken. **Fixed 2026-09-19.** `imageUrlFormatWarning` reads the FILE NAME first — `pathname.slice(pathname.lastIndexOf('/') + 1)` — and only then the last dot inside it, so `…/v1.2/photos/` and `…/img.v2/cat` are now silent because they have no extension at all, rather than because a junk tail happened to miss the set. The verdict is identical for every URL that exists today (a dot in a directory yields an "extension" carrying a `/`, and no entry in the set has one); what the change buys is that the reading says what it means and stays right if the set ever grows an entry a whole-path read could match across a directory boundary. The condition on this fix held: the docstring still refuses to let silence be read as "it is a JPEG", and the raw path is still read undecoded (`/pic%2Epng` has no extension here) for exactly that reason. The caller-order dependency is written down rather than guarded — every caller in `src/tools/publishing.ts` runs `assertHttpsUrl` first and an `https:` pathname always begins with `/`, so the no-slash shape is reachable only through another scheme, and `lastIndexOf` answering -1 handles it. Equivalent-mutant notes cover `dot === -1` vs `dot < 0` and `indexOf('/')` vs `lastIndexOf('/')`. Pinned by `the format hint reads the file name — the last path segment — and then its last dot (CC-PUB-24)` and `the format hint stays silent on a trailing dot and on a dot in a directory`, with `the format hint reads the path only — never the host, query, or fragment` and `no spelling of JPEG is ever flagged as non-JPEG` holding the surrounding behaviour still. Mutation-verified 2026-09-22 with nine hand mutants over the four lines: seven killed — the segment slice shifted by one in either direction, `segment.indexOf('.')` for `lastIndexOf`, `.toLowerCase()` dropped, `dot === 0` for `dot === -1`, the extension keeping its dot, and the no-dot guard neutered — and the two survivors are exactly the two equivalences the source already argues, `dot < 0` for `dot === -1` and `indexOf('/')` for `lastIndexOf('/')`; the second of those restores the pre-fix whole-path reading, which no URL can separate from this one because a dot in a directory yields an "extension" carrying a `/` and no entry in the set has one | post-M5 · unit |
| CC-PUB-25 | The publish warnings exist, and the applied payload does not carry them | Four things settled together while CC-PUB-22 was closed, all on the frozen tool surface and so none of them changed unilaterally. (a) The **applied** payloads of `post_image`, `post_reel` and `post_story` carry no `warnings` key at all — the hints live in `details` only, and promoting them is a new output field on a frozen surface. (b) Two same-extension offenders on one container produce two identical lines, because the message names the extension rather than the field it came from; de-duplicating loses the count, naming the field changes the sentence. (c) The prose still says "Instagram feed images must be JPEG" while the same message is now also shown for story images and reel covers, where "feed" is the wrong word. (d) A video-appropriate hint needs `src/api/media-spec.ts` to grow a second message — see CC-PUB-23. **Owner decision on all four**, since taken in part. **(c) fixed 2026-09-19; (a), (b) and (d) are still owner decisions.** The message now reads "URL extension \".png\" suggests a non-JPEG image; Instagram accepts JPEG only for images (feed, story, and reel cover). Format, byte size, and dimensions cannot be verified before Instagram fetches the URL." — the surfaces are named instead of the wrong one, and the unverifiability clause is unchanged, because that is the half that stops the hint being read as proof. Nothing about the output shape moved, so the frozen tool surface is untouched: the hint still lives in `details` only. Pinned by `the format hint names every still-image surface, not only the feed (CC-PUB-25)` in `test/api/media-spec.test.ts`, and the byte-for-byte copies of the sentence in `test/tools/publishing.test.ts` were updated with it. Still open and unchanged: (a) the applied payloads carry no `warnings` key, (b) two same-extension offenders still produce two identical lines, (d) a video-appropriate hint waits on CC-PUB-23. Mutation-verified 2026-09-22: six hand mutants on the sentence — each of the three surfaces dropped in turn, "cannot be verified" turned into "are verified", the unverifiability clause deleted outright, and the pre-fix sentence restored verbatim — and each of them kills exactly eight tests, because every assertion compares the whole message and, at the tool layer, the whole result object rather than a fragment. The set half of the same pass is CC-PUB-21 | post-M5 · unit |
| CC-PUB-26 | A CAROUSEL container created with **no children**, and nobody notices | `instagram_create_media_container` accepts `children` and `userTags` and forwards both to Graph, but no test had ever looked at the wire params for either — so deleting `children: args.children` from the call survived the whole suite. The failure is silent by construction: Graph accepts a `CAROUSEL` container with no children, the tool's own result reports `container_id` and nothing else, and the operator publishes an **empty album**. Dropped `userTags` are the same shape of loss — a post that tags nobody, with no error anywhere. Both were invisible because every existing assertion read the tool's OUTPUT, and neither argument appears in it. Two neighbours in the same handler had the same hole: the CC-PUB-4 recovery message (which names `instagram_get_container_status` and `instagram_list_media` as the two tools that can tell a caller whether re-posting would duplicate) was pinned only by `Do NOT post again blindly`, so pointing the advice at a tool that cannot answer survived; and `get_container_status`'s single logged field was not pinned to its argument, so a constant there made every status poll in the log identical — the log being what an operator reads to work out which container a failed publish was waiting on. **Closed 2026-09-02**, 115 → 117 tests, by asserting the wire params, the two tool names in the recovery advice, and the logged id against two different inputs | post-M5 · unit |
| CC-PUB-27 | A publishing quota whose rolling window is **zero** disappears from the answer entirely | Found by mutation testing 2026-09-08 in `src/api/publishing.ts`. `getPublishingLimit` copies the window onto its result under `if (quotaDuration !== undefined)`, and relaxing that to `if (quotaDuration)` survived the entire suite — twice, under two different drivers. The twin guard one line up (`quotaTotal !== undefined`) was already pinned, by a test that feeds a zeroed total and asserts it comes back as a real `0`; nothing did the same for the window, so no fixture ever carried `config.quota_duration: 0`. The two zeros are not the same fact, which is why one test does not cover the other: a zeroed *total* says the account may post nothing, a zeroed *window* says the rolling period has elapsed and the quota is free again. Dropping the field does not report either — an absent key reads as "the window is unknown", the exact opposite of "the window is over". **Closed 2026-09-08** with `getPublishingLimit reports a zeroed rolling window as a real duration of zero`, the sibling of the existing zero-total test. Module totals for the publishing sweep: 171 mutants against `publishing.ts`, `media.ts` and `media-spec.ts`, judged against the full suite — 170 killed, 1 proven equivalent (`added += 1` → `added -= 1` in `fetchPagedEdge`, where `added` is only ever compared `=== 0`, so the direction of the count is unobservable and the source already carries the note), and all 44 `media-spec.ts` mutants killed | post-M5 · unit |
| CC-PUB-45 | `every declared media URL field refuses a non-https URL at the schema boundary` covered six of the eight media URL fields the publishing package declares. Both of `instagram_post_story`'s, `imageUrl` and `videoUrl`, were absent from the test entirely, and the lists driving it were hand-written, so nothing moved when `src/tools/publishing.ts` moved. The published-contract pin cannot stand in for it: `httpsUrlSchema` is `z.string().refine(...)` and a refinement has no JSON Schema rendering at all, so dropping it emits byte-identical output. What is at stake is the audit log and the model's successful-call history, where a plaintext URL lands before the handler's own `assertHttpsUrl` refuses it. | **Closed 2026-09-23.** The swept set comes off `t.input` across `publishingTools`, floored at eight declared fields, with a second scan naming any input key that mentions a URL but does not end in `Url`/`Urls` — a filter that knows one spelling reports nothing about the others, per CC-PROC-150. Whether a field takes one URL or a list is read off the schema by probing both shapes and requiring exactly one to be accepted, so a field probed in the shape it does not take cannot look refused for the wrong reason; a counter proves the per-item list claim ran. Measured: pointing both story URL fields at one shared bare `z.string()`, which reproduces the `$ref` dedup so the published contract stays green, survived 143 of 143 tests before the repair and is killed alone after it. Positive control: the same mutation on `instagram_create_media_container`, a tool the pre-fix lists do name, fails pre-fix. | post-M5 · unit |

## 3. Rate limits & quotas (CC-RATE)

| ID | Scenario | Expected behavior | When / how |
|---|---|---|---|
| CC-RATE-1 | Usage headers absent on a response (Meta sends them inconsistently) | Budget snapshot keeps the last-seen value with its timestamp; `token_status` marks it "as of \<time\>", never crashes | M1 · unit |
| CC-RATE-2 | Malformed / unparseable usage-header JSON | Swallowed with a debug log; never breaks the actual response path | M1 · unit |
| CC-RATE-3 | `Retry-After` absurdly large (or absent) on 429 | Cap wait at 60 s; if the reset is hours away (BUC 24 h window), **fail fast with the estimate** instead of blocking the MCP call | M1 · unit |
| CC-RATE-4 | Hashtag 30-unique/7-days local counter: file corrupted, or same account used from two machines | Counter is advisory only — self-heals on parse failure (reset + warn); Meta's own rejection remains the hard signal. **The multi-machine blind spot is fixable**: `GET /{ig-user-id}/recently_searched_hashtags` returns the account's real 7-day window server-side *[verified 2026-07-30 — https://developers.facebook.com/docs/instagram-platform/instagram-graph-api/reference/ig-user/recently_searched_hashtags]*, so the counter should be reconciled against it rather than documented as unfixable (see [operations.md](operations.md) §1) | M4 · unit + docs |
| CC-RATE-5 | Proactive throttle (> 90 % usage) triggering during a composite mid-flow | Composite completes its current step but refuses to start new quota-consuming steps; partial-progress result explains why | M2 · unit |
| CC-RATE-6 | Semaphore saturation: > `IG_MAX_CONCURRENT` parallel tool calls | Queued fairly (FIFO); a queue-wait timeout produces a clear "server busy" error rather than hanging the MCP call indefinitely | M1 · unit |
| CC-RATE-7 | A hashtag argument that is non-empty but normalises to nothing (`"#"`, `"###"`, `"   "`) | **Closed 2026-09-01.** The input schema’s `.min(1)` constrains the **argument**; `normalizeHashtag` strips leading `#` and surrounding whitespace and can still leave `""`, which went out as `q=` — not a hashtag search — and, worse, consumed one of the **30 unique hashtags per 7 days** Meta allows the account, a budget that cannot be refunded and is shared with every other client of the same token. `instagram_search_hashtag` now rejects the empty result before the call, spending neither a request nor a budget slot. The message names the rule and gives an example but **never echoes the value**: error text reaches logs and model context, and this argument is untrusted model input — the same rule `api/discovery.assertValidUsername` already follows | post-M5 · unit |
| CC-RATE-8 | Quota telemetry wired but never once observed end-to-end: `onUsage` was dead in every test | **Closed 2026-09-06.** `createIgRequest` parses `X-App-Usage` / `X-Business-Use-Case-Usage` off every response and hands the snapshot to the `onUsage` callback the composition root supplies (`src/index.ts` → `log.debug('graph usage', …)`). That is the **only** thing in the process that tells an operator how much of the app's Graph quota is left. Graph reports it exclusively in **response headers**, and the child-process fetch stub (`test/helpers/entry-preload.ts`) could set a status and a body but **no headers at all** — so the seam had never fired end-to-end and deleting the whole `onUsage:` line from the entry left the entire suite green. The stub now takes a `headers` map, and three entry tests pin the record: the max across both headers with the winner buried in the *second* entry of the *second* business id; `X-App-Usage` alone, with the peak in `total_cputime` rather than the obvious `call_count`; and a header-less response, which must log **no `maxPct` key at all** rather than `0` — "Graph told us nothing" and "nothing consumed" are opposite facts and the second is the more reassuring lie. Eleven mutants across `parseUsage`, `parseBucUsage` and `maxOfUsageFields` were re-run against the new tests; all eleven die. The failure this closes is silent and slow: an app at 95 % of quota gets no warning and the first symptom is Graph refusing calls outright | post-M5 · unit |
| CC-RATE-9 | A rate limit that never lifts: the only bound on the retry loop was the one thing no test could see | **Closed 2026-09-07.** `createIgRequest` retries inside `for (let attempt = 0; ; attempt++)` — an unbounded loop — and on the HTTP-error path the sole exit for an error that stays retryable is `if (lastAttempt \|\| !isRetryableKind(...))`. Deleting `lastAttempt` left the entire suite green. The reason is uncomfortable once seen: **every** 429 and 5xx test in the file clears on a later attempt, so none of them ever reaches the fourth, and the one exhaustion test covered the **transport** arm (a `fetch` that throws), which is a different `if` on a different branch of the same loop. Nothing in the repo had ever asked what happens when Graph stays throttled — which is precisely the condition a rate limit *is*. Now pinned by a 429 that never clears: four calls, three backoffs, rejects `rate_limit`. The test's clock carries a **fuse**, and that detail is the transferable part: with a `sleep` that resolves instantly an unbounded client spins on microtasks without yielding long enough for a timer to land, and `npm test` passes no `--test-timeout`, so this regression would **hang** CI rather than fail it. The clock rejects on a fourth backoff — unreachable while the bound holds, and a millisecond-fast failure the day it does not | post-M5 · unit |
| CC-RATE-10 | Two guards in `parseRetryAfter` that are each individually dead — and must both stay | Mutation testing 2026-09-07 reported the early `if (trimmed === '')` and the `^\d+$` digit anchor as separately unkillable, which reads like an invitation to delete one. It is the opposite: they are belt-and-braces, and each is equivalent **only because the other covers the empty string**. Drop the blank check alone and `''` fails the digit test, falls through to `Date.parse('')` → `NaN` → `undefined`, the same answer. Loosen the anchor to `^\d*$` alone and the blank check has already returned. Drop **both** and a header spelled `Retry-After:` with nothing after it parses as `Number('') * 1000` = **0** — an immediate zero-delay retry against a server that just said stop, exactly the hammering the backoff exists to prevent. Recorded here so a future "remove the dead branch" pass removes at most one of them, on purpose | post-M5 · docs |

## 4. Pagination, data shape & truncation (CC-DATA)

| ID | Scenario | Expected behavior | When / how |
|---|---|---|---|
| CC-DATA-1 | Cursor invalidated (media deleted between page 1 and page 2) | Graph error mapped with "cursor may be stale — restart the listing"; `fetchAll` returns what it got + `truncated: true` + the error note | M1 · unit |
| CC-DATA-2 | Fields silently missing (Meta omits rather than nulls): `like_count` hidden by author, `media_url` absent on copyright-muted media, stories lacking counts | Output schemas mark such fields optional; text rendering says "unavailable", never `undefined`/crash | M1 · unit |
| CC-DATA-3 | Character-budget truncation landing mid-emoji / mid-surrogate-pair in a caption | Truncation is code-point-safe (never splits a surrogate pair or ZWJ sequence mid-way); property-tested with `fast-check` | M1 · unit |
| CC-DATA-4 | `fetchAll` hits `IG_MAX_ITEMS` exactly at a page boundary | `truncated: true` iff more data exists — off-by-one covered by tests | M1 · unit |
| CC-DATA-5 | Media ID valid but object deleted / expired story | 100-class with subcode → mapped "object no longer exists" rather than generic invalid-parameter **Amended 2026-09-26:** no deleted-object mapping exists in `core/errors.ts` — the error is classified `validation` with Meta's own message; the context is in the `instagram_get_media` description. Whether to add a subcode-33 hint is an owner decision. | M1 · unit |
| CC-DATA-6 | Unexpected `media_product_type` values (new types Meta adds, e.g. ad-linked media) | Enums for Meta-owned vocabularies are **open** (string with known values documented), so new values pass through instead of failing validation | M1 · design + unit |
| CC-DATA-7 | Graph adds new fields to a response with `outputSchema` declared | Additive Meta changes never break structured output — but **the stated mechanism was wrong, corrected 2026-08-29**. A tool's `output` is a `z.ZodRawShape`, and `src/mcp/registry.ts:577` hands that raw shape to the SDK; `.shape` carries the field map and **discards the unknown-keys mode**, so `.passthrough()` applied to a *top-level* output object is inert. Measured, not reasoned: `businessOutput._def.unknownKeys === 'passthrough'` while `z.object(businessOutput.shape)._def.unknownKeys === 'strip'`, and the schema the SDK publishes from the raw shape is `additionalProperties: false`. This holds for **every** tool in the surface, not just `discovery`. Passthrough survives only one level down, on nested objects that travel as whole schemas rather than as shapes. The protection that actually exists is upstream: the api layer normalises field by field (`discoverBusiness` copies each property; no spread), so an unknown Meta field is dropped a layer before the output object is built and can never collide with a strict top level. The dead `.passthrough()` on `businessOutput` was deleted and the asymmetry pinned by test through a real `McpServer` + `InMemoryTransport`. One residual, inert and left in place: `commentDetailOutput.passthrough()` in `src/tools/comments.ts` — the same top-level case, and its own note already calls it unobservable and says not to contort a test into killing it. **The second residual is closed (re-checked 2026-09-01):** the comment in `test/tools/media.test.ts` that implied passthrough *would* set `additionalProperties: true` now states the opposite correctly, and explains why the closed top level makes `get_media`'s field list load-bearing rather than cosmetic | M1 · design + unit |
| CC-DATA-8 | A pagination cursor arrives as JSON `null` (Graph's normal single-page response `{"paging":{"cursors":{"after":null}}}`) | Before 2026-08-29 `fetchPagedEdge` copied it through because `null !== undefined`, `src/tools/media.ts` placed it in `structuredContent.paging.after`, whose schema is `z.string().optional()`, and the MCP SDK failed output validation — **`instagram_list_media` errored on a perfectly valid page**. Reproduced end-to-end through the real SDK, not by reading. Fixed at the *publication* site rather than by loosening the schema: `after` is emitted only when it is a non-empty string. `.nullable()`/`.nullish()` were rejected — they would publish `null` as a documented result and delete the guard. `''` is excluded too, because every tool *input* schema types `after` as `z.string().min(1)` and tests pin that `after: ''` is refused; publishing a cursor the model will then be denied is worse than publishing none. **Compatibility fact:** `paging.after` is now *absent* rather than `null`/`''`, and this also affects the two `fetchPagedEdge` callers in `src/api/comments.ts`. `truncated` is unchanged | post-M5 · unit |
| CC-DATA-9 | A carousel's inline `children` field arrives with Graph's `{ data, paging }` envelope | `normalizeDetail` flattens it to a bare array. **Investigated 2026-08-29 and found correct, not a defect** — recorded so it is not "fixed" later by mistake. It matches the published output schema (`children: z.array(childOutput).optional()`), `instagram_get_media` is not a list tool so the `{ items, paging }` convention in [tools.md](tools.md) does not apply, and a carousel holds **at most 10 children** (CC-PUB-6), so no second page can exist for a cursor to point at. **Residual, unverifiable from this repo:** no captured fixture has ever seen the real shape of inline `children` — `test/fixtures/` holds only a synthetic list example. Recorded as an explicit unverified upstream assumption in the `normalizeDetail` docstring; a `media-detail` capture would settle it | post-M5 · live |
| CC-DATA-10 | A `null` value reaches `buildUrl`'s query parameters | `src/core/host.ts` skips only `undefined`, so `String(null)` is appended as the literal string `"null"`. Observed under `fetchAll` with a `null` cursor: `…/999/media?fields=id&after=null` goes out on the wire. The `QueryParams` type says `string \| number \| boolean \| undefined`, but nothing validates at runtime, so the type is not the guarantee it looks like. Not fixed: an existing test pins the deliberate policy that a `null` cursor does **not** mean "exhausted", and changing the skip condition would change what that test defends. **Closed 2026-08-30 — and that last sentence was wrong.** It conflated two questions owned by two layers: *does a `null` cursor mean the edge is exhausted* (a pagination policy, `api/`) and *if a `null` does reach `buildUrl`, should four characters spelling `null` go out on the wire* (URL construction, `core/host.ts`). The test it pointed at — `listMedia fetchAll does not accept a null cursor as proof the edge is exhausted` — asserts `calls.length === 2` through a **fake `req` that never calls `buildUrl` at all**, so the two never touch. Measured: the whole of `test/api/media.test.ts` passes 35/35 after the fix, untouched. The guard is now `value === undefined \|\| value === null`; no cast was needed, because TS deliberately exempts `null`/`undefined` from the no-overlap check (TS2367 fires for `'a' === 'b'` and `string === number`, not for this). `QueryParams`' type annotation is documented as a *declaration of intent* and the runtime guard as the load-bearing one. Mutation-checked on the compiled output: dropping the `null` test → 2 failures, dropping `undefined` → 5, `\|\|`→`&&` → 5, widening to `!value` → 2; only `== null` is equivalent, and it carries an Equivalent-mutant note | post-M5 · unit |
| CC-DATA-11 | `fetchAll` is given an edge whose `after` cursor is present but is not a position in the edge (`null`, or an empty string) | **Closed 2026-08-31.** Of the two candidate rules the row left open — "a `null` cursor ends the walk" vs "a repeated cursor ends the walk" — the first was taken and then widened: what ends the walk is a cursor that **cannot be used**, not one of a particular shape. A single predicate `isUsableCursor` (`typeof === 'string'` and non-empty) is now used at **both** the walk guard and the publish step, so a cursor can never be good enough to follow yet too broken to hand back — that asymmetry was the actual defect, since the publish step had always normalised `''` and `null` away identically while the walk followed one of them. Order carries the meaning: `undefined` is checked first and breaks cleanly (Graph omitting the cursor is Graph saying the edge is finished — no `truncated`, no `note`); only what survives that check is *broken* rather than *done*. Measured after: one request instead of two, `items: ['1']` not `['1','1']`, `truncated: true`, no `after`, and a note naming the reason. A capped stop that lands on an unusable cursor gets its own note, which deliberately does **not** advise a larger `maxItems` — a larger cap would not stop there at all, it would fall into the guard below and stop on the same cursor with the same items. The pinned test this replaces (`calls.length === 2`, `items: ['1','2']`) was not merely defending the wrong outcome: its fake produced a fresh page on the second call keyed off `params.after === undefined`, i.e. it simulated forward progress the transport cannot make, because `buildUrl` drops a `null` parameter (CC-DATA-10). `''` is pinned separately from `null` because the verdict is shared but the mechanism is not — `''` really does reach the wire, as `&after=`. `media.test.ts` 35 → 36; 10 mutants, 9 killed, the falsy-shorthand rewrite equivalent only because of the reordering and carrying a note that says so. **Extended by audit 2026-09-23 to the single page.** The rule covered only the `fetchAll` walk; the default single-page read (`fetchPagedEdge` with `fetchAll` unset) still reported a page whose `after` was present but unusable as `truncated: false`, i.e. as the end of the edge. It now reports `truncated: true` with the unusable-cursor note, on `list_media`, `list_comments` and `list_tagged_media` alike — an absent cursor is still the end. Closed by `listComments reports a single page ending on an unusable cursor as truncated (CC-DATA-11)`, `real McpServer: list_media reports a single page ending on an unusable cursor as truncated (CC-DATA-11)` and `a single comments page ending on an unusable cursor is reported truncated, not complete (CC-DATA-11)`; mutants that drop the branch or its `!== undefined` guard are killed | post-M5 · unit |
| CC-DATA-12 | A `null` value reaches a **POST body** rather than a query string | `src/core/http.ts` builds the form body with the identical `if (value !== undefined)` shape `buildUrl` had, so a `null` is sent as the literal string `"null"` in the `x-www-form-urlencoded` payload. Same defect, same fix, different sink — found while closing CC-DATA-10 and left alone only because it was outside that change's file scope **Closed 2026-08-31.** The guard is now `value === undefined \|\| value === null`, the same spelling and the same reasoning as `buildUrl`, so the two sinks cannot drift apart again. Reproduced first: the pre-fix wire body was `caption=ok&alt_text=null&share_to_feed=false&thumb_offset=0&empty=` — which also shows the constraint the fix had to respect, since `false`, `0` and `''` were already correct and must stay on the wire (a form body that drops `false` changes a caller's meaning). The rest of the file was swept for the same shape and there is no second instance: the remaining truthiness tests are on the *container* (`opts.body`, where truthiness is strictly safer than `!== undefined`), on a locally computed `string \| undefined`, or on response data. **The reachability is weaker than CC-DATA-10’s and the comment says so rather than borrowing its urgency:** no in-repo call site populates `opts.body` at all (see CC-PROC-25), and neither `scripts/*.mjs` entry point does, so a `null` needs either a future api function or a JavaScript consumer of the shipped `.d.ts`. The guard is justified as sink-permanence, not as a live hole. Mutation-checked on the compiled output: dropping the `undefined` half → 3 failures, dropping the `null` half (the original defect) → 2, `\|\|`→`&&` → 3, widening to `!value` → 1 (killed solely by the falsy-preservation test, which is what it is for); `== null` and `form.set` are equivalent and carry notes | post-M5 · unit |
| CC-DATA-13 | A non-finite or exponential number reaches a query parameter | `limit=NaN`, `limit=Infinity` and `since=1e%2B21` are all type-legal and go out on the wire. `since`/`until` are unbounded `z.number().int()` — zod rejects `NaN`/`Infinity` but accepts `1e21`, so the last one is reachable from the model. **Deliberately not dropped**: unlike a nullish value, a number is not an assertion of absence, and silently deleting the parameter would turn a caller's bug into a successful request with a different meaning. Recorded in the `buildUrl` docstring rather than "fixed" | post-M5 · unit |
| CC-DATA-14 | A query-parameter value has no string form (an object with a `null` prototype, or one whose `toString`/`valueOf` throws) | **Closed 2026-08-31 — it refuses.** No coercion, no skipping, no placeholder: you cannot invent a query value for something that has none, and the module's contract already was that what leaves it is an `InstagramError` of kind `validation`, so this is the module keeping a promise it had already made rather than a new policy. The thrown error **names the offending key** — a caller staring at "cannot convert object to primitive value" has no way to find which parameter it was — and carries neither the value nor the underlying error's text, because both are caller-controlled and this project treats caller-controlled text as potentially secret-bearing. **The register's claim that this was the *only* raw escape from `core/host.ts` was wrong**, measured and corrected: a value whose own `toString` throws escaped identically, which is why the guard is deliberately wider than the null-prototype case that opened the row. The remaining escapes are not parameter values and are filed as CC-DATA-17. Mutation-checked 9/9, two of which (re-stringifying after validation, and `String(v)` vs a template literal) survived the first pass and are **not** equivalent — the first is a TOCTOU re-entry into caller code, the second turns a `Symbol` from a successful request into a silent refusal | post-M5 · unit |
| CC-DATA-15 | A POST is given an **empty** body object rather than no body | **Closed 2026-08-31.** A body that contributes no fields now leaves the wire exactly as `body: undefined` does — `init.body` and the `content-type` header appear or disappear together. The test is `form.size > 0`, the **field count**, not the emptiness of the encoded string: "did the caller give us anything to send" is the question actually being asked, and `{ caption: '' }` must still send `caption=` because an empty string is a value the caller chose, not an absence. Measured before: `body: {}` and a fully-nullish body both produced `init.body = ""` plus the header, and a DELETE really did carry an empty form body. One existing assertion was **tightened**, not weakened — the CC-DATA-12 test pinned `body === ''`, which was the incident rather than the intent. The two mutants that survive (`toString() !== ''` and its truthiness) are genuinely equivalent, since a `URLSearchParams` holding one pair serialises to at least `=`, and carry notes saying so | post-M5 · unit |
| CC-DATA-16 | The **last** page overflows `IG_MAX_ITEMS` and carries no cursor at all (`paging: {}`) | The capped stop returns `truncated: true` with no `after` and no `note` — the same "short listing, no cursor, no reason" shape CC-DATA-11 just fixed for the unusable-cursor path. Found while mutation-testing that fix: the mutant dropping the `nextAfter !== undefined` half of the new note condition survived until this case was pinned as note-free. Left unchanged deliberately, because nothing here is broken — the edge genuinely ended and the tail was cut by the cap, so unlike the unusable-cursor path the one useful message would be "raise `maxItems`", which on **this** path is valid advice. Pinned as `note === undefined` so the two stops cannot quietly merge. Owner decision — whether CC-DATA-4's exhausted-and-capped stop deserves a note of its own | post-M5 · unit |
| CC-DATA-17 | A **non-parameter** input to `src/core/host.ts` has no string form | Two more raw `TypeError`s leave the module without becoming an `InstagramError`, found while closing CC-DATA-14 and left open on purpose — the fix there was scoped to parameter values, and widening it under the same commit would have made the diff unreviewable. (1) A **non-string `path`** hits the same implicit ToString inside the base-URL template literal and throws the identical error. (2) A host that **is** on the allowlist but carries surrounding whitespace passes `assertAllowedHost`, which normalises (`trim().toLowerCase()`) only for the comparison and then lets the raw value be interpolated, so `new URL` rejects it with `TypeError: Invalid URL`; a tab-padded host, unlike a space-padded one, does produce a proper `InstagramError`. Note this **fails closed** — the request is not built, nothing unvalidated reaches the wire — so what is wrong is the shape of the failure, not the safety of it. Neither is reachable from an in-repo call site today: `path` is supplied by the api layer and the host comes from module constants in `core/auth.ts`. Same class as CC-DATA-14, same fix shape. **Fixed 2026-09-19** — that shape, applied twice inside `buildUrl`. (1) The base-URL template converts `path` inside a `try`; a ToString that throws becomes `InstagramError({ kind: 'validation' })` — "Refusing request: the request path cannot be converted to a string" — naming the argument and never echoing it, the rule the path invariant's own messages follow, because a value that reaches there is untrusted input. Only the conversion is wrapped: an input that *does* have a string form (a one-element array stringifies to its element, a number to its digits) reaches `assertPinnedGraphPath` exactly as before and is accepted or refused on what it spells, so the accepted set is unchanged. (2) The caller's raw host spelling is tested for membership in `ALLOWED_HOSTS` before it is interpolated, and anything else is refused with "Refusing request: host \"…\" is allowlisted only once trimmed and lower-cased and cannot be interpolated into a URL as written" — the host is echoed here precisely because `assertAllowedHost` has already established that, trimmed and lower-cased, it is one of the two allowlist literals. The test sits in `buildUrl` rather than in `assertAllowedHost` because that assertion's trim-and-lower-case tolerance is a pinned contract of its own (a padded host is still recognised as allowlisted, and refused as nothing else); this guard decides only what may reach a URL. What changed is the shape and the wording of the failure, never its safety — both already failed closed, and the space-padded host had additionally been blamed on the PATH by the parse-survival check. `InstagramError` with `kind: 'validation'` is now the ONLY error shape the function throws. Four tests in `test/core/host.test.ts`: `buildUrl refuses an allowlisted host in any spelling other than its exact literal (CC-DATA-17)`, `the exact-spelling guard leaves both canonical host literals untouched`, `buildUrl refuses a path with no string form instead of leaking a TypeError (CC-DATA-17)`, `the string-form guard still hands a stringifiable non-string path to the path invariant`. Mutation-verified 2026-09-22 with eight hand mutants, eight killed, no survivors and nothing strengthened: the host guard normalising before it compares (the pre-fix reading), inverted, given `kind: 'upstream'`, and stripped of its verbatim echo; the string-form guard removed entirely (the raw `TypeError` escapes again), given the wrong kind, given the wrong wording, and widened to refuse every non-string path — that last one killed by `the string-form guard still hands a stringifiable non-string path to the path invariant`, which is the test that stops the fix narrowing the accepted set | post-M5 · unit |
| CC-DATA-18 | A query-parameter value coerces to a string that carries no information — a plain object (`[object Object]`), an array (`1,2`), a `Symbol` (`Symbol(x)`), a `BigInt` | All go out on the wire as their coercion rather than being refused. Measured while closing CC-DATA-14 and left as-is, because it is the **same policy CC-DATA-13 already settled** for `NaN`/`Infinity`: a value is not an assertion of absence, and silently dropping or rejecting it would turn a caller's bug into a different request or a refusal for input the type system was never asked to police. Recorded so the boundary is visible: `core/host.ts` refuses only values with **no** string form, never values with a useless one | post-M5 · unit |
| CC-DATA-19 | Untrusted third-party text arrives as `null`, a number, or an object where the api-layer type says `string` | **Closed 2026-09-01.** The api layer’s types are a **cast, not a proof** — `core/host` states outright that `req` only casts the Graph body — so a caption typed `string \| undefined` is really "whatever Meta put in the JSON", and Meta does emit `null` for media with no caption. Measured: such a value reached `fence()` and threw `TypeError: … .split is not a function` **from inside the handler**, which the registry then rendered as an `upstream` Instagram error — another party’s data failing the call under a mislabelled kind. `fenceField` now fences a real string and **drops** anything else. Dropping is the only honest option: a non-string cannot satisfy the declared `z.string().optional()` output either, so publishing it as-is would only trade the `TypeError` for an output-validation error, and it must never be published unfenced. An empty string is real text and stays fenced. The same reasoning covers `businessToRecord`’s `media` edge, which `api/discovery` fills from a **truthiness** test on the wire and therefore types as an array without having checked | post-M5 · unit |
| CC-DATA-20 | One malformed entry in a media page has no `id`, and the tool’s output schema declares `id` required | **Closed 2026-09-01.** A single id-less entry failed structured-output validation and took the **whole page** down with `MCP error -32602` — a page of otherwise good media lost to one degenerate row. An id-less media object is also unusable on its own terms: no other tool can address it. `mediaRecords` now drops entries that are not objects carrying a non-empty string `id`, and `instagram_search_hashtag` filters its returned ids the same way (`z.array(z.string())` would reject an empty one, and the caller spends each id as the next call’s `hashtagId`, which rejects `""` anyway). Dropped **silently**, because the tool output has no field to report it in and inventing one would change a published schema; losing one degenerate entry beats losing the page. Discovery’s `after` cursor now goes through the same usability predicate, extending CC-DATA-11’s rule — a handle is published only if it can be spent — from `api/media`’s pager to the discovery tools | post-M5 · unit |
| CC-DATA-21 | Meta answers with an **empty** `x-fb-trace-id` header | The header was forwarded as `fbtraceId: ''` — an id-shaped field carrying no id, which passes every `!== undefined` check between the error and the operator, so the support ticket quotes an empty trace and Meta has nothing to look up. The `?? undefined` idiom treats "absent" and "present but blank" as different facts when they are the same one. Closed 2026-09-02 in `src/core/refresh.ts` by keeping the value only when it is non-blank, tested over both `''` and `'   '`. **Closed 2026-09-02 at `src/core/http.ts` too** — the wider instance, on the path every other Graph call takes — with the same `isNonBlank` guard, deliberately the same shape so the two cannot drift. Pinning it needed a second test the header table could not give: a real `Headers` flattens `'   '` to `''` before the client sees it, so the table cannot tell a blank id from a zero-length one. `fetchImpl` is an injectable seam and nothing guarantees every transport is an `undici` one — a proxy agent, a stubbed `Response`, a future runtime may answer a header lookup verbatim — so a bare `get` returning what it was given drives the guard directly.
