| CC-PROC-66 | A per-attempt timeout was retried only because no test ever timed out an idempotent call; an explicit `idempotent: false` was honoured only because no test ever passed it | Found by mutation testing 2026-09-18 in `src/core/http.ts`, two survivors on the retry decision after a thrown `fetch`. (1) The per-attempt deadline is `AbortSignal.timeout` folded into the same combined signal as the caller's abort, so when the timer fires the signal handed to `fetch` **is** aborted — the guard must read `opts.signal?.aborted`, never the combined one. A mutant that consulted the combined signal treated every timeout as a caller abort and gave up after the first slow attempt; it survived because both timeout tests used a POST (never retried whichever signal is read) and every GET retry test threw a synthetic `TypeError` without aborting anything. (2) `opts.idempotent ?? method === 'GET'` rewritten to `\|\|` discards an explicit `false` on a GET and keeps retrying a read the caller marked non-replayable; every fixture either omitted the option or set it `true`. Both are the documented contract (`docs/operations.md`: a timeout on an idempotent call is retried; the override is a value, not a default) with no test on the value that flips them. Closed by `a per-attempt timeout on an idempotent GET is retried — only the caller’s own abort is final` (real 20 ms deadline, first fetch never answers, second succeeds, `calls.length === 2`, one backoff) and `idempotent:false on a GET switches retries OFF — the override is a value, not a default` (`test/core/http.test.ts`). No production change | post-M5 · unit |
| CC-PROC-67 | The throttle warning could lose its text and its number, and the request debug line its path, behind `warns.length === 1` | Found by mutation testing 2026-09-18 in `src/core/http.ts`. The one assertion on the proactive-throttle warning was a count, so rewriting the message and dropping `usagePct` from the record both survived; the request debug test proved the record carried no URL and no token but never that it carried the `path`, so `{ method, host, path }` could shrink to `{ method, host }`. A count is the weakest fragment (CC-PROC-54): it holds for any record at all. The warning is the one line an operator greps for when the client starts pausing; the debug line is how a request is matched to a Graph endpoint without ever logging the query string. Closed by extending `usage above 90% triggers a proactive throttle sleep and a warn log` (`deepEqual(log.warnRecords, [{ msg: 'approaching Instagram rate limit; throttling before returning', fields: { host: 'graph.instagram.com', usagePct: 95 } }])`) and `the request debug log carries no URL and no token` (the `graph request` records pinned whole as `{ method: 'GET', host: 'graph.instagram.com', path: '/123/media' }`). No production change **Amended 2026-09-22 (CC-PROC-82):** that closure pinned the warn *record* in one test only, and that test drives the throttle from `x-app-usage` alone, where `usage.maxPct` and `usage.appUsagePct` happen to be the same number. `usagePct: usage.maxPct -> usage.appUsagePct` therefore still survived the whole suite; the mirror mutation to `bucUsagePct` died. The row's claim holds for the message but not for the reading it reports. | post-M5 · unit |
| CC-CFG-20 | Every settings and configuration refusal was pinned by its variable name only — bounds order, echoed value, allowed set, guidance and the *other* variable named were all free | Found by mutation testing 2026-09-18 across `src/core/settings.ts`, `src/core/config.ts` and `src/core/config-write.ts`: twelve survivors of one shape. In `settings.ts` the tests asserted `kind === 'validation'` and that the message mentions the variable (`assertValidationError(fn, 'IG_X')`), so `[1, 64]` could read `[64, 1]`, `, got 65` could vanish, the allowed set's `debug \| info \| warn \| error` could become a comma list, the boolean wording could be rewritten and the "put an authenticating reverse proxy in front" sentence — the documented answer to a non-loopback `IG_HTTP_HOST` — could be dropped. In `config.ts` the fragments `/IG_APP_SECRET/`, `/no access token/` let the fb-login refusal name `IG_APP_ID` twice (CC-PROC-56: the wrong variable named), the no-default refusal lose its `(the default account token)` clause, and the auth-path refusal stop echoing the offending value or swap the canonical and alias names. In `config-write.ts` `/access token is required/` left the `writeCredentials:` prefix — which write path refused — unpinned. These are the first-run errors an operator reads before anything else works. Closed by whole-message tables: `every refusal is pinned whole — variable, bounds, allowed set, offending value, guidance` (`test/core/settings.test.ts`, 15 `[env, exactMessage]` rows), `every configuration refusal is pinned whole — variable names, echoed value, guidance` (`test/core/config.test.ts`) and `a blank access token is rejected with a validation error` (`test/core/config-write.test.ts`, now `assert.equal` on `writeCredentials: an access token is required.`). Noted equivalent, not a gap: `parseBoolEnv` treating `'0'` as "use the default" is unobservable because both boolean knobs (`IG_PRETTY_JSON`, `IG_ALLOW_DESTRUCTIVE`) default to `false` and `loadSettings(env)` takes no defaults — it becomes observable the day a boolean knob defaults to `true`. No production change | post-M5 · unit |
| CC-CFG-21 | `account: 'bra'` could have selected the `brand` token, `IG-LOGIN` could have passed as an auth path, and `ig_profile_BRAND_ACCESS_TOKEN` could have defined a profile | Found by mutation testing 2026-09-18 in `src/core/config.ts`, four near-miss survivors (rule 7). `resolveProfile`'s `p.name === target` loosened to `startsWith` survived because names were only ever resolved by their exact (case-folded) spelling — a typo would select a different account's token. `isAuthPath` folding case survived because auth paths were only tested by their two canonical values — a documented-invalid spelling would be accepted silently. The `IG_PROFILE_` prefix matched case-insensitively survived because the prefix tests moved the prefix (`OLD_IG_PROFILE_…`) but never respelled it — a stray lower-case variable would become a `brand` profile carrying its value as a token. And the alias guard `existing[suffix] !== undefined` reduced to truthiness survived (CC-PROC-46): a present-but-blank `IG_PROFILE_X_AUTH_PATH=''` then no longer blocks the `_AUTH_MODE` alias when the canonical key is enumerated first, so the same environment resolves differently depending on the order the process's `env` object enumerates. Closed by `resolveProfile matches the whole name — a prefix, an extension or a substring is unknown`, `an auth-path value is matched case-sensitively — IG-LOGIN and Fb-Login are unknown`, `the IG_PROFILE_ prefix is matched case-sensitively — ig_profile_ in another case defines nothing` (five near-miss keys, `profiles.map(name)` deepEqual `['default']`) and `a present-but-blank _AUTH_PATH beside an _AUTH_MODE alias resolves the same in both orders` (`test/core/config.test.ts`; renamed on 2026-09-19, see CC-CFG-22). Observation recorded at the time: a present-but-blank canonical `_AUTH_PATH` was filled by the `_AUTH_MODE` alias on the **default** profile but by **inference** on a named profile — the asymmetry CC-CFG-22 closed the next day. No production change in this pass | post-M5 · unit |
| CC-AUTH-31 | A Graph envelope with `code: "190"` would have taken the token-expired path on a string it never should have read as a code | Found by mutation testing 2026-09-18 in `src/core/errors.ts`. Every fixture gave `code` / `error_subcode` as numbers and `message` / `fbtrace_id` as strings or absent, so a `finiteNumber` that coerces (`Number(value)`) and a `nonEmptyString` fed `String(value)` both survived: `'190'`, `[190]` (which `Number()` reads as 190), `true` and `''` (→ 0) were never fed in, nor a numeric or object `message`. The consequences differ in kind: a coerced `"190"` classifies the error as `auth` — token-expired handling, a refresh, an operator told to log in — on a malformed envelope that carried no numeric code; a coerced `message` prints `[object Object]` where the status-only fallback (`Instagram Graph API error (HTTP 400)`) is the honest answer, and a coerced `fbtrace_id` fabricates a trace id (`"12345"`) Meta cannot look up. Wrong type, not just empty (CC-PROC-49): the guards were right, and no fixture could tell. Closed by `a code or subcode that is not a number is dropped, never coerced — "190" is not 190` (whole `{kind, status, code, subcode}` per fixture, five fixtures including `' 190 '`) and `a non-string message, error_user_msg or fbtrace_id is treated as absent, never coerced` (`test/core/errors.test.ts`). No production change | post-M5 · unit |
| CC-PROC-68 | `graph.instagram.com@evil.com` would have passed the host allowlist under a mutant that strips userinfo | Found by mutation testing 2026-09-18 in `src/core/host.ts` (`assertAllowedHost`). The allowlist tests covered a prefix, a suffix and a substring of the allowed host (the same file's version-pin guard learned that lesson in CC-PROC-41) but never a host string carrying userinfo, so `includes(normalized.slice(indexOf('@') + 1))` — accept whatever follows the last `@` — survived: `user@graph.instagram.com` slips through as harmless, and `graph.instagram.com@evil.com` (the allowlisted name as the *userinfo*, the attacker's host after the `@`) is the SSRF shape the allowlist exists to refuse. Both fence directions (CC-PROC-55): the suite proved the guard was not too loose on the host part and never that it was not too loose on the parts around it. Closed by extending `the allowlist is an exact match — never a prefix, suffix, or substring` with `user@graph.instagram.com`, `user:pass@graph.facebook.com` and `graph.instagram.com@evil.com` (`test/core/host.test.ts`). No production change | post-M5 · unit |
| CC-PROC-69 | `remaining: 0`, `retried: false`, `cursor: ''` and `time: 0` could all vanish from a log line — every log fixture used truthy values | Found by mutation testing 2026-09-18 in `src/core/log.ts`, three survivors under the CC-PROC-46 rule in the very file that rule was written from. The field-copy loop reduced to `if (value)` dropped nothing observable because no test ever wrote a record whose fields were `0`, `''`, `false` or `null` — exactly the values an operator reads to diagnose "why did it stop" (`remaining: 0`, `retried: false`, `fbtraceId: null`, a bound `attempt: 0`). `time` framed as `time \|\| null` and `printableTime` rejecting `0` both survived because every clock fixture read 1 ms or later: the epoch, or a consumer's fake clock at 0, was never on the wire, and both mutants render it as `null` — the same shape the clock-threw path uses, so a `time: 0` record reads as "timestamp dropped". Closed by `a falsy field value is a value — 0, empty string, false and null are written, not dropped` (whole-record `deepEqual`) and `a clock that reads zero stamps the record with 0 — the epoch is a time, not a missing one` (healthy and degraded framing both carry `time: 0`) in `test/core/log.test.ts`. No production change | post-M5 · unit |
| CC-PROC-70 | `IG_LOG_LEVEL=debug\n` would have enabled debug output, `timeout` would have been renamed `fields.timeout`, and the "fields dropped" marker could be redacted into `[cut]` | Found by mutation testing 2026-09-18 in `src/core/log.ts`, four survivors on the module's literals and on the direction of its redaction. `isLogLevel` folding case or trimming survived because the invalid-level tests used names outside the vocabulary (`loud`), never a variant of a real one — `INFO`, ` info`, `debug\n` (a mis-quoted env file) would be forgiven instead of falling back to `info` with the documented warning. The reserved-key set matched by `startsWith` survived because the shadowing tests used exact reserved names — `timeout`, `timestamp`, `msgId`, `levelName`, `logErrors` are ordinary caller fields that would silently change key on the wire. And in the degraded line (a record whose fields were not serialisable) the `logError` marker run through the redactor survived because no test exercised that path with a string-rewriting redactor: the failure reason **must** go through it (it can echo a hostile `toJSON` message), the framing — `level` and the marker — must not, or the operator loses the only clue that fields were dropped. Closed by `a near-miss level name is not a level — case and whitespace are not forgiven` (whole two-record output per fixture: the fallback warning with `requestedLevel` / `effectiveLevel`, then the info line), `only an exact reserved name is shadowed — a prefix or a case variant keeps its own key` and `the degraded line runs the reason through the redactor but never its own marker or level` (`msg` and `logErrorDetail` redacted to `[cut]`, `level: 'error'` and `logError: 'log fields dropped: not JSON-serialisable'` intact) in `test/core/log.test.ts`. No production change | post-M5 · unit |
| CC-CFG-22 | `IG_PROFILE_BIZ_AUTH_PATH=''` beside `IG_PROFILE_BIZ_AUTH_MODE=ig-login` inferred `fb-login`, while the same pair on the default profile honoured the alias | Follow-up to the CC-CFG-21 observation, 2026-09-19. `readDefaultRaw` resolved `clean(IG_AUTH_PATH) ?? clean(IG_AUTH_MODE)`, so a present-but-blank canonical fell through to the alias; `readNamedRaw` folded the two spellings while walking `Object.entries` and its alias guard (`existing[suffix] !== undefined`) treated the blank canonical as *set*, so the alias was dropped and the path went to inference — `fb-login` whenever app credentials were present. Same `.env` shape, a different Graph host, depending only on whether the keys were the bare or the prefixed ones; a `login`-written profile that an operator later blanked by hand (`IG_PROFILE_BIZ_AUTH_PATH=` to “unset” it, alias left in place) is exactly the shape that hits it. **Fixed 2026-09-19:** `readNamedRaw` now collects the alias spelling apart from the canonical one and folds it in after the walk with the same `clean(canonical) ?? clean(alias)` rule, so the outcome is order-independent and identical on both kinds of profile; the profile is still registered on its first key of either spelling, so an alias-led profile keeps its env position in `profiles` (and in the “configured profiles:” list of the unknown-account refusal). Closed by `a present-but-blank _AUTH_PATH beside an _AUTH_MODE alias falls through to the alias, in both orders` (named and default profile, same env shape, same answer), `both _AUTH_PATH and _AUTH_MODE blank on a named profile leave the path to inference` and `a profile whose first env key is the _AUTH_MODE alias keeps its env position` (`test/core/config.test.ts`); four hand mutants (alias-wins swap, late registration, alias fold dropped, canonical not cleaned) each killed by a named test | post-M5 · unit |
| CC-CFG-23 | `login --scope instagram_basic` (singular), `login --auth-path ig`, `login -x`, `login --bogus`: every unknown argument was dropped on the floor and the flow ran with the defaults | Candidate recorded in CC-CFG-17 and taken on 2026-09-19 in `src/cli/login.ts`. `parseArgs` had no `default:` case — a token that matched none of the known flags was skipped, so a mis-spelled flag was indistinguishable from none at all: `--scope` requested the default scopes, `--auth-path fb` / `--auth-mode fb` fell back to the `IG_AUTH_PATH` default or the `--path` usage error, `--app-secrett=…` left the secret to the env, and a stray positional (`login instagram`) was silently taken as nothing. **Fixed 2026-09-19:** the parser returns `{ unknownArgument, hint? }` for the first token it does not know — an unknown long flag, an unknown short flag, a positional that is not an auth-path name (`instagram`, a blank `''`), a second positional path (`login ig fb` → “the auth path is already given”) — and `runLogin` prints `login: unknown argument '<token>'.` plus the help text and exits 2 before `--help`, `--path` and every step that could touch the network or the config home. A `--flag=value` is named by its flag only (`'--app-secrett'`; the value is the one part of the line that could be a secret). Three near misses with a known origin carry a hint from a fixed map: `--scope` → “did you mean --scopes?”, `--auth-path` / `--auth-mode` (the env spellings) → “did you mean --path?”. `--help` no longer short-circuits parsing: `login --help --bogus` and `login --bogus --help` both refuse, naming only the first unknown token, so a help request never hides a mistake on the same line. A value-taking flag still consumes the next token whatever it looks like (`--scopes --bogus` is a scope list, not a refusal). One behaviour change alongside: a bare positional path now outranks the `IG_AUTH_PATH` env default exactly as `--path` does (`IG_AUTH_PATH=fb login ig` → ig-login; before, the env default won because the positional only filled an *unset* path). Closed by `an unknown long flag is refused with exit 2, the token named whole, and nothing past the parser runs`, `an unknown short flag is refused the same way, wherever it sits on the line`, `an unknown positional is refused: a near miss of a path name, a stray word, a blank token`, `a second positional path is refused whichever spelling gave the first: login ig fb stops`, `an unknown --flag=value echoes only the flag name, never the inline value`, `the near-miss hints are a fixed map, each keyed on a slip with a known origin`, `an unknown argument outranks --help on either side of it, and only the first one is named`, `a value-taking flag consumes the next token whatever it looks like, so it is never the unknown one`, `a bare positional outranks the environment default, exactly as --path does` and the renamed `the inline =value form belongs to the long flags: -p=ig is refused as an unknown argument` (`test/cli/login.test.ts`; every case pins the whole stderr and that the step log stays empty. The help half of that stderr is supplied by `helpText()`, which runs `--help` itself, so these assertions pin the refusal's *relation* to the usage — one line, a blank line, the whole usage — and nothing about the usage body's wording; the body is pinned separately, see [[CC-CFG-26]]). The entry's `argv.slice(3)` note in `src/index.ts` was rewritten in the same pass: `slice(2)` would now hand `'login'` to the parser as an unknown positional, so the bare-`login` entry test kills that mutant | post-M5 · unit |
| CC-PROC-71 | `instagram-mcp-ai docter` started the stdio MCP server, and `doctor --bogus` / `refresh now` ran the command with the token dropped | Symmetric follow-up to CC-CFG-23, 2026-09-19, in `src/index.ts`. The entry routed on `argv[2] === 'login' / 'doctor' / 'refresh'` and treated anything else as “no subcommand”, so a typo (`docter`, `serve`, `Login`) fell into the server branch: a process parked on stdin waiting for JSON-RPC, which from a terminal is a hang with no message — and one that first resolved the profile, so with no credentials it printed “No default profile configured” for what was a usage mistake. `doctor` and `refresh` take no arguments and ignored every extra token, so `doctor --help` ran the doctor and a mistyped flag was indistinguishable from a clean run. **Fixed 2026-09-19:** `assertUsage` runs right after the Node-version check and before the env file and the profiles are read. An empty argv still starts the server; `--help` / `-h` print the four-line usage to stderr and exit 0; any other first token that is not `login` / `doctor` / `refresh` is refused with `instagram-mcp-ai: unknown subcommand '<token>'.` plus the usage, exit 2; a token after `doctor` or `refresh` is refused with `<command>: unknown argument '<token>'; <command> takes no arguments.` plus the usage, exit 2, the token cut at its first `=` so an inline value is never echoed. `login` keeps its own parser (CC-CFG-23). stdout stays empty in every refusal — it is the protocol channel. No client config passes an argument to the server (the manifest, the plugin and the guides all launch the bare entry), so no launcher can hit the new exit. Closed by `an unknown subcommand is refused with exit 2 and the usage, before any profile is read` (`docter`, `serve`, `Login`, `refresh-token`, `--path`, `''` in a sandbox with no credentials at all; `--help` and `-h` pin the whole usage text, exit 0) and `doctor and refresh refuse any argument: the first stray token is named, a value after = is not` (`--app-secret=<secret>` names `--app-secret` and the secret is absent from stderr; the request log stays empty) in `test/index.test.ts` **Amended 2026-09-26:** both refusals (unknown subcommand and stray argument) now cut the echoed token at its first `=` and pass it through the token-shape redactor (CC-PROC-208). | post-M5 · unit |
| CC-PROC-72 | The token-shape backstop masks the server's own `IG_*` environment-variable **names**, so the one diagnostic that must print a name printed `[REDACTED]` | Found 2026-09-19 by the first CC-CFG-13 entry test: the startup warning listing unrecognised `IG_*` names came out as `names: ['[REDACTED]', '[REDACTED]', 'IG_WRITEMODE']`. The Instagram shape in `core/redact.ts` (and its deliberate copy in `core/errors.ts`, CC-PROC-19) was `/IG[A-Za-z0-9_-]{20,}/` — `IG` plus twenty body characters, underscore included — and `IG_PROFILE_DEFAULT_ACCESS_TOKEN` is `IG` plus twenty-nine. Every profile key clears the floor, and so does the plain `IG_PROFILE_<NAME>_ACCESS_TOKEN` in a "set …" error. The warning exists for exactly the profile-key typos it could not name; a tool error telling the operator which key is missing had the same hole, unnoticed because nothing asserted on a *name* passing through a sink. The over-redaction was policy ("preferred to under-redaction") and the corpus even pins a long `IGNORE_…` identifier as agreed collateral — but a name and a token are separable by one character: every token Meta mints has an alphanumeric family letter after `IG` (`IGQ…`, `IGAA…`), and every environment-variable name has an underscore there. **Fixed 2026-09-19** by narrowing that one position in **both** copies to `/IG[A-Za-z0-9][A-Za-z0-9_-]{19,}/`: the floor, the body alphabet (underscores and hyphens still legal *inside* a body) and the `IGNORE_…` over-redaction are unchanged, and CC-PROC-19's drift corpus gained three entries (`set IG_PROFILE_BRAND_ACCESS_TOKEN first` verbatim, an `IG-` run verbatim, an `IGQ` body of all underscores still masked) so the two dictionaries cannot disagree about it. Pinned in `core/redact.ts` by `an IG_* environment-variable name is not a token and is printed verbatim`. Residual, accepted: a token that genuinely began `IG_` or `IG-` would now rely on the exact-value pass alone — every configured and every minted token is registered there, which is the pass that does the real work anyway | post-M5 · unit |
| CC-PROC-73 | A resume that published nothing, a post that went live, and media still processing all journaled as one indistinguishable line: the action, against an id | Settled in passing 2026-09-02 and carried in §10 as a decision with no case row. `withWriteGate` journals whenever `result.isError !== true`, so the `already_published` arm — a call that re-sends nothing, and whose `targetId` is the *container* because there is no media id to plumb through (CC-PUB-4) — appended a record shaped exactly like a real publish, and the `in_progress` arm did the same for media that had not posted at all. The trail could say *that* a write happened and against which id, never *what happened to it*, and the arm an operator is most likely to read as a post is the one that published nothing. **Applied 2026-09-19:** `perform` may now report a `status` beside `targetId`, and `recordWrite` spreads it in under the same idiom — present when reported, the key absent and never `null` for a write whose action already names its only outcome, which is every write outside the composite publish flow. `executePublish` reports the three wire words the result itself carries — `published`, `already_published`, `in_progress` — so a journal line and the response the model received need no translating between two vocabularies; `instagram_publish_media` and the five comment writes report none. `status` does not match `SECRET_KEY_PATTERN`, so `redactJournalEntry` passes it through as written. **Pinned 2026-09-22**, because the change had shipped unpinned — no assertion anywhere read a record's `status`, so deleting the spread killed no test — by `the journal carries the outcome a perform reports, and only when it reports one` in `test/mcp/write-mode.test.ts` (whole-record assertion with the outcome present, then a second write reporting none, proving the key is absent on disk rather than serialized as null) and `each publish outcome is journaled by name, so the trail says which one happened` in `test/tools/publishing.test.ts` (all three arms end to end, each outcome checked together with the id it was journaled against) | post-M5 · unit |
| CC-PROC-74 | An operator-facing sentence runs under 100 % coverage while nothing asserts the half of it that says what to do next | Two `login` CLI messages were reachable, covered and unpinned. A listen failure reads as two sentences — the address and the errno, then `Another login may be running, or the port is taken — pass --redirect-uri with a free port that is also registered in your Meta app.` — and the test checked `127.0.0.1:8723`, `/EADDRINUSE/` and `err.cause`, never the second one: deleting the remedy left the suite green and the operator with an errno and no next step. `requireToken`'s `Token exchange response did not include an access_token.` was matched at three sites by `/access_token/`, a fragment that names the field, the query parameter and half the vocabulary of the file, so it survives nearly any rewrite of the line — including one that blames the wrong thing. **Pinned 2026-09-22** by whole-sentence equality in `test/cli/login.test.ts`: `a listen failure surfaces as a validation error naming the address and the way out` asserts the full message and keeps the `cause` check, and both `a 200 exchange without an access_token is an auth error, not a silent empty token` and `an access_token that arrives empty is refused like a missing one` assert the exact text — which is also what turns the second title's claim into a checked one. Dropping the remedy and rewording the token line now fail three tests. Coverage is blind to this class by construction: the line executes and the branch is taken, and only an assertion that reads the text can tell the shipped sentence from a worse one | post-M5 · unit |
| CC-PROC-75 | A message IS asserted, but by a fragment so short that a worse message still matches it | The weaker half of CC-PROC-74: coverage sees a passing assertion, so nothing flags it. A sweep of every `assert.match` / `.test()` over a message text in the suite whose pattern is 14 characters or less returned 54 sites; most are right as they stand — a structural shape (`/^[0-9a-f]{32}$/` for the OAuth state), a query parameter on a built URL, or a deliberately narrower companion to an exact-equality test elsewhere in the same file (`test/core/auth.test.ts` checks only the profile name at one site because it pins the whole fb-login remedy sentence at another; `test/mcp/registry.test.ts` pins the unknown-package sentence three times). Four were load-bearing and alone. The JSON-`null` exchange case read `/access_token/` and `/HTTP 400/`; `a blank Graph error message falls back to the HTTP status` read `/HTTP 500/` and asserted no `kind`, so it accepted the empty Graph message with a status appended — the exact outcome the case exists to prevent; `a non-JSON error body degrades to an HTTP-status message and never surfaces the page` read `/HTTP 502/` beside `!message.includes('helpdesk@corp.example')`, a blocklist of one string standing in for the claim that **nothing** of an attacker-influenced page is echoed into the operator's logs; and `redactResult: a redactor returning a record without content also withholds the result` read `.includes('withheld')`, eight characters of an eleven-word refusal whose `upstream` kind decides whether the model retries. **Pinned 2026-09-22** by whole-sentence equality plus `kind` and `status` in `test/cli/login.test.ts` and `test/mcp/registry.test.ts`. Proved with fragment-compatible mutants — rewrites that keep every fragment the old assertions read: `OAuth token exchange failed (HTTP ${status}). Check the app secret.` and `The exchange returned no access_token field.` were green before and now fail three `login` tests, and shortening the withhold sentence to `The result was withheld (redactor output unusable).` now fails two registry tests instead of one. The rule this leaves behind: a fragment pin is worth only what its shortest distinguishing content is worth, and a negative check against one known-bad substring is not the same claim as an exact match | post-M5 · unit |
| CC-PROC-76 | A tool description reads correctly to a human, but no test pins the clause the model acts on | A `description` and its `.describe()` texts are not documentation: they are the only instructions the model gets before it decides whether, and with what arguments, to spend a call. Coverage cannot see a word of them — they are strings hanging off a spec object, so rewording one runs every line of the suite exactly as before, and a clause can be deleted in a tidy-up with a green `check`. A per-package pass pinned the load-bearing clauses of every tool description and argument text by exact fragment, with a comment per clause naming what a model that read the opposite would do. Two corrections came out of it. First, the survey instrument was wrong: counting `assertMentions` calls measured an idiom, not coverage. `publishing` scored zero and was taken for unpinned, but already carried about 25 pinned fragments written as `assert.equal(doc.includes(f), true, msg)`, while `account`, `comments` and `media` pinned each whole description as one opaque `deepEqual` blob. So the pass buys first coverage only for `insights`, `discovery` and the 27 clauses `publishing` had left open; elsewhere it buys per-clause failure locality and a recorded reason per clause, which a blob equality cannot give. Second, deciding novelty by substring relation (is a candidate fragment contained in, or containing, some already-asserted one) reports false duplicates: `FINISHED` was pinned on the `creationId` field text, which silently absorbed a proposed pin of `poll instagram_get_container_status until FINISHED` on a different string entirely. The correct test is literal presence of the fragment earlier in the file, then a judgement on whether it is asserted about the same string. | post-M5 · unit |
| CC-CFG-24 | `XDG_CONFIG_HOME=.` wrote a 0600 credentials file into whatever directory the MCP client happened to start the server in — and a test had already done it, leaving `<repo>/instagram-mcp-ai/.env` in the working tree | Found 2026-09-22 by forensics on repo-root litter, not by mutation. A `-rw-------` `.env` under a `drwx------` `instagram-mcp-ai/` had sat in the repo root since 2026-09-06; its two keys were `IG_ACCESS_TOKEN` and `IG_AUTH_PATH`, and the token was byte-identical to `LONG_TOKEN` in `test/core/config-write.test.ts` with `authPath: 'ig-login'` — so the suite wrote it, and the file header (“written by the `login` CLI”) is only the template `config-write.ts` emits. The mechanism: `resolveConfigHome` honoured any value `clean()` returned and `configHome` honoured any explicit `configDir`, so a value that was merely *relative* went straight into `path.join(base, 'instagram-mcp-ai', '.env')` and resolved against the process cwd. Only the blank shape had been guarded (CC-CFG-8's `a blank configDir override falls back to the platform config home`), which is why the adjacent hole survived. For an operator the blast radius is larger than for the suite: an MCP client starts the server in a directory of its own choosing, so an `XDG_CONFIG_HOME=.` inherited from a shell profile drops a live token beside whatever project is open (ignored by that project's VCS only by luck), and the next start from another directory reads a different file and reports the account as unconfigured while the token sits in a forgotten folder. `$XDG_STATE_HOME` had the same shape one file over, where it scatters the applied-write journal — the audit trail of every post — so that no single file answers “what did this server post?”. **Fixed 2026-09-22** by the rule the XDG base-directory spec already states: a base-directory variable is honoured only when absolute, otherwise it is ignored in favour of the documented default — `absoluteHome()` in `src/core/config-write.ts` on both the `%APPDATA%` and `$XDG_CONFIG_HOME` branches, and the same check on `$XDG_STATE_HOME` in `src/core/settings.ts`. Two deliberate exceptions: an explicit `IG_ENV_FILE` / `IG_WRITE_JOURNAL` names one file rather than a base directory and is still used verbatim (`a path knob is not validated` stays true), and an explicit `configDir` is a caller's own choice so it is obeyed — but `path.resolve`d, because the returned path is what the CLI prints for the operator to inspect and `./instagram-mcp-ai/.env` names a different file the moment they change directory. The fix inverts the risk for the suite, which is the second half of this row: a relative config home no longer lands in the cwd, it lands in the developer's REAL `~/.config/instagram-mcp-ai/.env`, where `writeCredentials` would merge a fake token over a live `IG_ACCESS_TOKEN` and the test would still pass. So `configHomeEnv` in `test/helpers/config-home.ts` now throws on a non-blank relative directory (blank is still allowed: it is the “set but empty” shape the resolver's own tests inject, and resolving a path writes nothing). Closed by `a RELATIVE config home is ignored, because it would follow the process cwd` (both platform branches, five relative spellings, and a padded absolute value still honoured) and `a relative configDir override still produces an absolute credential path` in `test/core/config-write.test.ts`, `a RELATIVE XDG_STATE_HOME is ignored, so the audit trail cannot follow the cwd` and `an explicit IG_WRITE_JOURNAL may still be relative — it names a file, not a base` in `test/core/settings.test.ts`, and `configHomeEnv refuses a relative directory, which would name the real config home` in `test/harness.test.ts`. One more test guards the artefact rather than the code: `no test wrote a credentials store into the repository itself` fails on the presence of `<repo>/instagram-mcp-ai`, because this leak outlives the process that makes it — whichever run creates the directory, the next run names it. Four hand mutants (each of the two source guards removed, the harness throw disabled, a leaked directory created) each killed exactly one of those tests | post-M5 · unit |

Structurally, a probe taken here over all 37 files in `src/` twelve seconds apart found nothing moving at all while a lane was demonstrably live, which is the point: contention is intermittent, and a detector that samples exactly twice per mutant samples it the same way and sees the quiet stretches. The replacement is the **paired-run protocol**: for each mutant, run the full measurement set clean, then with the mutant staged, **in the same window**, and take the set difference of failing test **names**. Run the same build from the repo root and it is 1879/1879, exit 0. The failures are loud but they are all in the same direction (a file the test cannot find), which is exactly what a real regression would look like, so the channel is only safe stated whole: **build to a scratch `--outDir`, pass explicit file paths, and run with `cwd` = the repo root**. | post-M5 · process |

Four reproducible sub-classes. (1) DECLARED-TYPE PREMISE: three notes in `src/api/**` argued from a TypeScript field type in a module where `req<…>` CASTS the wire body instead of validating it, so `statusCode?: string` and `GraphListResponse.data?: T[]` describe what Graph sends, not what the compiler enforces — the sound argument is REACHABILITY, and a malformed `status_code: 0` or `data: ''` would in fact separate `??` from `\|\|`. (2) ENUMERATION OMITTING A MEMBER ([[CC-PROC-114]]): `createMediaContainer`’s “every arm below is guarded by an exact `!== undefined` test” is false of `isCarouselItem === true`, and `defineTool`’s list of what an arrow leaves unchanged omitted `.prototype` and constructability. (3) NON-SEQUITUR: `src/mcp/result.ts` derived non-overlap of the two fence delimiters from “both start with `[` and both end with `]`”, which implies nothing — `[a[b]` and `[b]c]` satisfy it and overlap; the valid argument, that neither string contains a `[` anywhere but index 0, sat two sentences away. (4) MIS-CITED MEASUREMENT ([[CC-PROC-118]]). The rule: an audit lane must re-derive every PREMISE from the code, not merely run the mutation, and a lane whose only output is a verdict has audited half the note. Cross-ref [[CC-PROC-109]]. | post-M5 · process |
| CC-PROC-118 | An equivalence note that cites a measurement is asserting a fact about a RUN, and that citation rots exactly the way prose does — nobody re-takes it when the surrounding code moves. | `src/mcp/transport.ts`’s `close` loop carried “Dropping BOTH does hang”, a claim whose shape made the pair look unmeasurable in principle. Re-measured 2026-09-23 under [[CC-PROC-116]] discipline (Python `str.replace` under `assert count == 1`, the drop printing `applied 1 1`): control `tag=TCTRL exit=0 seconds=1 notok=0`, mutant `tag=TBOTH exit=1 seconds=45 notok=2`, one new killer — “shutdown closes an exchange that is still in flight before it stops the listener”. It does not hang. TAP reports `duration_ms: 5015.939209` and `failureType: 'testCodeFailure'` with `error: 'timed out waiting for shutdown to close the in-flight transport'`, raised by the test’s own `waitFor` bound at about five seconds. The subagent lane that flagged the note was wrong about the mechanism too, describing a runner timeout under `--test-timeout=90000`. Three descriptions, one fact: the guarantee IS measured, by a named test, in five seconds. The note now says that, and still keeps the loop — which of the two halves provides the guarantee is not separable in process, and a graceful teardown and a severed socket look identical to `httpServer.close()`. Rule: when a note cites a run, re-take the run before trusting either the note or the report questioning it ([[CC-PROC-109]]). | post-M5 · process |
| CC-AUTH-44 | A caller injects something that is not a function at `refreshToken`'s `exchange` seam — `0`, `''`, `false`, `null` — from JavaScript, or from an untyped consumer of the shipped `.d.ts`. | The bad value is KEPT: the call fails closed with a local `TypeError`, having opened no socket, and is never discarded in favour of the default transport. `??` and `\|\|` are NOT interchangeable here — `params.exchange` is a declaration, not a validation, exactly as `exchange<TokenExchangeWire>` eight lines below is called a cast rather than a check, and exactly as `accessToken` above is validated at runtime because “this is exported, so the compiler is not the only caller”. They separate in the direction that matters: `\|\|` would discard the bad value, build a real `createTokenExchange()` and put `client_secret` on a live request to `graph.facebook.com`. Found 2026-09-23 by the core-B equivalence audit, whose note claimed the two spellings were indistinguishable because “a `TokenExchangeFn` is never falsy” — true of a VALUE of that type, and beside the point for a seam nothing validates. No `typeof` guard was added upstream: a garbage transport is the caller’s programming error, the behaviour already fails closed, and a guard would make the two spellings genuinely interchangeable and remove the only thing that makes the choice observable. Pinned by “a non-function transport at the exchange seam fails closed, off the network”, which asserts the FETCH COUNT is zero rather than only reading the error type, so it keeps its meaning if the file’s network rail is ever respelled. See [[CC-PROC-117]]. | post-M5 · unit |
| CC-PROC-119 | An equivalence note names WHICH guard makes a mutant unobservable, and names the wrong one of two that both hold. | A note of the form “X is unobservable because Y” is read by the next maintainer as a maintenance instruction: Y is the thing not to touch. When two guards both hold and the note credits the one that is not doing the work, it misdirects exactly the future edit it exists to prevent — and unlike a false premise ([[CC-PROC-117]]) it survives review, because everything it says is true. Two instances on 2026-09-23, both with correct conclusions. `src/core/auth.ts` credited `GraphHost` having exactly two members and said a third host would be needed to separate `host === FB_HOST` from `host !== IG_HOST`; the type is erased, the provider is exported, `http.ts` hands `authParams` the caller’s raw host spelling after `assertAllowedHost` compared only a trimmed, lower-cased copy, so `' graph.instagram.com '` separates them today — and what stops the mutant reaching the wire is `buildUrl` re-testing that exact spelling against the allowlist ([[CC-DATA-17]] (2)). `src/core/settings.ts` credited the `\d{1,3}` octet cap for refusing `1270.0.0.1` when `Number(p) <= 255` refuses it independently, so widening the digit cap alone changes nothing and it is the RANGE test that must not be loosened. The check is cheap and mechanical: for each guard the note leans on, ask whether the mutant becomes observable when that guard ALONE is removed. If the answer is no, the note is crediting the wrong conjunct. | post-M5 · process |
| CC-PROC-120 | An equivalence note carries a scope LABEL wider than the property it measured — a header reading `(module-wide)` over a body that only reasons about the module’s caller-supplied input fields. | The label is what a reader checks a guard against, so it must name the measured scope, not the file. The audit of 2026-09-23 found `src/tools/publishing.ts` labelled `(module-wide)` while five `x !== undefined` guards on API-RESPONSE fields in the same module — `st.statusCode`, `st.status`, `limit.quotaTotal`, `limit.quotaDuration`, `limit.remaining` — are non-equivalent and pinned by two tests. This is not [[CC-PROC-114]]: the enumeration under the label was correct and complete for the fields it named, and only the header over-claimed, which is exactly why review passed it. Mechanical check: take the label literally, pick a member of that scope the body never mentions, and mutate it. | post-M5 · process |
| CC-PROC-121 | An equivalence note draws its scope boundary by asserting how the mutant OUTSIDE the boundary behaves — “dropping the option entirely is a different mutant and is not what this note covers; that one hangs the runner” — and that assertion was never measured and is false. | An exclusion is a claim about the EXCLUDED mutant, so it carries its own burden of measurement; the covered mutant’s verdict says nothing about it. `src/core/clock.ts` pinned `{ once: true }` relaxed to `{ once: false }` correctly, then pushed the option-less spelling out of scope with a runtime claim about it. Measured 2026-09-23 in a snapshot, both mutants built and proven applied in the emit: they are the SAME mutant. `addEventListener(type, listener)` with no options is spec-identical to `{ once: false }`, and both are killed by one test — “systemClock.sleep detaches its abort listener when the signal aborts” — at the same line with the same assertion (`1 !== 0`, expected `0`, actual `1`), zero `testTimeoutFailure` under a 20s per-test limit, each run finishing in under a second. Mirror image of [[CC-PROC-120]]: there the label was too WIDE for the body; here it is too NARROW, and what makes the narrowing look earned is a false claim about the neighbour rather than a gap in the enumeration ([[CC-PROC-114]]). Mechanical check: for every neighbour a note pushes out of scope, mutate the neighbour too and diff the kill sets — identical sets mean the boundary is fiction. Same “does hang” shape as [[CC-PROC-118]], found in a different file by a different lane, which makes it a habit rather than a slip. | post-M5 · process |

Measured 2026-09-23: each of the three, mutated on its own to a truthiness test, SURVIVED the whole suite — a real gap, not a redundancy. `test/cli/doctor.test.ts` now carries “a ZERO code, subcode or status is still named in the diagnostic bracket”, and each of the three was re-measured against it separately rather than in one batched run ([[CC-PROC-114]]). `mapGraphError` was already pinned to carry a zero through at `test/core/errors.test.ts:946`; this closes the display end of the same promise. | post-M5 · unit |
| CC-DATA-46 | `doctor`’s reachability line guarded the handle clause on presence alone (`account.username !== undefined`), so a CLEARED Instagram username — which the API returns as `''`, not as an absent key — rendered ` (@)`: a handle clause naming no handle. | Guard `!== undefined && !== ''` in the DISPLAY layer, not in `api/account.ts`. | CC-CFG-32 | `PACKAGE_PROFILES` was sealed with a single `Object.freeze` on the table while its three member arrays stayed mutable, under a test whose own comment said the freeze exists so “no importer ... can widen a profile at runtime”. `Object.isFrozen(PACKAGE_PROFILES)` answers `true` while `PACKAGE_PROFILES.core.push('discovery')` succeeds: the assertion certified the shallow fact and the property the comment claimed was false. | Freeze both levels and assert the property rather than the call. A profile list widened in-process changes the tool surface of every server started afterwards, which is what `IG_TOOL_PACKAGES=publisher` exists to bound. The `deepEqual` beside it is a different claim — it pins the members at import time and cannot see a later push. Measured 2026-09-23: dropping the freeze kills the new test and nothing else. | post-M5 · unit |
| CC-PROC-122 | A census greps ONE spelling of a marker and then states a conclusion about the whole population. | Enumerate the spellings before counting. The equivalence-note sweep grepped `Equivalent-mutant note:`, which matches 122 sites in `src/` — but four more sites carry the same claim under `Equivalent-mutant note ` without the colon (2), `Equivalent-mutant notes:` (1) and `Equivalence note,` (4), and every one of them fell outside the "all notes measured" claim in WORKLOG entry 91. | CC-PROC-123 | A mutation runner restores only the file it is about to mutate, while an earlier tag in the same loop mutated a different file. | Restore every file the run can touch, not the one this tag edits. Restore the whole file set from the pristine copy before each tag, and prove the intended edit landed ([[CC-PROC-116]]). | post-M5 · process |
| CC-PROC-124 | A test pins a sentence of source prose by a fragment that ends on the last word before the source’s line wrap. | Choose fragments that cross a wrap or sit well inside one line. A fragment flush against a wrap is pinned to the FORMATTING, not to the words: re-wrapping the comment or the string at a different width breaks the test while changing no word, and the failure then reads as a content change and sends the next reader looking for a behavioural regression that is not there. The clause-coverage probe compares on collapsed whitespace for exactly this reason. | post-M5 · process |
| CC-PROC-125 | `Object.freeze` is applied to a `Set` or a `Map` to make an exported collection immutable. | It is decoration. A Set’s members live in internal slots rather than own properties, so `.add()` and `.delete()` succeed silently on a frozen Set even under strict mode while `Object.isFrozen` still answers `true` (measured 2026-09-23). On a frozen ARRAY, `push`, index assignment and `length` all throw `TypeError`. Export a frozen array and look the member up with `.includes()`. Swept 2026-09-23 after the `cli/scopes.ts` and `mcp/registry.ts` conversions: all 12 `Object.freeze` call sites in `src/` now freeze an array or a plain object, and no Set or Map is frozen anywhere. See [[CC-CFG-33]]. | post-M5 · process |
| CC-PROC-126 | A comment refutes itself — the claim in one sentence is contradicted by the mechanism the next sentence explains. | `src/tools/ids.ts` said a mutation pass removing `.min(1)` and the pattern one at a time “reports two survivors where there is no defect”, and its own next sentence named the mechanism — the `minLength: 1` the call puts into the published JSON Schema — by which one of the two is killed. Measured 2026-09-23: dropping `.min(1)` is killed by five tests, every one a published-contract pin and not one a rejection test; dropping the pattern is killed by eleven. It reports none. A survival claim is a measurement ([[CC-PROC-109]]), and the cheapest test of one is to read the sentences around it. | post-M5 · process |
| CC-PROC-127 | An `Object.freeze` sits one level shallower than the property the comment beside it claims, certified by an `Object.isFrozen` assertion that reads only the shallow fact. | Assert the property, not the call. `Object.isFrozen(X)` is true for a one-level freeze whose members are still mutable, so the assertion passes on exactly the value the comment says it rules out; pair it with a mutation the property forbids (`assert.throws(() => members.push(...), TypeError)`). | CC-CFG-34 | The published tool surface was sealed with one `Object.freeze` on the array of specs, leaving every `ToolSpec` in it — and the `annotations` record inside that — an ordinary writable object. | Freeze the members, not the container. `mcp/registry.ts` decides the forced-read-only surface with `spec.annotations.readOnlyHint !== true`, and `readOnlyHint` is an optional field on a plain object, so before this freeze two property writes from anything that could reach a spec turned a deployment that asked for `reader` into one that hides and deletes comments as the operated account (measured 2026-09-23). `src/tools/index.ts` now freezes each spec, its annotations and its input/output shapes on the way out, and stops there: `input` and `output` hold zod instances that memoise on the instance, so freezing a schema would turn the library’s own bookkeeping into a `TypeError` thrown from inside zod. | CC-CFG-35 | A vocabulary is frozen and the freeze is justified by “`z.enum` captures the live array”. Half of that is true, and the false half is the one the justification rests on. | Measured against zod 3.25.76 on 2026-09-23: `z.enum(ARRAY)` stores the array BY REFERENCE — `schema._def.values` is that same array — and `.options` and `.enum` read through to it forever, but the parse path memoises the member set on the FIRST parse. So a push landing before the schema’s first use widens VALIDATION, while a push landing after it widens only what the schema ADVERTISES: the `inputSchema` that `zod-to-json-schema` renders for the client, and the metric list `${ACCOUNT_METRICS.join(', ')}` interpolates into the tool description. The two halves then disagree — the server offers a metric it will refuse — which is worse than either drift alone, and it is why the vocabularies in `src/api/insights.ts` and `src/api/media-spec.ts` are frozen rather than merely typed `readonly`. | CC-PROC-128 | A `c8 ignore` block is written around a STATEMENT so the coverage gate stops asking about one unreachable BRANCH inside it. | Exempt the arm, not the code that holds it. Ten blocks were audited on 2026-09-23; six were wider than the fact they stated. The worst exempted `buildManifest`’s only `return` and the arrow that builds each package entry — the lines every manifest test drives — for a `?? []` on a `Map.get` whose key came from `groups.keys()`; another spent `/* c8 ignore next 3 */` on a whole function for one `\|\| '(none)'`. Nothing fails when the gate stops watching covered code, which is why these survive review: the metric stays at 100% and says less. Placement is mechanical and was measured, not reasoned: v8-to-istanbul anchors a branch record at the START of the whole logical or conditional expression, so a hint on the line above the unreachable operand leaves the record standing (`doctor.ts` still reported the `??` head while the ignore sat one line lower); the whole expression must be on the ignored line and the statement on its own. Only the `/* … */` form is recognised — `// c8 ignore next` is silently inert — and Prettier will hoist or inline a hint that sits mid-expression, moving what “next” points at without touching the comment text. Two of the ten also stated the wrong reason ([[CC-PROC-119]]): one named the `Object.hasOwn` guard that does NOT exclude `'all'` (the enclosing `else` does), the other said `listenHostFor` raises only an `InstagramError` when its own `new URL` raises `TypeError` — what rules that out is the earlier parse of the same string, not the thrower. | post-M5 · process |
| CC-PROC-129 | A scrape is widened to catch a drift it was blind to, and the widened pattern is blind to the same drift for a new reason — its own boundary. | Run the widening against the drift; do not reason about it. `test/core/errors.test.ts` checked that every module path the `core/errors.ts` docstrings name resolves to a real file, but only inside backticks, so re-stating the [[CC-PROC-17]] phantom as plain prose restored the defect with the test green. The widened scrape ended in `(?![\w./-])` — a boundary that rejects a match followed by a dot, which is exactly what a path wears at the end of a sentence. The drift the positive control had planted, `The authoritative redaction layer is mcp/redact.ts.`, sailed through the new pattern as cleanly as through the old one; reading the regex had not shown it, and only the control did ([[CC-PROC-112]]). The boundary a path scrape wants is `(?![\w-])`: a path may legitimately END on the characters its separators use. The general rule is that widening a pattern is a mutation of the test, and a mutation is measured, not declared — the same standard [[CC-PROC-109]] sets for a note. | post-M5 · process |
| CC-PROC-130 | A test name quantifies over a set — *every* key, or an unqualified subject — and the population it actually drives is a list typed into the test. | Read the population from the subject, not from memory. `test/mcp/write-mode.test.ts`’s *“every documented id key names the target”* drove nine key names hand-typed into a `cases` array with nothing tying them to `TARGET_ID_KEYS` in `src/mcp/write-mode.ts`. Measured 2026-09-23: appending a tenth key for a new write tool keeps the suite green while *every* quietly stops being true — that tool’s prompt then reads `(none — this call creates new content)` for a call that edits an existing object. Worse, the array’s ORDER is the most-specific-first rule the prompt depends on, and alphabetising it — an ordinary tidy-up — also survived: every existing fixture carries one key, or two whose winner is the same either way, so an intent carrying `targetId` beside `mediaId` would have named the container instead of the object consent was given for.
