| CC-INS-16 | A caller passes `metrics: []` to an insights tool. | **Found by audit 2026-09-23.** The schemas had no minimum length, and the api layer substitutes its default only for an absent list, so an empty one went out as `metric=`: a rate-limited call on a request Graph rejects, against a description that promises a default set. All three `metrics` arrays are now `.min(1)`, so the call is refused before it is sent. Closed by ``an EMPTY metric list is refused by all three tools, not sent as `metric=` `` and the pinned `minItems: 1` in `real McpServer: the published contract of every insights tool is pinned exactly`. | post-M5 · unit |
| CC-PROC-198 | The stdio client exits while a call is still running, and the next response is written to a closed stdout. | **Found by audit 2026-09-23.** The SDK's `StdioServerTransport` listens for `'error'` on stdin only, so the `EPIPE` on stdout was an uncaught exception that killed the process. Measured on the built entry: an applied write whose Graph POST was already sent lost its journal line because a concurrent read answered first. `startStdio` now handles stdout errors by the logger's own sink-gone rule: it warns once and closes the transport, so the process exits after in-flight calls finish, and any other stdout error is rethrown. Closed by `a client that closes stdout does not kill the calls still in flight` and `a stdout failure that is not a closed pipe still reaches Node untouched`. | post-M5 · unit |
| CC-PROC-199 | A test file is renamed away from `*.test.ts`: `.spec.ts`, `-test.ts`, `.test.mts` or `.cjs`. | **Found by audit 2026-09-23.** Both directions of the corpus guard are keyed on the `.test.ts` suffix, so on a fresh build the renamed file either compiled to a name the runner glob never matches or was not compiled at all, and the test left the suite with every gate green. The guard now fails on any code file under `test/` that imports `node:test` and is not named `*.test.ts`. Closed by `the corpus guard fails a test file whose name the runner glob never reaches`. | post-M5 · unit |
| CC-PROC-200 | The release workflow runs the quality gate with the npm token in scope, and a manual dispatch skips the tag/version check. | **Found by audit 2026-09-23.** `npm publish` ran the gate through `prepublishOnly`, so every dev dependency and test child inherited `NODE_AUTH_TOKEN`. The version check ran only on a `release` event, so a `workflow_dispatch` from any branch published that branch as `latest`. The gate is now its own step before the token is set, publish runs with `--ignore-scripts`, and the check is unconditional against the release tag or the dispatched ref. Closed by `the release job runs the gate before the npm token is in scope`. | post-M5 · unit |
| CC-COM-13 | Graph declines a hide, unhide, delete or comments toggle with `{ success: false }` | **Found by audit 2026-09-23.** All four handlers awaited the api call, discarded its acknowledgement and returned the echoed argument (`{ hidden: id }`), so the model was told the comment was hidden and the write gate — which journals every result that is not an error — recorded a moderation action that never happened. The handlers now raise `kind: upstream` ("Instagram declined to hide comment C1 (success: false).") on an explicit `success: false`; an ack with no `success` key discloses nothing and is still not read as a refusal. Closed by `a write Graph declined with success:false is raised, not reported as done or journaled` and `an acknowledgement without a success key is not read as a refusal`. | post-M5 · unit |
| CC-DATA-53 | A comment's `text`/`username`, a tagged item's `caption`, or a `replies.data` arrives in a shape the cast did not promise | **Found by audit 2026-09-23.** The api layer casts the Graph body, so a `null` text reached `fence()` and threw `Cannot read properties of null (reading 'split')`, and a truthy non-array `replies.data` reached `.map` — one odd row failed the whole `list_comments` / `get_comment` / `list_tagged_media` page. A non-string field is now dropped (an empty string stays fenced) and a non-array reply envelope is read as "not disclosed". Closed by `a null or non-string comment text, username or caption is dropped, not a crashed page` and ``a reply envelope whose data is not an array is read as "not disclosed", never mapped``. | post-M5 · unit |
| CC-DATA-54 | The token can act on more Facebook Pages than `/me/accounts` returns in one page (25 by default) | **Found by audit 2026-09-23.** `listLinkedAccounts` made one GET and ignored `paging`, so every Page past the first page was dropped without a trace — including, possibly, the one linked to the account the operator wanted. It now walks the edge through the shared `fetchPagedEdge` up to `IG_MAX_ITEMS`; the first request is byte-identical to before. A list longer than the cap is still cut without a signal, because the tool's `{ items }` output contract is pinned — recorded, not changed. **Corrected 2026-09-23:** the cut is now signalled — see CC-DATA-55. Closed by `listLinkedAccounts follows the cursor so Pages past the first page are not dropped`, `listLinkedAccounts stops at the item cap instead of walking an unbounded edge` and `list_linked_accounts lists Pages from every page of the edge, bounded by IG_MAX_ITEMS`. | post-M5 · unit |
| CC-CFG-42 | `IG_ENV_FILE` names a path that does not exist, is a directory, or cannot be read | **Found by audit 2026-09-23** — CC-CFG-5 promised a clear startup error, and none existed. The explicit path replaces the candidate list, and the loop loaded only files that existed, so a typo loaded no env file at all: the operator read "No default profile configured" about a file they had configured, and with a token in the real environment `doctor` exited 0 without any other knob that file set. The named file is now loaded on its own and dotenv's `error` is refused before anything else runs, naming the variable and the path; the explicit branch keeps `override: false`. Closed by `an IG_ENV_FILE that names no readable file refuses to start and names the variable` and `an explicit IG_ENV_FILE never overrides a value the MCP client passed in`. | post-M5 · unit |
| CC-PROC-201 | The operator-facing docs describe behaviour the code does not have | **Found by audit 2026-09-23.** A claim-by-claim check of `docs/tools.md`, `security.md`, `operations.md`, `troubleshooting.md`, `stability.md` and `messaging.md` against the code found 24 false statements, all corrected in place: previews reporting publishing-quota impact and composites pre-checking the quota (neither exists); refusal at 100 % BUC usage and usage exposed by `token_status` (it reports `rateLimitBudget.available: false`); reconciliation against `recently_searched_hashtags` (never called); character-budget truncation of results (none); per-tool done/error/duration logs (only `tool invoked` and `graph request` at debug); the Graph version constant living in `core/settings.ts` (it is `GRAPH_VERSION` in `core/host.ts`); a `login --config-dir` flag and an `@mention` tool (neither exists); Path-A expiry being reported and warned on (`IG_TOKEN_EXPIRES_AT` is written but never read, so it is always unknown) — since read back, see CC-AUTH-52); Tier-1 refresh needing the app secret (it does not); `get_container_status` being idempotent-annotated; the HTTP transport binding non-loopback with a token (any non-loopback host is refused); and the write gate described without its elicitation stage. Remaining doc drift in `.env.example`, `architecture.md` and `plugin-install.md`'s npm-publication callouts is out of this pass. | post-M5 · docs |
| CC-DATA-55 | `instagram_list_linked_accounts` on a token with more Pages than `IG_MAX_ITEMS` | **Found by audit 2026-09-23.** CC-DATA-54 made the tool walk the edge but kept its bare `{ items }` output, so a list the cap cut short read as the complete set. `listLinkedAccounts` now returns the whole paged result, and the tool publishes `paging.truncated`, a resumable `paging.after` (and the walk `note` when the cap cuts a page) exactly as `instagram_list_media` does; a new optional `after` input continues the listing. Closed by `listLinkedAccounts reports a capped walk as truncated instead of a complete set`, `listLinkedAccounts resumes from a caller-supplied cursor`, `list_linked_accounts reports a capped walk as truncated, with a cursor to resume from` and `list_linked_accounts publishes the walk note when the cap cuts a page`. | post-M5 · unit |
| CC-COM-14 | `instagram_hide_comment` applied to a comment the media owner wrote on its own media | **Found by audit 2026-09-23 (closes CC-COM-5(a)).** Instagram acknowledges the call and keeps the comment visible, and the tool answered with a bare `{ hidden }`. The acknowledgement carries no author, and telling the cases apart would cost a GET of the comment and of the account id, so every applied hide now carries a fixed `note` stating the rule, and the tool description states it up front. Closed by `hide_comment tells the model an owner-authored comment stays visible (CC-COM-5a)`. | post-M5 · unit |
| CC-AUTH-52 | A Path A token whose expiry `login` / `refresh` recorded in `IG_TOKEN_EXPIRES_AT` (or `IG_PROFILE_<P>_TOKEN_EXPIRES_AT`) | **Found by audit 2026-09-23.** The record was written and never read, so `instagram_token_status` and `doctor` reported every Path A expiry as unknown, even for a lapsed token. `loadProfiles` now reads it into `tokenExpiresAtSec`. Only a recordable expiry is accepted: a whole number of Unix seconds from `1` to `253402300799` (9999-12-31T23:59:59Z, CC-AUTH-66), or `0` for never. Anything else is unknown, never an error, and the key alone creates no profile. `doctor` labels the line with the env var it came from. Because the record can be stale (a token pasted by hand, or one the MCP client passes in), `doctor` reports a lapsed record as a warning and leaves the verdict to the reachability check. Closed by `the recorded expiry reads back for the default and named profiles; 0 means never`, `a malformed recorded expiry reads as unknown, never as a crash or a bogus date`, `a named profile reads only its own expiry key, not a sibling field`, `an expiry recorded for a profile that has no other keys creates no profile`, `token_status (Path A) reports the expiry recorded by login/refresh, still with no network call` and `path A reports the expiry login/refresh recorded, naming where it came from`. | post-M5 · unit |
| CC-AUTH-53 | `login` / `refresh` writes a token whose exchange returned no lifetime over a profile that already has a recorded expiry | **Found by audit 2026-09-23.** The old `TOKEN_EXPIRES_AT` line survived the rewrite; once CC-AUTH-52 reads it back, it would report the new token on the old token's schedule. `writeCredentials` now removes that profile's own record when the new credentials carry no `expiresAtSec`, leaves other profiles' records alone, and rewrites an existing record in place when they do. Closed by `a write without expiresAtSec removes the old expiry, and only that profile's`, `a write with expiresAtSec updates the recorded expiry in place` and `expiresAtSec is persisted and reads back as the profile expiry`. | post-M5 · unit |
| CC-AUTH-54 | Startup fails with a message that echoes a secret (e.g. a token pasted into `IG_AUTH_MODE`, echoed by the config refusal) | **Found by audit 2026-09-23.** `main().catch` wrote `err.message` to stderr verbatim. It now routes the message through `createRedactor()` — the live secret registry plus the token-shape backstop — so a token never reaches the startup-failure line as typed. Closed by `the startup-failure line is redacted: a token echoed by an error never reaches stderr`. | post-M5 · unit |
| CC-PROC-202 | The remaining docs describe behaviour the code does not have (the pass after CC-PROC-201) | **Found by audit 2026-09-23.** A claim-by-claim check of `plugin-install.md`, `auth.md`, `setup-guide.md`, `architecture.md`, `release-checklist.md`, `workplan.md`, `roadmap.md`, `docs/index.html` and `.env.example` corrected: the npm package described as unpublished (0.7.0 is on npm); the landing page's write gate described as needing BOTH `apply:true` and `IG_WRITE_MODE=apply` (either suffices, and `apply:false` always previews); `IG_TOOL_PACKAGES` as a comma/space list (commas only); `IG_REFRESH_AFTER_DAYS` as an auto-refresh threshold (nothing refreshes automatically — it is the `expiring_soon` warning threshold); npm publishing as OIDC trusted publishing (it authenticates with `NPM_TOKEN`; OIDC signs provenance); doctor reporting a rate-limit snapshot and publishing quota (it reports neither); refresh as Path A only (both paths); the comments package as 9 tools (8; `set_comments_enabled` is tagged `media`, CC-PROC-9); `getAuthMode()` (it is `resolveAuthPath()`); and the Path A core count of 25 tools (24). The README env row is regenerated from `.env.example`. | post-M5 · docs |
| CC-AUTH-55 | `login` fails after it has sent the app secret, the code or a token, and the upstream message quotes one of them | **Found by audit 2026-09-23.** The catch arm wrote `login failed: <message>` verbatim, relying on Graph never echoing what it was sent — and on the persist step never quoting the long-lived token it failed to write. `runLogin` now collects the app secret, the authorization code, the short-lived and the long-lived token as the run obtains them and passes the failure message through `createRedactor({ extraSecrets })`, so each is masked by exact value, token-shaped or not. Closed by `runLogin redacts the app secret, the code and the short token out of a failure line` and `runLogin redacts the freshly minted long-lived token out of a persist failure`. Amends CC-AUTH-24: `login` still registers nothing in the process-wide registry; its redactor is local to the run. **Amended 2026-09-26:** each value is registered in its raw AND form-encoded spelling (CC-AUTH-76). | post-M5 · unit |
| CC-AUTH-56 | The MCP client passes `IG_ACCESS_TOKEN` while the config-home file still holds `IG_TOKEN_EXPIRES_AT` for a different token | **Found by audit 2026-09-23.** The env files merge under the client's environment key by key, so the client's token was paired with the file's recorded expiry and `token_status` / `doctor` reported the other token's expiry as this one's. `loadEnvFiles()` now returns which file set each key, `strayExpiryKeys(env, sourceOf)` reports every expiry record whose source differs from its own profile's token, and `main()` drops them before profiles load. Closed by `strayExpiryKeys: a record from another source than its token is reported` and `a token the client passes is never reported with the config home's recorded expiry`. **Residual:** the hand-edited-token half is closed by CC-AUTH-59 (a fingerprint binds the record to its token). Still open: a bare record set by hand carries no fingerprint and is taken as written, and a record for a token that was later revoked still reads as valid — only `doctor`'s reachability check sees that. | post-M5 · unit |
| CC-AUTH-57 | Path A's expiry is a local record, but `token_status` and `doctor` worded it as a fact about the token | **Found by audit 2026-09-23.** Path A has no introspection endpoint; the expiry comes from `IG_TOKEN_EXPIRES_AT`, which `login`/`refresh` write. `summarizeTokenExpiry` now takes the record's key and says "recorded in <KEY>" for every state, with path-specific wording when the record is missing or unrepresentable, and names the key of a named profile. Closed by `summarizeTokenExpiry: a local record is worded as a record, never as a token fact` and `token_status (Path A) names a named profile's own record key`. | post-M5 · unit |
| CC-AUTH-58 | Insights `paging.next`/`previous` come back from Graph as full request URLs carrying `access_token` and `appsecret_proof` | **Found by audit 2026-09-23.** The insights readers handed those URLs to the caller as-is, so a credential rode in structured output. `sanitizePaging` now strips both parameters from every paging URL. Closed by `getAccountInsights strips credentials from the paging URLs it hands back`. Side effect, accepted: the URL is re-serialised, so its encoding can differ from Graph's byte for byte. | post-M5 · unit |
| CC-PUB-49 | `content_publishing_limit` returns a row without `quota_usage`, or with `null` | **Found by audit 2026-09-23.** The reader read an absent usage as 0, so `get_publishing_limit` stated a full quota it had not measured. An absent or null `quota_usage`, or a missing data envelope, now raises an upstream `InstagramError` that says the quota cannot be stated; an empty `data: []` still reads as 0 used (not live-verified). Only `get_publishing_limit` reads it — no write composite depends on it. Closed by `getPublishingLimit refuses a row that carries no quota_usage instead of reading it as 0`. | post-M5 · unit |
| CC-INS-17 | Graph leaves a requested metric out of `data` because it has no row for it | **Found by audit 2026-09-23.** A partial answer looked complete. Every insights reader now compares the requested metrics with the rows returned and names the missing ones — in `notes` for account insights, in `note` for the others; a data-less body lists every requested metric. Closed by `getAccountInsights names every requested metric Graph returned no row for` and `every insights reader lists all requested metrics as missing on a data-less body`. | post-M5 · unit |
| CC-DATA-56 | `search_hashtag` receives a match without a usable id | **Found by audit 2026-09-23.** The id-less match was dropped silently, so an empty `ids` read as "no such hashtag" when Instagram had matched one. The tool now reports `omittedWithoutId` and a note, singular or plural. Closed by `search_hashtag counts a match returned without an id instead of hiding it` and `search_hashtag reports a single match returned without an id` (the latter kills the `> 0` → `> 1` mutant). | post-M5 · unit |
| CC-DATA-57 | `list_media` / `discover_business` drop id-less media on a page that also hit the item cap | **Found by audit 2026-09-23.** The drop count now joins the cap note instead of replacing it, and `discover_business` puts its clamp note first. Closed by `instagram_list_media counts one dropped id-less item beside the cap note, never replacing it` and `discover_business joins the clamp note and the omitted-media note, clamp first`. **Open:** `get_media` still drops id-less carousel children without a count. **Closed by CC-DATA-60.** | post-M5 · unit |
| CC-DATA-58 | `get_hashtag_media` stops on the item cap mid-page, or Graph returns an unusable cursor | **Found by audit 2026-09-23.** The cap set `truncated` with no reason, and an unusable cursor was withheld — which was this reader's only end-of-results signal, so a broken cursor looked like the last page. The cap now publishes `CAP_MID_PAGE_NOTE`, and an unusable cursor sets `truncated: true` with `UNUSABLE_CURSOR_NOTE`, the constant `fetchPagedEdge` shares. Closed by `get_hashtag_media publishes the cap note when the item cap cut the page` and `getHashtagMedia reports an unusable cursor as a truncated page with a note, never as end-of-results`. | post-M5 · unit |
| CC-DATA-59 | `discover_business` is asked for more media than its cap | **Found by audit 2026-09-23.** The request was clamped silently. The result now carries `mediaLimitApplied` and a clamp note, and neither when the request fits or uses the default. Closed by `discover_business reports no clamp when the request fits the cap, and none for the default`. | post-M5 · unit |
| CC-CFG-43 | A Path-A token installed through the MCPB bundle with both app fields filled | **Found by audit 2026-09-23.** `resolveAuthPath` infers `fb-login` whenever both `IG_APP_ID` and `IG_APP_SECRET` are set, and the manifest exposed no `IG_AUTH_MODE` field, so the GUI could not pin the path: a Path-A token plus app fields went to the Facebook-Login host and failed. The manifest now declares an **Auth path** field that defaults to `ig-login` and passes it as `IG_AUTH_MODE`; `docs/mcpb-install.md` documents it. Closed by `the MCPB auth-path default pins Path A even when both app fields are filled`. | post-M5 · unit |
| CC-CFG-44 | The user guides drift from the write, profile and journal behaviour | **Found by audit 2026-09-23.** Docs-only drift, fixed by review: the write-journal path now names `IG_WRITE_JOURNAL` / `XDG_STATE_HOME`; the `reader` profile includes discovery; all 17 read-only tools declare `outputSchema`; `docs/mcpb-install.md` no longer promises auto-refresh and marks the app fields Path B only; `CONTRIBUTING.md` uses `login --path ig`; the README and `docs/troubleshooting.md` now say a write is applied by `apply: true` **or** `IG_WRITE_MODE=apply` without `apply: false`, and that an elicitation-capable client is asked to confirm. **Open:** `docs/operations.md` still says "server default 25", which must change together with `test/docs-design.test.ts`. **Closed by CC-CFG-45.** | post-M5 · doc |
| CC-DATA-60 | `get_media` reads a carousel whose `children` include an item without a usable id | **Found by audit 2026-09-23.** The id-less child was filtered out silently, so an album read as holding fewer items than it does. The result now carries `omittedWithoutId` and a `note` (singular or plural), and neither when every child has an id; the tool description says so. Closed by `get_media counts every id-less carousel child instead of hiding it (CC-DATA-60)`. | post-M5 · unit |
| CC-AUTH-59 | The token in the env file is replaced by hand after `login`/`refresh` wrote its expiry record in the same file | **Found by audit 2026-09-23 (closes the hand-edit half of CC-AUTH-56).** The record was paired with whatever token sat beside it, so the new token was reported with the old one's expiry. `login`/`refresh` now write `IG_TOKEN_EXPIRES_AT` as `<seconds>:<fingerprint>`, where the fingerprint is the first 12 hex digits of the SHA-256 of the token — never the token itself — and `parseExpiresAt` reads the record only for the token it was written for; any other token reads as unknown. A bare `<seconds>` record set by hand still reads, unchecked; a malformed fingerprint reads as unknown. Closed by `a fingerprinted record reads back only for the token it was written for (CC-AUTH-59)` and `a token replaced by hand after a write does not inherit the recorded expiry (CC-AUTH-59)`. | post-M5 · unit |
| CC-CFG-45 | `docs/operations.md` §4 says list tools have a "server default 25" page size | **Found by audit 2026-09-23 (closes the open note on CC-CFG-44).** The server applies no default: no list tool gives `limit` a default, and an omitted `limit` is not forwarded, so the page size is Instagram's choice. The sentence now says that, and `test/docs-design.test.ts` derives the no-default half from the four `limit` inputs in `src/tools/` rather than pinning a literal 25. Closed by `§4 states the item cap DEFAULT_SETTINGS actually applies`. | post-M5 · doc |
| CC-INS-18 | An insights row's `values` series contains intervals that are not objects | **Found by audit 2026-09-23.** CC-INS-15 filters those intervals out so the call survives, but it did so silently: a model summing the series stated a partial total as the whole period's. The row now carries `omittedValues` with the count, and a wire field of that name is removed first so the count is always the server's own. Closed by `an insights series that lost intervals says how many, and a clean one says nothing (CC-INS-18)`. | post-M5 · unit |
| CC-COM-15 | A comment has more replies than Graph's inline `replies` edge returns in one page | **Found by audit 2026-09-23.** The nested edge is paged like any other, and its `paging` envelope was dropped whole, so a thread cut at its first page read as the complete conversation — the view a moderation decision is made on. `list_comments` and `get_comment` now mark such a comment `repliesTruncated: true` when the edge carries a non-empty `paging.next` (cursors alone are sent on the last page too). The URL is not republished: it carries the access token, and no tool follows it. Not yet observed live. Closed by `a reply thread Graph cut at its first page is marked, and a complete one is not (CC-COM-15)`. | post-M5 · unit |
| CC-DATA-61 | A tool that publishes an `outputSchema` fails with a typed `InstagramError`, and the caller is an MCP SDK client | **Found by the live-QA runner 2026-09-23** on `get_online_followers` / `get_audience_demographics` (the follower-threshold refusal, code 100). `errorResult` attached `structuredContent: { error: {...} }`, and `@modelcontextprotocol/sdk` 1.30.0 `Client.callTool` validates `structuredContent` against the tool's outputSchema whenever it is present, `isError` or not, so every typed error from all 17 read tools reached SDK clients as `MCP error -32602: Structured content does not match the tool's output schema` and the kind, message and code were lost. **Fixed:** an error result now carries no `structuredContent`; the text line is the whole contract, `Instagram error (<kind>): <message>` plus ` (code N)`, ` (subcode M)` or ` (code N, subcode M)`, so nothing the envelope held is dropped. Pinned through a real SDK `Client` over `InMemoryTransport` (`test/tools/insights.test.ts`, which failed before with exactly the -32602 above), and in `test/mcp/result.test.ts` / `test/mcp/registry.test.ts`; mutants re-adding the envelope, dropping either code, or testing `code` by truthiness are killed. A client that parsed `structuredContent.error` must read the text line instead ([stability.md](stability.md)). | post-M5 · unit |
| CC-CFG-46 | An MCPB host passes an optional `user_config` field the user left empty as its literal template, e.g. `IG_ACCOUNT_ID=${user_config.IG_ACCOUNT_ID}` | **Found in MCPB packaging review 2026-09-23.** Read as a value, the template outranks the config-home file (the client's environment always wins), so it became the account id every Graph request was sent to; as `IG_ENV_FILE` it named a nonexistent file and refused the start. **Fixed:** `unsetPlaceholderKeys` (`src/core/config.ts`) names every `IG_*` key whose whole trimmed value matches `/^\$\{user_config\.[^}]+\}$/`, and `main()` deletes them before the env files load, so the key reads as unset and a file still fills it. A value that merely contains the text is left alone. Pinned by a unit test and by a spawned-entry test (`test/index.test.ts`) that fails before with the `IG_ENV_FILE` refusal; anchor, namespace, trim, `IG_` prefix and the delete loop are each killed by a mutant. | post-M5 · unit |
| CC-COM-16 | A comment, reply or tagged item arrives without a usable `id`, as `null`, or with a scalar of the wrong type (`timestamp: null`, `like_count: '3'`) | **Found by audit 2026-09-23.** Every comment output schema requires `id` and types its scalars, and the SDK validates structured content, so ONE such entry — even a reply three levels down — failed the whole `list_comments` / `get_comment` / `list_tagged_media` call as MCP error -32602; a `null` entry in `data` threw a `TypeError` in `normalizeComment` first. The api layer now hands a non-object entry through untouched, and the tool layer leaves every entry without a non-empty string id out, counts the drops at every depth of the thread, and publishes `omittedWithoutId` plus a note (joined with `; ` to any paging note) so a thread of five does not read as a thread of two. A scalar whose type does not match its schema is dropped rather than nulled, and `get_comment`'s nested `media` is dropped when it has no id. Closed by `real McpServer: list_comments counts id-less comments and replies instead of failing the page (CC-COM-16)`, `real McpServer: get_comment counts an id-less reply and survives loose context fields (CC-COM-16)`, `real McpServer: list_tagged_media counts id-less media beside the unusable-cursor note`, `list_comments reports a single id-less comment in the singular` and `listComments hands a null entry through for the tool layer to count, not a crashed page (CC-COM-16)`. The `typedField` `!== undefined` guard was removed as an equivalent mutant (deleting an absent key is a no-op) | post-M5 · unit |
| CC-PUB-50 | A container create or `media_publish` answers 2xx without an `id` | **Found by audit 2026-09-23.** CC-PUB-29 keeps the api layer pass-through, so the absent id reached the tools: `create_media_container` reported `status: 'created'` with no `container_id`, `publish_media` and the composites reported `published` with no `media_id`, and each journaled a write with no target; a composite whose create came back id-less went on to poll `GET /undefined` and failed on an object that never existed. The new `src/tools/ack.ts` `acknowledgedId` refuses such an ack as `upstream` with advice fitted to the step: a missing container id says nothing was published and a re-run starts over (a silently made container expires, CC-PUB-3); a missing carousel album id also lists the child containers already made; a missing publish id says the post **may already be live** and names the container to check with `instagram_get_container_status` rather than inviting a second publish. Closed by `create_media_container acknowledged without an id is raised, not reported created (CC-PUB-50)`, `publish_media acknowledged without an id is raised as possibly live, not reported published (CC-PUB-50)`, `a composite whose container create has no id stops before polling a container named undefined`, `a carousel album acknowledged without an id names the child containers already made` and `a composite publish acknowledged without a media id is raised with the container to check`. A `resumableFailure` wrap around the composite publish arm was removed as dead: the refusal already names the container, so the wrap always returned it unchanged | post-M5 · unit |
| CC-COM-17 | A comment write is answered with HTTP 200 carrying a Graph error envelope, or a reply / comment create answers without an `id` | **Found by audit 2026-09-23.** `core/http.ts` maps only a non-2xx status, and an error envelope has no `success` key, so `assertAcknowledged`'s `success: false` check waved `{ error: { … } }` through as a silent success — hide, unhide and delete were reported done and journaled. `assertNoErrorEnvelope` now maps the envelope exactly as the same body on a 4xx would be mapped, so the kind, code and subcode do not depend on the status line Meta sent; `reply_to_comment` and `create_comment` refuse an id-less ack with advice to check the thread before posting again, since Graph may have posted it. Closed by `an error envelope delivered with HTTP 200 is raised, not read as a silent ack (CC-COM-17)`, `a reply or comment acknowledged without an id is raised, not reported as posted (CC-COM-17)` and the four `test/tools/ack.test.ts` cases | post-M5 · unit |
| CC-CFG-47 | A credential (e.g. a 32-hex app secret) is pasted into `IG_AUTH_MODE` / `IG_AUTH_PATH` | **Found by audit 2026-09-23.** The unknown-value refusal quoted the value as typed. It is thrown inside `loadProfiles`, before any secret is registered for redaction, so only the token-shape backstop stood between it and the `failed to start:` line on stderr (and the same refusal in `doctor` and `refresh`) — and a 32-hex app secret matches no token shape. The value is now echoed only while it is at most 24 characters (`instagram_business_login`, the longest plausible typo, is exactly 24); a longer one is reported by length alone. Closed by `an auth-path refusal never echoes a value long enough to be a mis-filed credential (CC-CFG-47)`, which pins both sides of the 24/25 boundary with a hex-only fake, and an end-to-end case in `test/index.test.ts`. `src/core/settings.ts` quoted raw values the same way (`got "${raw}"`, five sites) and ran even earlier — **closed 2026-09-23 by CC-CFG-50/CC-CFG-51** | post-M5 · unit |
| CC-CFG-48 | A credential-store write fails after the temp file is written (`chmod` or `rename` fails), or the lock cannot be released after a failed write | **Found by audit 2026-09-23.** `replaceFile` left `.env.<hex>.tmp` — a full copy of the token and app secret — behind on any failure after `writeFile`, and each write picks a fresh random name, so nothing later replaced or removed it. It is now removed best-effort before the wrapped store error is thrown. Verifying that fix exposed a second defect: `withStoreLock` released the lock in a `finally`, so when the same condition (a directory turned read-only) made the release fail too, its raw `EACCES` on `.env.lock` replaced the wrapped error that explained the failed write. The release is now best-effort on the failure path; on the success path it still throws. Closed by `a rename that fails after the temp write leaves no credential-bearing temp sibling (CC-CFG-48)` and `a temp sibling that cannot be removed never masks the write failure that stranded it (CC-CFG-48)` (skipped on Windows and as root, like the other permission tests) | post-M5 · unit |
| CC-CFG-49 | The docs promise an `IG_ACCOUNT_ID` lookup, a preview GET, or a wider host allowlist than the code has | **Found by audit 2026-09-23.** `.env.example`, `docs/mcpb-install.md`, `docs/index.html` and `manifest.json` said an unset `IG_ACCOUNT_ID` is looked up ("auto-resolved"); the code addresses `me` and makes no lookup, which is the account itself only on Path A — on Path B `me` is the Page or user behind the token, so the id must be set there. `CONTRIBUTING.md` said a preview performs a read-only GET; a preview makes no Graph call at all. `SECURITY.md`, `docs/setup-guide.md`, `docs/plugin-install.md`, `docs/architecture.md` and `docs/auth.md` were brought in line with the code: request records carry only method, host and path; the allowlist is exactly `graph.instagram.com` and `graph.facebook.com`. Docs-only; checked against `ALLOWED_HOSTS`, `accountId ?? 'me'` and the preview arm of `withWriteGate` | post-M5 · docs |
| CC-CFG-50 | A credential is pasted into a numeric, boolean, host or enum `IG_*` setting | **Found by audit 2026-09-23** (the open half of CC-CFG-47). `src/core/settings.ts` quoted the raw value at five refusal sites (`got "${raw}"`), before any secret is registered for redaction, so a token or app secret filed under the wrong name reached stderr on `failed to start:`. **Fixed:** one `describeValue(raw)` feeds all five; a value longer than `MAX_ECHOED_VALUE_LENGTH` (24, the same bound as CC-CFG-47) is reported by length alone with the hint that it may be a credential set under the wrong name. Pinned by `a settings refusal never echoes a value long enough to be a mis-filed credential (CC-CFG-50)` on both sides of the 24/25 boundary | post-M5 · unit |
| CC-CFG-51 | A setting's value carries control, format or line/paragraph-separator characters | **Found by audit 2026-09-23.** Echoed verbatim, such a value can forge a second log line or hide text on a terminal. **Fixed:** `describeValue` refuses to quote any value matching `[\p{Cc}\p{Cf}\p{Zl}\p{Zp}]` and says it contains control or invisible characters instead. Pinned by `a settings refusal never quotes control or invisible characters (CC-CFG-51)`, which carries one sample per class (including `U+2029`, added after a mutant dropping `\p{Zp}` survived) | post-M5 · unit |
| CC-CFG-52 | Numeric settings spelled with a sign, padding, leading zeros or as an empty string | **Pinned by audit 2026-09-23**, no behaviour change: `+5`, ` 5 `, `05` and `0` read as decimal and an empty value is unset. Recorded so a stricter parser has to change a named test rather than drift silently: `numeric spellings: sign, padding and zero are read as decimal; empty is unset (CC-CFG-52)`. `-0` still reads as `0` in the message — tracked as open | post-M5 · unit |
| CC-CFG-53 | A credential value the env-file format cannot carry verbatim, or a merge that changes a key it never meant to touch | **Found by audit 2026-09-23.** `formatValue` chose one quote form per line, but dotenv's quoted forms skip a backslash-escaped quote, so `trailing\` in single quotes ran on into a later line holding a `'` and swallowed that key; a value with all three quote kinds plus `#`, or a newline beside a literal `\n`, has no spelling dotenv reads back at all; and a hand-quoted value spanning lines leaves its tail behind as a new key once its first line is replaced. Each was written without a word — a different secret stored, or another profile's key lost. **Fixed:** `formatValue` picks bare, `'…'`, `` `…` `` or `"…"` (escaping CR/LF) in that order, and `assertReadsBack` re-parses the merged text with dotenv before anything is written: every key set must read back as its value, every removed key must be gone, and every other key — including one only the merged side has — must read as before. A mismatch is a `validation` error naming the keys, never a value, and nothing is written. Pinned by `every tricky value round-trips through dotenv + loadProfiles, other keys untouched (CC-CFG-53)`, `a value no env-file form can carry is refused, naming the key only, nothing written (CC-CFG-53)`, `a quoted value that would swallow a later line is refused, not written corrupt (CC-CFG-53)` and `a key the merge brings to light is refused, even one this write never names (CC-CFG-53)`. Mutation-verified; the one survivor, dropping `values.keys()` from the checked key set, is equivalent and documented at the site. **Open:** the backtick and double-quoted forms are expanded by a POSIX shell that `source`s the store | post-M5 · unit |
| CC-CFG-54 | Power loss right after a credential-store rename; an entry already sitting at the temp name | **Found by audit 2026-09-23; reverses the durability half of CC-CFG-12.** Without an `fsync` the renamed store could come back zero-length after a crash, losing every profile's credentials, not only the one being written. `login` and `refresh` are rare, so the disk barrier the original decision priced in costs nothing that matters. **Fixed:** `replaceFile` opens the temp sibling with `wx` at 0600 (an entry already at that random name — a planted symlink — is refused rather than followed), writes, `sync()`s and closes it before the rename, then flushes the directory best-effort on POSIX (`syncDir` swallows `EINVAL` from mounts that refuse it: the store has already been replaced). Pinned by `the temp file is fsynced before the rename, and the directory after it (CC-CFG-54)`, `a failed file fsync aborts the write, wrapped, with the store and directory untouched (CC-CFG-54)`, `a directory fsync the filesystem refuses does not fail a completed write (CC-CFG-54)` and `an entry already at the temp name is refused, not followed (CC-CFG-54)`, which pins `crypto.randomBytes` to predict the name | post-M5 · unit |
| CC-CFG-55 | A stale store lock that cannot be removed | **Found by audit 2026-09-23.** A directory at the lock's name is refused by `wx` (`EEXIST`) and judged stale by age, but `rm` without `recursive` cannot clear it, and the raw `ERR_FS_EISDIR` escaped `writeCredentials` unwrapped. **Fixed:** that `rm` is wrapped with `storeFailure(err, 'write', filePath)` and fails at once instead of waiting out the lock timeout. Pinned by `a stale lock that cannot be cleared fails at once, wrapped, not as a raw fs error (CC-CFG-55)`. **Open:** a writer that legitimately holds the lock longer than `LOCK_STALE_MS` (30 s) has it judged stale and stolen | post-M5 · unit |
| CC-CFG-56 | The store lock cannot be released after a successful write | **Found by audit 2026-09-23.** The release on the success path was a bare `rm`, so a failure there reported a completed save as a failure and the operator retried a `login` whose token was already on disk. **Fixed:** released best-effort, like the failure path; a lock left behind costs at most one wait, since the next writer clears it once stale (or fails naming it per CC-CFG-55). Pinned by `a lock that cannot be released after a successful write does not fail the write (CC-CFG-56)` | post-M5 · unit |
| CC-PUB-51 | A carousel child container is acknowledged without a usable id | **Found by audit 2026-09-23.** `createCarouselContainer` pushed whatever `child.id` held into `children`, so a missing, empty or non-string id went on to the album call and failed there with Meta's generic error, after the other children were already created. **Fixed:** each child id must be a non-empty string; otherwise an `upstream` `InstagramError` is thrown and `carouselAbortError` reports it with the ids of the siblings already created. Pinned by `a carousel child acknowledged without an id aborts the carousel before the album (CC-PUB-51)` and `a carousel child answering with an empty or non-string id is refused the same way (CC-PUB-51)` | post-M5 · unit |
| CC-PUB-52 | A container in `ERROR` status carries a long or hostile status detail | **Found by audit 2026-09-23.** The `ERROR` arm interpolated `status` unbounded and unquoted into the error message, while an unknown status code was already quoted and capped. **Fixed:** both go through one `quoteBounded(text, MAX_ECHOED_STATUS)` (200 characters). Pinned by `the ERROR detail is quoted and bounded like an unknown status code (CC-PUB-52)` | post-M5 · unit |
| CC-PUB-53 | A publishing call is answered HTTP 200 with a Graph error envelope | **Found by audit 2026-09-23.** `createMediaContainer`, `getContainerStatus`, `publishMedia` and `getPublishingLimit` read a 200 body as success, so `{ error: {...} }` became "no id returned" or, for status, "no status yet" — polled until the deadline. **Fixed:** a shared `graphBody(body)` throws `mapGraphError(200, body)` when `error` is an object, so Meta's own kind, message and code reach the caller. Only an object counts: a null or scalar `error` beside an id stays an acknowledgement. Pinned by `a write answered 200 with an error envelope reports Meta’s error, not a missing id (CC-PUB-53)`, `createMediaContainer and publishMedia map a 200 error envelope rather than dropping it (CC-PUB-53)`, `getContainerStatus maps a 200 error envelope instead of reading it as "no status yet" (CC-PUB-53)` and `only an object \`error\` is an envelope: a null or scalar \`error\` beside an id is an ack (CC-PUB-53)`. docs/tools.md gains a "Write acknowledgements" paragraph | post-M5 · unit |
| CC-PUB-54 | A publishing call is answered with a JSON `null` or a scalar | **Found by audit 2026-09-23.** Property reads on the body threw a raw `TypeError`. **Fixed:** `graphBody` maps a non-object body to `{}`, so the existing "no id" / "no status" handling answers with a typed error. Pinned by `a JSON null or scalar body is read as an empty acknowledgement, never a TypeError (CC-PUB-54)` | post-M5 · unit |
| CC-DATA-62 | `search_hashtag` receives a `null` or non-object entry in `data` | **Found by audit 2026-09-23.** `.map((r) => r.id)` threw on a `null` match and the whole search failed. **Fixed:** entries are filtered with `isRecordObject` before their ids are read, and the dropped ones count toward the existing omission note. Pinned by `search_hashtag counts a null match instead of crashing on it (CC-DATA-62)` | post-M5 · unit |
| CC-DATA-63 | `search_hashtag` receives a non-array `data` | **Found by audit 2026-09-23.** A non-array `data` crashed the tool. **Fixed:** it is read as no matches. Pinned by `search_hashtag answers no ids when Meta sends a non-list \`data\` (CC-DATA-63)` | post-M5 · unit |
| CC-DATA-64 | `list_linked_accounts` receives a `null` or non-object Page entry | **Found by audit 2026-09-23.** The row mapper in `listLinkedAccounts` dereferenced the entry and the listing failed. **Fixed:** the mapper passes such a row through untouched, and the tool drops it and reports it through `omittedWithoutId` and `note`, joined to the walk note with `; `. Pinned by `listLinkedAccounts hands a null entry through for the tool layer to count, not a crashed page (CC-DATA-64)`, `list_linked_accounts joins the omission note to the walk note (CC-DATA-64)` and `real McpServer: list_linked_accounts counts a null Page entry instead of failing (CC-DATA-64)` | post-M5 · unit |
| CC-DATA-65 | `get_account` receives a profile without a usable `id` | **Found by audit 2026-09-23.** The outputSchema requires `id`, so an SDK client rejected the whole result with -32602. **Fixed:** `profileId(wireId, requested)` publishes the configured account id when Meta omits it; through `me` with no configured id it throws an `upstream` error that says to retry or configure the id. Pinned by `get_account through \`me\` fails with a clear upstream error when Meta omits the id (CC-DATA-65)` and `real McpServer: get_account publishes the configured id when Meta omits it (CC-DATA-65)` | post-M5 · unit |
| CC-DATA-66 | `get_media` receives inline `children` whose `data` is not an array, or a non-object body | **Found by audit 2026-09-23.** A non-list `children.data` was published as the children and broke the output schema; a non-object body crashed `normalizeDetail`. **Fixed:** inline children are kept only when `children.data` is an array, otherwise the tool falls back to `/children`; a non-object body is an `upstream` error. Pinned by `getMedia keeps inline children only when \`children.data\` is an array (CC-DATA-66)` and `instagram_get_media falls back to /children when the inline edge is not a list (CC-DATA-66)` | post-M5 · unit |
| CC-DATA-67 | `getMediaChildren` receives a non-array `data` or a `null` body | **Found by audit 2026-09-23.** It returned the value as-is or threw. **Fixed:** it returns `data` only when it is an array, otherwise `[]`. Pinned by `getMediaChildren accepts only an array \`data\` and survives a null body (CC-DATA-67)` | post-M5 · unit |
| CC-DATA-68 | `get_media` has neither inline children nor fallback children | **Found by audit 2026-09-23.** An empty `children: []` was published for a single image. **Fixed:** `children` is published only when it is a non-empty array. Pinned by `instagram_get_media omits \`children\` when both the inline edge and the fallback are empty (CC-DATA-68)` | post-M5 · unit |
| CC-INS-19 | An insights interval carries no readable measurement | **Found by audit 2026-09-23.** `metricRow` kept any object in `values[]`, so an interval whose `value` was missing, non-finite or a string was published as data. **Fixed:** `isReadableInterval` keeps an interval only when its `value` is a finite number or an object (a breakdown map); the rest are counted and noted. Pinned by `an interval with no readable measurement is counted, not published as data (CC-INS-19)`. **Closed** by CC-INS-20 (`total_value`) and CC-INS-21 (its breakdowns) | post-M5 · unit |
| CC-DATA-69 | A paged listing is answered with a page that holds no readable list | **Found by audit 2026-09-23.** `fetchPagedEdge` read `page.data` unchecked: a `data` that was not a list crashed on `.length`, and a JSON `null` body threw a raw `TypeError`. **Fixed:** `readPageData` reads a page only when it is an object whose `data` is absent (an empty edge) or a list; anything else ends the walk `truncated: true` with `UNREADABLE_PAGE_NOTE`. Pinned by `fetchPagedEdge reports an unreadable page as an incomplete read, not a crash or an empty listing (CC-DATA-69)` | post-M5 · unit |
| CC-DATA-70 | An unreadable page arrives after readable ones | **Found by audit 2026-09-23.** Resuming from the cursor the unreadable page advertised would skip whatever it held without a trace. **Fixed:** the items already read are kept, and `after` is the cursor that REQUESTED the unreadable page, so a retry re-reads exactly that page. Pinned by `fetchPagedEdge keeps the pages it read and resumes AT an unreadable page, never past it (CC-DATA-70)` | post-M5 · unit |

Pinned by `listComments and listTaggedMedia report an unreadable page instead of crashing (CC-DATA-71)` | post-M5 · unit |
| CC-DATA-72 | `/me/accounts` is answered with an unreadable page | **Found by audit 2026-09-23.** `listLinkedAccounts` crashed the same way. **Fixed** by CC-DATA-69. Pinned by `listLinkedAccounts reports an unreadable /me/accounts page instead of crashing (CC-DATA-72)` | post-M5 · unit |
| CC-DATA-73 | `list_media` receives an unreadable page | **Found by audit 2026-09-23.** The call failed instead of returning what it had read. **Fixed:** the result is `truncated: true` with the note, through the live output validator. Pinned by `real McpServer: an unreadable list_media page is a noted, truncated result, not a failed call (CC-DATA-73)` | post-M5 · unit |

Pinned by `list_media leaves out and counts null and scalar rows, like every other id-less row (CC-DATA-74)` | post-M5 · unit |

Pinned by `list_comments and list_tagged_media surface an unreadable page as a note, not an empty success (CC-DATA-75)` | post-M5 · unit |

Pinned by `getContainerStatus reports the requested id when Graph sends no usable one (CC-DATA-76)` | post-M5 · unit |

| CC-PROC-203 | A Graph response body is unboundedly large | **Found by audit 2026-09-23.** `readBody` called `res.text()`, so a proxy or upstream streaming without end was buffered whole, once per concurrent request. **Fixed:** the body is read as a counted stream and refused past 16 MiB (an unencoded `Content-Length` over it before any byte) with a non-retried `upstream` error; the stream is cancelled. **Open note closed 2026-09-24:** `core/refresh.ts` and `cli/login.ts` had still called `res.text()` uncapped; the reader moved to `core/body.ts` (its own module, because `core/refresh.ts` must not import `core/http`) and all three readers share it, each refusing with its own error — both token exchanges answer `upstream` with the status, never `auth`, quoting no byte of the body. | CC-CFG-58 | A live but slow writer's store lock is cleared by age | **Found by audit 2026-09-23.** A lock older than 30 s was cleared even while its writer (a hung `fsync`, a paused debugger) was alive, and the last rename discarded the other profile's token. **Fixed:** the lock records `pid@host`; on this host the owner's liveness (`kill(pid, 0)`, only `ESRCH` is gone; this process's own pid counts only while it still holds the lock) decides, and age remains the fallback for an empty, malformed, group-addressing or foreign-host record. Residuals (pid reuse, shared hostname + home) accepted in the source. Pinned by `a fresh lock whose owner has exited on this host is cleared at once (CC-CFG-58)` | post-M5 · unit |

| CC-CFG-62 | The lock record `pid@host` could not tell one holding of a lock from the next. A writer whose lock had been judged stale and re-taken removed the new owner's lock on release, and two writers judging the same old lock stale could each `rm` it — the second deleting the first one's fresh lock — so two writers ran at once and one profile's token was lost. | The record is `pid@host@<128-bit hex nonce>` (bare `pid@host` still parses); a process counts its own pid alive only for the exact record it holds, and both the release and the stale clear re-read the record and remove the lock only if it is unchanged. Residual, accepted: no compare-and-unlink exists, so a one-syscall window remains; a pre-nonce build judges nonced records by age alone. | post-M5 · unit |
| CC-DATA-91 | Graph error text (`error.message` / `error_user_msg`) reaches the `InstagramError` message unbounded (up to the 16 MiB body cap) and with newline, U+2028/2029, ANSI and bidi characters intact, so it can forge lines in the tool result, logs or terminal | `mapGraphError` passes the text through `quoteUntrusted` (moved from doctor to `core/untrusted.ts`): every Cc/Cf/Zl/Zp character is rendered as `\u{hex}`, and text over 1000 code points is cut and ends `… (N characters in all)` | post-M5 · unit |
| CC-DATA-92 | Capping upstream text before the downstream exact-value redactor runs can leak a prefix of a secret that has no token shape (e.g. a 32-hex app secret), because the redactor cannot match half of it | The cut is word-safe: it backs off to whitespace, so a secret is either kept whole (and masked downstream) or dropped whole. Token shapes are still stripped over the whole text before the cut | post-M5 · unit |
| CC-DATA-93 | The `login` token-exchange refusal printed Meta's `error.message` raw on the `login failed:` line: a newline forged a second line, and its length was unbounded | `exchangeError` applies the same 1000-code-point word-safe cut and escaping, with no pre-stripping; the `runLogin` catch still redacts with every secret the run holds | post-M5 · unit |
| CC-PROC-204 | SIGTERM/SIGINT to a server on the HTTP transport skipped its graceful teardown: `src/index.ts` discarded the `RunningHttpTransport` handle and nothing in `src` handled a signal, so the process died at the signal and `close()` (CC-PROC-33) was reachable only from tests | `closeOnSignal` (`src/mcp/transport.ts`) is wired in `src/index.ts`. The first signal calls `close()` once, bounded by `SHUTDOWN_TIMEOUT_MS` (10 s), and exits 0 on a clean close or 1 on rejection or timeout. A second signal while closing exits at once with 128 + signo (143/130). Every line goes to the stderr logger. stdio deliberately stays on Node's default, because stdin EOF is its clean close. The test preload's SIGTERM fallback stands aside when the entry has its own handler. Pinned by unit tests with injected `SignalHooks` and by a subprocess test that sends SIGTERM to the built entry and asserts exit 0, empty stdout and both shutdown log lines | post-M5 · unit |
| CC-AUTH-61 | `login` receives an `expires_in` that is negative, non-finite, or whose sum with the clock is not a recordable expiry (at or before the epoch, or past 9999-12-31T23:59:59Z) | `computeExpiresAtSec` (`src/cli/login.ts`) delegates to `expiryFromLifetime` (`src/core/time.ts`), the rule `refresh` shares (CC-AUTH-64). Exactly `0` is "never expires". A negative lifetime is stored as the past instant it names, previously stored as "never". Any sum at or before the epoch, including one from a pre-1970 host clock, and any sum past 9999-12-31T23:59:59Z stores no expiry at all (CC-AUTH-65, CC-AUTH-66). For these, `login` prints `Token expiry: unknown (upstream returned an expires_in of X, which resolves to no recordable timestamp; no expiry was stored).` rather than the misleading "no lifetime returned". A wire `NaN` or ±`Infinity` never reaches that line: `numOrUndef` drops it first, so it prints "no lifetime returned". Pinned by unit tests on each bound and on the printed line | post-M5 · unit |
| CC-AUTH-62 | The ig-login code exchange answers with the token wrapped as `{data:[{access_token, user_id, …}]}` instead of the flat body | `unwrapIgCodeExchange` (`src/cli/login.ts`) unwraps a `data` array of exactly one entry. A flat body, including one carrying `data: []`, is read flat. Two or more entries, or a top-level `access_token` beside a `data` entry, is refused as `upstream`: `Instagram code exchange response is ambiguous: … expected exactly one. Nothing was stored; run login again.` A degenerate entry stays the existing "missing access_token" auth error. fb-login's exchange is unchanged | post-M5 · unit |
| CC-CFG-63 | `login` / `refresh` run with `IG_ENV_FILE` set: the server reads only that file (it REPLACES the candidate list), but the writer always wrote the config-home `.env`, so a fresh token sat where the server never looked | `resolveStoreTarget` (`src/core/config-write.ts`) makes a set, trimmed, non-blank `IG_ENV_FILE` the write target when no explicit `configDir` is given. The file is written at 0600 and merged in place, with its lock and tmp siblings beside it. A `~` value is the home directory; a relative value is refused as `validation` at the one reading both sides share, so the server refuses it at start too (CC-CFG-70). The parent directory is neither created nor chmodded, so a missing parent is a wrapped error. A blank value falls back to the config home, and an explicit `configDir` wins. The entry drops an `IG_ENV_FILE` that an env file itself supplied, because the server never followed it and the writer must not either. Pinned by unit tests and by entry tests for `refresh` both ways | post-M5 · unit |
| CC-CFG-64 | An MCP client's JSON `env` hands `XDG_STATE_HOME` / `IG_WRITE_JOURNAL` over unexpanded (`~/state`, `~/audit/writes.jsonl`). The relative-state-home rule (CC-CFG-24) silently dropped `~/state` for `~/.local/state`, and the verbatim journal path made `mcp/write-mode.ts` create `<cwd>/~/audit/`, so the audit trail of every applied write landed somewhere other than where the operator pointed it, with no diagnostic | `resolveWriteJournal` (`src/core/settings.ts`) expands a leading `~` (`~` or `~/…`, plus `~\…` on win32) to `os.homedir()` in both variables before the absolute check, via `src/core/home-path.ts`, the rule `config-write.ts` now imports for CC-CFG-60 as well. A `~` anywhere but the start is an ordinary character. A relative `IG_WRITE_JOURNAL` is still used as given; a relative `XDG_STATE_HOME` is still ignored | post-M5 · unit |
| CC-CFG-65 | `~user/…`, `~+`, `$HOME/…`, `${HOME}/…` and `%VAR%…` in `XDG_STATE_HOME` or `IG_WRITE_JOURNAL` name a directory only to a shell: the state home was silently ignored for the default, and the journal path became a literal `$HOME` folder in the cwd | Refused at `loadSettings`, so the entry fails at startup (and `doctor` with it), with `kind: validation`: `<variable> is "<value>", which only a shell can expand — it is not a path this server can use for the write journal; set it to an absolute path`. The echoed value has control, format and line-separator characters escaped. An explicit `IG_WRITE_JOURNAL` is checked instead of the state home, so a bad `XDG_STATE_HOME` beside it is not reached. `$` / `%` after the first character are ordinary | post-M5 · unit |
| CC-AUTH-63 | The reachability GET for the configured account id answers with a different id (Path A or B), while every tool keeps addressing the configured one | `doctor` prints an `Account identity MISMATCH` WARN naming the configured id and the answered id, both quoted and capped at 64 characters. | CC-PROC-205 | The Claude Code marketplace entry pointed at the repo root (`"source": "./"`), so the plugin root held the repo's `package.json`. On install Claude Code copies the plugin root into `~/.claude/plugins/cache/` and runs `npm ci --ignore-scripts` there when it holds a `package.json`: measured with Claude Code 2.1.280 from a local clone, the cached plugin was 224 MB (88 MB `node_modules` with TypeScript, ESLint and c8, plus the working tree) for a plugin that only runs `npx -y instagram-mcp-ai@<version>` | **Found in plugin install test 2026-09-24. Fixed:** the plugin moved to `plugins/instagram-mcp-ai/`, which holds only `.claude-plugin/plugin.json`, and the entry reads `"source": "./plugins/instagram-mcp-ai"`. The catalog stays at the repo root `.claude-plugin/marketplace.json`, so `/plugin marketplace add IvanBBaev/instagram-mcp` is unchanged. After the move the cached plugin holds only the manifest and no install runs. Pinned by `the plugin root carries no package.json, so installing the plugin runs no npm install (CC-PROC-205)` and by the exact-source assertion in `test/release/plugin-manifest.test.ts` | post-M5 · unit |
| CC-CFG-66 | `IG_ENV_FILE` is `~/ig.env` (an MCP client's JSON `env` passes it unexpanded) | **Found by audit 2026-09-24.** dotenv expands a leading `~` on its own, so the entry READ `<home>/ig.env`, but `login` / `refresh` refused the same value as relative: the one store variable that replaces the candidate list could not be refreshed in the spelling `XDG_CONFIG_HOME` accepts (CC-CFG-60). Fixed: `namedEnvFile` in `core/config-write.ts` is the single reading of `IG_ENV_FILE`, used by `loadEnvFiles` and `resolveStoreTarget`; it expands the home directory's own `~` (`core/home-path.ts`), so read and write land on the same file. The relative-path refusal now says a leading `~` is the home directory. | post-M5 · unit (`test/core/config-write.test.ts`, `test/index.test.ts`) |
| CC-CFG-67 | `IG_ENV_FILE` is `~user/…`, `~+/…`, `$HOME/…`, `${HOME}/…` or `%APPDATA%\…` | **Found by audit 2026-09-24.** The entry passed these to dotenv: `$…`/`%…` were reported as "could not be read as an env file", and `~root/ig.env` was silently read as `<home>/root/ig.env` (dotenv's `~` expansion ignores what follows it); the writer refused them as relative. Fixed: `namedEnvFile` runs `assertNotShellSpelling`, so the start and any write stop with `IG_ENV_FILE is "<value>", which only a shell can expand — it is not a path this server can use to find the credential store; set it to an absolute path`, before any request. A `$` or `%` past the start is deliberately NOT refused: it sits inside an absolute name both sides open byte for byte, and real names use it (`\\host\C$\…`, `C:\$Recycle.Bin`, `100%`); pinned in `test/core/home-path.test.ts`. | post-M5 · unit (`test/core/config-write.test.ts`, `test/index.test.ts`, `test/core/home-path.test.ts`) |
| CC-CFG-68 | The store (config-home `.env` or `IG_ENV_FILE`) is a symlink, e.g. into a dotfiles repository | **Found by audit 2026-09-24.** Reverses the "deliberately unfixed" half of CC-CFG-12: the rename replaced the LINK with a regular file, so the linked file stopped receiving tokens after the first `login` while every read (dotenv, `readExisting`) followed the link. Fixed: `followStoreLink` resolves a final-name symlink before the write; the temp sibling, the `.lock` and the rename sit beside the target (still a fresh `0600` inode renamed into place, never an in-place write), two links to one file share one lock, the success line names the target, and only the directory holding the name (the config home) is prepared — the target's directory is neither created nor re-permissioned. Refused before anything is touched: a dangling link (`…: it is a symbolic link whose target does not exist; …`) and, where a uid exists, a link owned by another user (`…: it is a symbolic link owned by another user, so it is not followed; …`), since a planted link would otherwise choose which of the operator's files is rewritten. A self-loop still reports `ELOOP`. The two former residuals are closed: the link is resolved again under the lock (CC-CFG-72), and every link in a chain is owner-checked (CC-CFG-73). | post-M5 · unit (`test/core/config-write.test.ts`, POSIX-only) |
| CC-AUTH-64 | `login` or `refresh` receives `expires_in: 0` from a successful exchange | **Found by audit 2026-09-24.** The two writers disagreed on the same answer. `login` recorded `0` ("never expires"), while `refresh` recorded `floor(now)`, so `token_status` and `doctor` reported the token as expired. Both now go through one helper, `expiryFromLifetime` in `src/core/time.ts`, which treats exactly `0` (or `-0`) as "never", following the `debug_token` convention. A successful exchange does not mint a dead token, and a later code 190 remains the authority (CC-AUTH-1). Closed by `an expires_in of 0 records "never expires", the same as login`, `expiryFromLifetime: a lifetime of exactly 0 is "never expires", whatever the clock` and `refresh renders the never-expires sentinel as "never", not as the 1970 epoch` (now reached through `expires_in: 0`). | post-M5 · unit |
| CC-AUTH-65 | An `expires_in` whose sum with the clock lands at or before the epoch (a large negative lifetime, or a pre-1970 host clock), or an `expiresAtSec` handed to `writeCredentials` that is negative, fractional or out of range | **Found by audit 2026-09-24.** The writers produced records that their own reader refuses. `login` stored the signed record `-1994816000:<fp>` for a sixty-day token on a 1906 clock, and `parseExpiresAt` drops any signed value, so the record read back as unknown. | CC-PROC-206 | The stdio client closes stdin (EOF) while an applied write waits at the confirmation prompt. | **Found by audit 2026-09-24.** The SDK's `StdioServerTransport` listens for `'data'` and `'error'` on stdin only, never `'end'`, so EOF changed nothing inside the protocol. The `elicitation/create` request stayed pending on its `CONFIRM_TIMEOUT_MS` timer, and the process held on for 120 s waiting for an answer no one could send. Measured on the built entry: still alive 8 s after EOF, then killed. `startStdio` now closes the transport on stdin `'end'`, sharing one latch with the stdout-`EPIPE` close (CC-PROC-198), so a client that shuts both ends closes it once; a stdin that closes without `'end'` (a reset pipe, a destroyed stream) takes the same path and logs the departure once. The close rejects every pending server→client request with `ConnectionClosed`. The write gate reads that as a refusal: the write is not performed, nothing is journaled, and the entry exits 0 within milliseconds. A call already past its gate still finishes and journals. Closed by `stdin EOF closes the transport, so a pending confirmation prompt fails at once` (unit, including the single-close latch), `a stdin closed without EOF closes the transport too, and logs once`, `the close that follows a clean EOF does not log the departure a second time` and `stdin EOF at a pending write confirmation exits at once and performs nothing (CC-PROC-206)` (built entry: exit 0, no Graph request, no journal file). | post-M5 · unit |
| CC-PROC-207 | The stdio client leaves (stdin EOF or closed stdout) while a composite publish is in its container-status poll. | **Found by audit 2026-09-24. Decision, no code change.** Tool handlers never receive the SDK's abort signal, so closing the transport does not stop the poll. Measured on the built entry: the process stayed up for about 60 s after EOF (the internal `DEFAULT_MAX_POLL_MS` budget), made 20 more status GETs, then journaled `status:"in_progress"` with the container id and exited 0. Its result was never delivered, because the client had gone. This is kept deliberately. The write was authorized before the client left, and cutting the flow off mid-poll would leave a created container with no journal line — the resume id would be lost. The linger is bounded by the poll budget plus one request timeout, and the journal line is what the operator resumes from. Documented in architecture.md §8. | post-M5 · decision |
| CC-AUTH-67 | `needsRefresh` in `core/refresh.ts` is exported and tested but has no production caller. | **Found by audit 2026-09-25.** It computed a refresh decision nothing makes: refresh is operator-driven (auth.md §3) and `IG_REFRESH_AFTER_DAYS` is applied only by `summarizeTokenExpiry` as the `expiring_soon` threshold. | CC-AUTH-68 | `debug_token` (Path B) returns an `expires_at` outside the recorded range: in milliseconds, negative, a fraction, or past 9999-12-31T23:59:59Z. | **Found by audit 2026-09-25.** Path A's reader refused such values, but Path B was bounded only by the `Date` range, so `1790000000000` reported a valid token until year 58692 and `-86400` an expired one dated 1969. `summarizeTokenExpiry` now holds both paths to `isRecordableExpiry` (`0`, or whole seconds `1`..`253402300799`); anything else is `unknown` with a warning naming the value. The profile now carries `tokenExpiryUnverified`, and `token_status`/`doctor` add an "unverified" `warning` to every dated state (`valid` and `never` included) telling the operator to run `refresh`/`login`. No output-schema change: the existing `warning` field carries it. | CC-DATA-102 | A wire string carrying DEL, C1 controls, bidi overrides, zero-width or tag characters, or U+2028/U+2029 reaches a tool result's text block | **Found by audit 2026-09-25.** Every success result's text block is `JSON.stringify` of its payload, and `JSON.stringify` escapes only C0 controls, `"` and `\`. A caption, username, id, cursor or insights label therefore reached the model with bidi overrides, invisible characters and raw line separators intact. After U+2028, U+2029 or NEL, a fake `Instagram error (auth): …` line started a line of its own. Measured on all 28 tools with a hostile fixture: 23 of them echo a wire or argument string, and every one of those shipped the raw characters. The registry's per-call wrapper now renders every text block, error results included, through `escapeTextBlocks` → `escapeInvisible` in `src/core/untrusted.ts`. It writes each control (except the line feed), format and separator code point as a lowercase JSON `\uXXXX` escape, one per UTF-16 unit. The text still parses to `structuredContent`. The rendering runs after secret masking, so the `\b`-anchored hex backstop still sees the raw separator in front of a token. Rule documented in security.md §7. Closed by the table-driven guard `test/tools/untrusted-output.test.ts` (`no text block carries a raw control, format or separator character`, `no line of a text block is a forged framing line`, `the text block parses back to exactly the structuredContent it renders`, each tagged CC-DATA-102, across 4 modes: preview/applied × compact/pretty), four `escapeInvisible` unit tests (untrusted) and four `escapeTextBlocks` tests (registry), including the redactor order and a non-string block passed through. | post-M5 · unit |
| CC-DATA-103 | Hostile wire text in `structuredContent` | **Decision 2026-09-25, pinned by test.** `structuredContent` is data and carries a wire string as received, inside the untrusted fence where the field is free text. It is not escaped, stripped or cut, because legitimate text needs these characters: ZWJ emoji sequences, RLM in a right-to-left caption, a soft hyphen. Ids and cursors must also round-trip byte for byte. The escaping belongs to the text rendering (CC-DATA-102). A client that feeds `structuredContent` to a model owns its rendering. Closed by `structuredContent carries hostile wire text as received, never escaped or cut` (guard, which also pins the five tools that echo no wire text: `get_publishing_limit`, `hide_comment`, `unhide_comment`, `delete_comment`, `set_comments_enabled`) and `escapeTextBlocks: the text block escapes invisible characters while structuredContent keeps them` (registry). | post-M5 · decision |
| CC-CFG-69 | `HOME` (`%USERPROFILE%`) is blank, whitespace or relative — e.g. `HOME="   "` — and a path is derived from it | **Found by audit 2026-09-25**, from a `   /instagram-mcp-ai/.env` directory (blank name) in the repo root, dated 2026-08-09; the file was never read. `os.homedir()` returns the variable verbatim, so every home-derived path was relative and followed the cwd: `configDir: '~'` wrote `<cwd>/   /instagram-mcp-ai/.env` (the artefact's exact shape), the default `~/.config` / `%USERPROFILE%\AppData\Roaming` became relative, and `IG_ENV_FILE=~/x.env` named `<cwd>/   /x.env`. A blank `configDir`, `XDG_CONFIG_HOME` or `APPDATA` has always been trimmed to "unset" (CC-CFG-8); the likely origin is a `clean()` mutant run against `a blank configDir override falls back to the platform config home` from the repo root, which wrote the store before its assertion failed. **Fixed:** `homeDirectory()` in `src/core/home-path.ts` refuses a non-absolute home as `validation`, naming `HOME` / `USERPROFILE`; `expandHomeTilde` and both `resolveConfigHome` fallbacks use it, so no current path writes a store under a blank or relative directory. That blank-`configDir` test now runs in a temporary cwd, and a second canary (`no test wrote a credentials store one directory down, blank-named directories included`) looks for `<repo>/<any entry>/instagram-mcp-ai/.env` with `existsSync` only. The write-journal fallback in `src/core/settings.ts` joined the raw `homedir()` until CC-CFG-75 routed it through `homeDirectory`. | post-M5 · unit (`test/core/home-path.test.ts`, `test/core/config-write.test.ts`, `test/harness.test.ts`) |
| CC-CFG-70 | `IG_ENV_FILE` is relative, e.g. `relative.env` | **Found by audit 2026-09-25.** The entry passed a relative value to dotenv, which read it against the cwd the MCP client chose, while `login` / `refresh` refused it: a server could run for weeks on a file no refresh would ever update. **Fixed** by refusing it at the one reading both sides share, `namedEnvFile` in `src/core/config-write.ts`, so the server now refuses to start (`instagram-mcp-ai failed to start: IG_ENV_FILE is set to "relative.env", which is not an absolute file name. …`). Refusal was chosen over resolving against the cwd because `.env.example` has always said the value must be absolute, and an MCP server's cwd cannot be known from the shell that runs `login` (CC-CFG-24). **Behaviour change** for anyone whose server started with a relative value. | post-M5 · unit + spawned entry (`test/core/config-write.test.ts`, `test/index.test.ts`) |
| CC-CFG-71 | An env file contains an unexpanded MCPB template as a value, e.g. `IG_ACCOUNT_ID=${user_config.IG_ACCOUNT_ID}` | **Found by audit 2026-09-25.** The CC-CFG-46 scrub ran over the client's environment only, before the files load (dotenv expands nothing). The template reached Graph as the account id, and because the key was now set, the project `.env` could not supply it. **Fixed:** `loadEnvFiles` in `src/index.ts` runs `unsetPlaceholderKeys` over the keys each file adds and deletes them again, so a later candidate still fills them. | post-M5 · spawned entry (`test/index.test.ts`) |
| CC-CFG-72 | A symlinked store is re-pointed between the write's resolution of the link and its lock | **Found by audit 2026-09-25**, closing the CC-CFG-68 residual. `followStoreLink` ran before `withStoreLock`, so the write went to the file the link used to name, under that file's lock, while every reader now opened another. **Fixed:** `writeCredentials` resolves the name again inside the lock and refuses a mismatch (`…: it named <old> when this write began and names <new> now that the write holds the lock, so nothing was written; run the command again`). The write still goes to the resolved path, so re-pointing the link after the check cannot redirect it. | post-M5 · unit (`test/core/config-write.test.ts`, POSIX-only) |
| CC-CFG-73 | The store is a chain of symlinks, and a link after the first is owned by another user | **Found by audit 2026-09-25**, closing the CC-CFG-68 residual. Only the name's own link was owner-checked, and `realpath` then followed every later hop without checking it. **Fixed:** `resolveLinkChain` walks the chain one hop at a time, owner-checks every link (`…: the symbolic link <hop> on the way to it is owned by another user, so it is not followed; …`), refuses a dangling hop, and reports `ELOOP` after 40 hops. Chains are walked, not refused, because dotfiles managers (GNU Stow, relinked checkouts) create chains of the operator's own links. A symlinked *directory* component was followed by the kernel without an owner check until CC-CFG-74 closed it. | post-M5 · unit (`test/core/config-write.test.ts`, POSIX-only) |
| CC-CFG-74 | A directory on the way to the store — a plain directory, a symlinked directory, or the directory a link leads to — is owned by another user | **Found by audit 2026-09-25**, closing the CC-CFG-73 residual. Only store *links* were owner-checked; every directory component was followed by the kernel unchecked, and whoever owns a directory on the way can rename or re-point what lies beneath it, so they chose the file the token went to as surely as the owner of a store link. `resolveLinkChain` also joined each relative target with `path.resolve`, which cancels a `..` as text: `named.env` → `a/up/../ig.env` with `a/up` → `../dotfiles` was written to `a/ig.env` while every reader opened `ig.env`. **Fixed:** `realDirectory` in `src/core/config-write.ts` walks every component the way the kernel does (a directory link replaced by its target, `..` climbed physically, `ELOOP` after 40 directory links) and refuses one owned by neither this user nor root (`…: the path component <dir> on the way to it is owned by another user, so the store is not written through it; keep the store under directories owned by you or by root`). It runs for the store's directory and for the directory of every hop, before the lock and again under it (CC-CFG-72), so a config-home directory the write creates is judged once it exists. **Decision:** root-owned ancestors are accepted, because every path starts in root's directories (`/`, `/private`, the `/var` link, `/tmp`) and root can rewrite any file anyway; store links themselves stay strictly the operator's own. Skipped where there is no uid (Windows). **Behaviour change:** an `IG_ENV_FILE` inside another user's directory is now refused. Residual, accepted: a directory owned by this user or by root is not judged by its mode, so a group-writable one of the operator's own, or sticky `/tmp`, is walked through. | post-M5 · unit (`test/core/config-write.test.ts`, POSIX-only) |
| CC-CFG-75 | `HOME` (`%USERPROFILE%`) is blank, whitespace or relative and the write journal falls back to `~/.local/state` | **Found by audit 2026-09-25**, closing the CC-CFG-69 residual. The journal fallback joined the raw `os.homedir()`, so with `HOME="   "` the audit trail of every applied write went to `<cwd>/   /.local/state/…`, relative to whichever cwd the MCP client picked (CC-CFG-24). **Fixed:** `resolveWriteJournal` in `src/core/settings.ts` takes the home from `homeDirectory()`, refused as `validation` naming `HOME` / `USERPROFILE`, and only when neither `IG_WRITE_JOURNAL` nor an absolute `XDG_STATE_HOME` names the place. `DEFAULT_SETTINGS.writeJournal` is now a getter on the frozen object, resolved on read, so a bad home no longer has to be tolerated to keep the import from throwing. | post-M5 · unit + child process (`test/core/settings.test.ts`) |
| CC-CFG-76 | `IG_ENV_FILE` has a `..` after a symlinked directory, e.g. `/home/me/dots/up/../ig.env` with `up` a link | **Found by audit 2026-09-25.** `resolveStoreTarget` passed the value through `path.normalize`, which cancels `up/..` as text, while the entry opens the value as given and the kernel climbs from wherever `up` leads: `login` / `refresh` wrote a file the server never loads. **Fixed:** the writer uses the value exactly as `namedEnvFile` returns it, the same string the entry loads. | post-M5 · unit (`test/core/config-write.test.ts`, POSIX-only) |
| CC-DATA-104 | A token or registered secret sits right after a character `JSON.stringify` escapes (`\n`, `\t`, `\b`, `\f`, a C0 control, `"` or `\`) in a success result's text block | **Found by audit 2026-09-25.** The registry masked the finished result, so its text block was masked as TEXT, after `JSON.stringify` had already written those characters as escapes. | CC-PUB-56 | A container or child id read off a Graph response is not a Graph id (whitespace, a quote, a line feed, a bidi override, or more than 64 characters) and is named in an error message | **Found by audit 2026-09-25.** The carousel orphan list, the album ack-without-id refusal, the resumable-failure addendum, the publish-without-media-id warning and the ERROR, EXPIRED and unreadable-status errors interpolated the wire id raw. `quoteGraphId` in `src/core/errors.ts` returns an id matching `GRAPH_ID_PATTERN` (now defined there and re-exported by `src/tools/ids.ts`) bare, so every message about a real container is unchanged, and renders anything else through `quoteGraphText` (quoted, escaped, cut word-safe at 64 code points).
