Closed by the `(CC-PUB-56)` tests in `test/api/publishing.test.ts` (orphan list; ERROR, EXPIRED and unreadable status) and `test/tools/publishing.test.ts` (resumable addendum; ERROR still suppresses it; publish without a media id; album without an id). | post-M5 · unit |
| CC-AUTH-71 | `debug_token` (Path B) returns a `data_access_expires_at` outside the recorded range: in milliseconds, negative, a fraction, or past 9999-12-31T23:59:59Z | **Found by audit 2026-09-25.** CC-AUTH-68 bounded `expires_at` but not the field beside it, so `token_status` rendered `1790000000000` as a data-access window closing in year 58692 and `-1` as one that closed in 1969. `token_status` now renders `dataAccessExpiresAt` only when `isRecordableExpiry` holds; anything else is omitted with the existing "Data-access expiry is unknown" warning naming the value. `0` is recordable and still renders as the 1970 epoch, as before. Closed by `token_status holds a data-access expiry to the same range as expires_at (CC-AUTH-71)` and `token_status renders a data-access expiry at the upper bound itself (CC-AUTH-71)`. | post-M5 · unit |
| CC-DATA-105 | A `fetchAll` walk (media or comments) gets a Graph error on page 2+ that is not an invalid cursor: an expired token (`auth`), a revoked permission, a rate limit, or a Meta outage (`upstream`) | **Found by audit 2026-09-25.** `fetchPagedEdge` in `src/api/media.ts` swallowed EVERY `InstagramError` after the first page as CC-DATA-1 and published the partial result as `truncated: true` with "cursor may be stale (data changed between pages) — restart the listing". That advice is wrong for each of these errors, and a rate-limited caller that restarts at once spends the quota again. **Fixed:** only a `validation` error (Graph code 100, the stale-cursor shape) keeps the partial result; every other kind propagates with its own kind and code, as a first-page failure already did. Closed by `listMedia propagates a mid-walk Graph error that is not a stale cursor (CC-DATA-105)` and `listComments propagates a mid-walk rate limit instead of calling it a stale cursor (CC-DATA-105)`. | post-M5 · unit |
| CC-DATA-106 | `business_discovery` is answered with a whole body of JSON `null` | **Found by audit 2026-09-25.** `core/http` returns a JSON `null` body as `null` and `req` casts it, so `discoverBusiness` in `src/api/discovery.ts` read `business_discovery` off `null`. The raw `TypeError` surfaced as an internal error. CC-DATA-79 covered only a missing or non-object `business_discovery` field. **Fixed:** a `null` body gets the same `NO_PROFILE_NOTE` result; a scalar or array body already did. Closed by the body loop in `discoverBusiness says so when Graph answers without a business_discovery object (CC-DATA-79)`. | post-M5 · unit |
| CC-DATA-107 | A value handed to the redactor has an own `__proto__` key, as `JSON.parse` builds one from a Graph body or a tool result's JSON text block | **Found by audit 2026-09-25.** `redactValue` in `src/core/redact.ts` copied each key with `out[key] = value`, and for `__proto__` that is the prototype setter, not a data write: an object value became the clone's prototype and a primitive was ignored, so the key vanished from the clone, from `structuredContent` and from the re-serialized text block. Nothing leaked (the value was dropped, not shown), but the result silently lost a field. **Fixed:** every key is written with `Object.defineProperty` as an enumerable, writable, configurable data property, so `__proto__` is kept and its value redacted like any other, and no prototype is ever set. Closed by the `(CC-DATA-107)` tests in `test/core/redact.test.ts` (object and string values, a later key masking to the same spelling still wins, the clone stays writable). | post-M5 · unit |
| CC-DATA-108 | The secret redactor throws while masking a tool result — a JSON body nested deeper than its recursion can follow, or an injected redactor that fails | **Found by audit 2026-09-25.** `redactResult` in `src/mcp/registry.ts` failed closed only when the redactor RETURNED something unusable. A throw escaped the tool callback, so the MCP SDK rendered the exception's own message as the result, a text no redactor had seen. The default redactor does throw on real input: `JSON.stringify` writes an object nested 50,000 deep that `redactValue` cannot walk (`RangeError`). **Fixed:** a throw anywhere in the redaction is caught and the call returns the same "The tool result was withheld" error as the other unusable-redactor arms. Closed by `redactResult: a redactor that throws withholds the result and never echoes the exception (CC-DATA-108)` and `redactResult: a JSON body too deep for the redactor withholds the result (CC-DATA-108)`. | post-M5 · unit |
| CC-DATA-109 | An `IG_…` name sits right behind a letter-ending JSON escape (`\n`, `\t`, `\uXXXX`) in text that is masked as TEXT, not as a parsed value | **Assessed 2026-09-25, decision: kept, pinned by test.** In text the escape is the two characters `\` `n`, so the name's run starts at the `n`, the `IG_`-rooted exemption of the `IG` token shape does not apply, and an embedded `IG` (`DIGITALSTORE`) masks the rest of the name. Only text-masked blocks can show it: a JSON body is redacted as a value (CC-DATA-104), where the escape is a real line feed and the name is kept whole, and Graph error text is escaped as `\u{a}`, whose `}` ends the run. Accepting a run start behind an escape would also exempt a token glued onto such a name, which the backstop masks today; over-masking is the fail-safe side, so it stays. Pinned by `an IG_ name right behind a JSON escape is over-masked in free text, by decision (CC-DATA-109)`. | post-M5 · unit |
| CC-DATA-110 | A text block that is not exactly its own `JSON.stringify` rendering (prose around JSON, embedded JSON) carries a `"<secret key>":"value"` pair | **Assessed 2026-09-25, no change.** Such a block gets only string masking: registered exact values and the token shapes, not key-name masking. No path puts one in front of a client with a secret the registry does not hold. Every `src/tools` success goes through `json()`, which the registry decodes and redacts as a value. The other text blocks are error prose, built from Graph message text (token-stripped and bounded in `src/core/errors.ts`) or from a thrown error's message, and `core/http.ts` and `core/refresh.ts` swallow a JSON parse error instead of rethrowing V8's message, which quotes the body. Every secret this server holds (profile tokens, app secrets, the HTTP bearer, refreshed tokens) is registered when it comes into existence. A `"key":"value"` regex over free text would mask caption and comment content that only names such a key, and it would add a second, weaker spelling of the key rule. Revisit if a handler ever builds prose from a raw upstream body. | post-M5 · process |
| CC-DATA-111 | A wire id longer than 64 characters with no whitespace is named in a publishing message | **Assessed 2026-09-25, decision: kept.** `quoteGraphId` in `src/core/errors.ts` renders it as `"…" (N characters in all)`, with no prefix, because the cut is word-safe (CC-DATA-92). This Layer-0 code sees token SHAPES only. A registered secret with no shape (a 32-hex app secret, the HTTP bearer) is masked downstream on the finished message, and a hard cut at 64 code points through such a secret would leave a prefix the exact-value registry can no longer match. An id with whitespace still shows every whole word that fits. Showing a prefix of a single overlong run safely needs the exact-value redactor at the cut, which a Layer-0 mapper cannot import. Pinned by `quoteGraphId renders an overlong single-run wire id by its length only, never a hard-cut prefix (CC-DATA-111)`. | post-M5 · unit |
| CC-DATA-112 | `IG_PRETTY_JSON=true` and a tool other than the three account readers, including a write tool's preview, destructive-block or refusal result | **Found by audit 2026-09-25 (closes CC-DATA-30).** Every `json()` call site in `src/tools/{media,insights,comments,discovery,publishing}.ts` now passes `{ pretty: ctx.settings.prettyJson }`, and `previewResult` / `refusedResult` in `src/mcp/write-mode.ts` take the flag from the gate's context, so the write gate's own results follow it too. Threaded per call site rather than applied centrally: `defineTool` returns the spec it is given and `allTools` holds the exported spec objects, both pinned as identity, and a re-render after the fact would have to re-parse the text block. `test/tools/pretty-json.test.ts` is the guard that makes this safe: it enumerates `allTools` and drives every tool through five arms (preview, applied, destructive-blocked, declined, confirmer unavailable) plus the composite post tools' `already_published` and `in_progress` arms, and requires byte-for-byte `JSON.stringify(parsed, null, 2)` when the flag is on and compact output when it is off. The hand-written three-name allowlist is gone. Hand mutants (drop the flag at a media, publishing `published` / `already_published` / `in_progress`, account and write-gate refusal site; pass `false` at the destructive-block preview; hard-code `true` at a comments site and at the declined refusal) are all killed. | post-M5 · unit |
| CC-DATA-113 | `debug_token` (Path B) returns `data_access_expires_at: 0` | **Found by audit 2026-09-25.** Meta uses 0 for "no expiry / not applicable" on this field as on `expires_at`, but `isRecordableExpiry(0)` is true, so `token_status` published `dataAccessExpiresAt: "1970-01-01T00:00:00.000Z"`, a data-access window that closed decades ago. It is now omitted, the same shape as Meta leaving the field out, with no warning (Meta sent a meaningful value, not an unrepresentable one); the output-schema description says so. `expires_at: 0` was already `expiryState: 'never'` with no `expiresAt`, and is now pinned beside it on Path B. Test: `token_status (Path B) reads a zero expiry as "none", never as the 1970 epoch` in `test/tools/account.test.ts`; failed before the fix with `actual: '1970-01-01T00:00:00.000Z'`. Hand mutants (drop the zero guard from the rendering or from the warning condition; widen it to `<= 0` or to falsiness) are killed. Whether "no data-access expiry" should be stated explicitly instead of by absence is an owner decision. | post-M5 · unit |
| CC-DATA-114 | `mcp/result.ts` exports a `text()` builder that no `src` module calls | **Found by audit 2026-09-25.** Removed, with its three tests in `test/mcp/result.test.ts`; `test/mcp/registry.test.ts`, which used it to build stub handler results, now has a one-line local helper. Every tool body is JSON, so `IG_PRETTY_JSON` and `structuredContent` apply to all of them; a plain-text builder was an invitation to bypass both. The module docstring now counts three builders. Historical rows that name `text()` (CC-PROC-106, CC-PROC-182) are left as written. | post-M5 · unit |
| CC-INS-25 | `instagram_get_account_insights` with `metric_type: "time_series"`, bare or with a metric other than `reach` | **Found by audit 2026-09-26.** Meta's metrics table lists `time_series` for `reach` alone; every other account metric is `total_value` only. A bare `time_series` sent the whole default set of eleven, so the tool's default combination could never succeed, and an explicit total-only metric spent a call on a Graph rejection. **Fixed:** `TIME_SERIES_ACCOUNT_METRICS` (`['reach']`) is exported; a bare `time_series` defaults to it (the CC-INS-14 rule: the default for the thing the caller named), and any other metric with `time_series` is refused as `validation` before a call, naming the metrics and the fix. The CC-INS-2 precedent: a combination Meta's table rules out is refused client-side. `total_value` still takes every metric. Pinned by `time_series with no explicit metrics asks for reach, not all eleven (CC-INS-25)` and `time_series with a total-only metric is refused before a call is spent (CC-INS-25)` | post-M5 · unit |
| CC-INS-26 | `instagram_get_account_insights` with `since` after `until` | **Found by audit 2026-09-26.** The inverted window was forwarded to Graph, and when `since` was also past the retention floor it was reported as out of retention instead of inverted. **Fixed:** refused as `validation` before the retention check and independent of the clock; `since == until` stays allowed. Pinned by `a window that ends before it starts is refused, with or without a clock (CC-INS-26)` and `an inverted window is refused as inverted, not as out of retention (CC-INS-26)` | post-M5 · unit |
| CC-INS-27 | `instagram_get_audience_demographics` request shape | **Found by audit 2026-09-26.** Meta's reference marks `period` required and lists `lifetime` as the demographics metrics' only period, but the request omitted it. **Fixed:** `period=lifetime` is always sent. Pinned by `getAudienceDemographics builds metric_type=total_value with breakdown + timeframe` and `audience demographics sends period=lifetime, which Meta marks required (CC-INS-27)` | post-M5 · unit |
| CC-RATE-17 | A throttled response carries a `Retry-After` that is neither delta-seconds nor an HTTP-date, but that V8's `Date.parse` still accepts: `1.5`, `-1`, `+5`, `60,`, `Thu 5`, `12/31`, `Jan 1` | **Found by audit 2026-09-26.** CC-RATE-15 pinned the values `Date.parse` rejects (`2s`, `5;foo`, `5 seconds`), but V8's legacy fallback reads many others as a date: `'1.5'` and `'-1'` are 2001-01-01, `'+5'` is 2001-05-01, `'60,'` is 1960. Against the real clock each is a past instant, so `parseRetryAfter` in `src/core/http.ts` clamped the wait to 0 and the retry fired with no delay, which is the hammering CC-RATE-10 exists to avoid. Only the IMF-fixdate form (RFC 9110 §5.6.7, case-sensitive) now reaches `Date.parse`; anything else is `undefined` and takes the backoff. The obsolete RFC 850 and asctime forms also take the backoff (V8 reads asctime in local time). Tests: `a Retry-After that is not delta-seconds or an IMF-fixdate falls back to backoff (CC-RATE-17)` failed before the fix with `"1.5" … got 0`, and `an IMF-fixdate Retry-After on every weekday and month is still honored (CC-RATE-17)`. An IMF-fixdate `Date.parse` still rejects (`25:00:00`, day `99`) keeps the NaN guard covered. Hand mutants (drop the gate, drop either anchor, `/i`, any zone, optional comma, and dropping each of the 19 day and month names) are killed. The obsolete-form refusal is an owner decision, CC-RATE-18. | post-M5 · unit |
| CC-AUTH-72 | The `login` OAuth exchange endpoint accepts the connection and never answers | **Found by audit 2026-09-26.** `getJson` and `postForm` in `src/cli/login.ts` called `fetch` with no signal, and undici's default headers timeout is 300 s per stall with no bound on the body. So `login` could hang for minutes, or indefinitely on a trickled body, after the browser step, with the one-time code already spent. `refresh` has bounded its exchange at 30 s all along. Each of the four exchanges now carries `AbortSignal.timeout(LOGIN_EXCHANGE_TIMEOUT_MS)` (30 s), which also governs the body read. A timeout prints `login failed: The operation was aborted due to timeout` and exits 1. `exchangeCodeForToken` and `exchangeForLongLivedToken` take an optional `timeoutMs` for tests. Tests: `every login token exchange refuses redirects and carries a timeout signal`, `the default login exchange timeout is 30 s, the same budget refresh uses` and `a login exchange that never answers fails on its timeout instead of hanging`, all of which failed before the fix. Hand mutants (drop either signal, 60 s constant, any of the four call sites ignoring `timeoutMs`) are killed. **Amended 2026-09-26:** the timeout now rejects as an `InstagramError` of kind `upstream` with the `TimeoutError` on `cause` (CC-AUTH-74); the printed line is unchanged. | post-M5 · unit |
| CC-AUTH-73 | A `login` token endpoint (or a proxy in front of it) answers 3xx | **Found by audit 2026-09-26.** The `login` exchanges used `fetch`'s default `redirect: 'follow'`. A 307/308 on the Instagram code exchange POST re-sends the form body, `client_secret` and the authorization code, to whatever host `Location` names; a redirect of the three GET exchanges carries the secret in the query. `refresh` already refuses redirects for exactly this reason. Both helpers now pass `redirect: 'error'`, so a 3xx fails the login with a transport error and sends nothing on. Test: `every login token exchange refuses redirects and carries a timeout signal (CC-AUTH-72, CC-AUTH-73)`, which failed before the fix with `expected: 'error'`. Hand mutants dropping either `redirect` are killed. | post-M5 · unit |
| CC-DATA-115 | A cursor-paginated edge (`list_media`, `list_comments`, `list_stories`, `list_tagged_media`, `hashtag_media`, `list_linked_accounts`) answers its last page with `paging.cursors.after` still set and no `paging.next`, or a continuation page with `paging.next` and no `cursors` | **Found by audit 2026-09-26.** Meta's Paginated Results reference says `next` is the endpoint for the next page and that the last page omits it, while `cursors` may still be present; the repo's comment fixture (`example-list-comments.json`, synthetic) is exactly that shape. `fetchPagedEdge` and `getHashtagMedia` keyed the end of a listing on a missing `cursors.after`, so a single-page call reported `truncated: true` with a dead `after` token, and `fetchAll` spent one extra request on an empty page (or, with a server that repeats the page, stopped as `truncated` by the repeated-cursor guard). Both now decide through `nextPageCursor` in `src/api/media.ts`: no `paging.next` means the edge is finished, whatever `cursors` says; with `next` present, `cursors.after` is still the resume token, and only when `cursors` is absent is the `after` query parameter of the `next` URL used. A `next` with no usable cursor from either source is the CC-DATA-11 unusable-cursor case (truncated, with the note), and the repeated-cursor and page-cap guards still bound the walk, so a `next`-only loop cannot spin. The replies edge already keyed on `next` (CC-COM-15). Tests: the `end of listing` section of `test/api/media.test.ts`, four hashtag tests in `test/api/discovery.test.ts` and a `listComments` fixture test in `test/api/comments.test.ts`; they failed before the fix. Hand mutants on the changed lines are killed; a `\|\| null` on the URL fallback is equivalent and noted in the source. The URL fallback, and `business_discovery`'s nested media that drops its paging with no `truncated` flag, are owner decisions. **Amended 2026-09-26:** no `list_stories` tool exists — `list_media` never returns stories (CC-DATA-118); adding a stories tool is the owner decision CC-DATA-119. | post-M5 · unit |
| CC-PUB-57 | A media URL carries a control character the WHATWG parser silently drops: a trailing `\n` or `\r\n` pasted with it, a leading NUL, or a tab/LF inside the host or path (`https://cdn.exa\nmple.com/a.jpg`) | **Found by audit 2026-09-26.** `isHttpsUrl` in `src/api/media-spec.ts` judged `new URL(value)`, which strips leading and trailing C0 controls and removes every tab, LF and CR before it reads the scheme, so each of these passed as "a well-formed https URL". Every caller then forwards the RAW string to Graph as `image_url` / `video_url` / `cover_url` (the byte-for-byte pin), so the verdict was about a different URL than the one sent, and Meta was asked to fetch a host no resolver knows. **Fixed:** a value containing any `\p{Cc}` character is refused before parsing, through `isHttpsUrl`, `assertHttpsUrl` and `httpsUrlSchema`, with the ordinary https-only wording. Edge and interior SPACES stay accepted by decision: an interior space is percent-encoded, not dropped, and a padded URL is forwarded untouched per `a media URL is forwarded byte for byte, never rewritten on the way out`. Closed by `a URL the parser has to strip before it reads https is not the URL that gets forwarded` in `test/api/media-spec.test.ts` (failed before the fix with `expected: false, actual: true`) and `a media URL the parser had to clean up is refused, not forwarded raw` in `test/tools/publishing.test.ts` (image, carousel child, reel video and cover, story; nothing is created). Hand mutants (drop the check, narrow it to `\n`, anchor it at the start, drop the `u` flag, invert its result, widen it to `\s`) are killed. | post-M5 · unit |
| CC-AUTH-74 | A `login` token exchange times out, or the transport itself fails (connection refused, DNS, a redirect refused by `redirect: 'error'`) | **Found by audit 2026-09-26 (carried from CC-AUTH-72).** `getJson` and `postForm` in `src/cli/login.ts` let the raw rejection escape the exported `exchangeCodeForToken` / `exchangeForLongLivedToken` untyped: a `DOMException` named `TimeoutError` for the 30 s budget, a `TypeError('fetch failed')` for a transport failure. Every other failure of the same functions is an `InstagramError`, and `http.ts` and `refresh.ts` both type the same errors through `toInstagramError`. Both helpers now go through one `sendExchange`, whose catch arm is `throw toInstagramError(err)`: a timeout or transport failure becomes kind `upstream` with the original on `cause`, the message bounded and token-stripped by `boundGraphText`, and an `InstagramError` already mapped by `readJsonOrThrow` (an OAuth refusal, a non-JSON body) passes through unchanged. The operator-facing line is unchanged (`login failed: The operation was aborted due to timeout`, exit 1). Tests: `a login exchange timeout rejects with a typed upstream InstagramError, cause kept`, `a login transport failure or a refused redirect is typed as upstream`, `a mapped exchange refusal passes the transport wrapper unchanged`, `runLogin prints a timed-out exchange as one login failed line and exits 1`; the CC-AUTH-72 test now asserts the typed error and a `TimeoutError` cause. Hand mutants killed 2/2 (rethrow `err` raw; fallback kind `auth`). | post-M5 · unit |
| CC-AUTH-75 | A `login` exchange answers 200 with a whitespace-only `access_token` (`' '`, `'\t\n'`, a no-break space) | **Found by audit 2026-09-26.** `requireToken` refused only a non-string or an empty string, so `'   '` passed as a token. On the code exchange it was then sent to the long-lived exchange beside the app secret, and at the end of the flow the persist step refused it with a misleading `an access token is required`, after the one-time code had been spent. `requireToken` now also refuses a value that is empty after `trim()`, with the same `auth` error as a missing field (`Token exchange response did not include an access_token.`), so the run stops at the exchange that returned it. A token with a value and stray padding is still kept verbatim, not trimmed (the same choice as `refresh`). Tests: `a whitespace-only access_token is refused by every exchange`, `a padded access_token that has a value is kept verbatim, not trimmed`, `runLogin stops at a blank short-lived token: the secret is not sent on` (one fetch, exit 1, nothing persisted). Hand mutants killed 2/2 (drop `.trim()`; drop the blank check). | post-M5 · unit |
| CC-AUTH-76 | A `login` failure message quotes the app secret, the code or a token in its form-encoded spelling | **Found by audit 2026-09-26 (carried from wave 17).** CC-AUTH-55 registered each credential with the run's redactor by its RAW value only, but every exchange puts them through `URLSearchParams`, which writes `+` as `%2B`, `/` as `%2F`, `=` as `%3D` and a space as `+`. A transport or proxy error quoting the request URL or body, or a persist error quoting an encoded token, carried a spelling the exact-substring redactor did not know. `refresh` already registers both spellings through `surfaceForms` (`src/core/refresh.ts`); that helper is now exported and `runLogin` registers `surfaceForms(appSecret)`, `surfaceForms(code)` and both tokens. Exposure in practice is small (Meta's hex secrets and `EAA`/`IG` tokens are URL-safe), but the app secret and the code are operator- and redirect-supplied and can be anything. Tests: `runLogin masks the app secret and the code in their form-encoded spelling` (a POST body and a GET URL), `runLogin masks the short-lived token in its form-encoded spelling`, `runLogin masks the long-lived token in its form-encoded spelling`; each fixture is asserted to encode differently. Hand mutants killed 4/4 (each of the four registrations reverted to the raw value). | post-M5 · unit |
| CC-RATE-18 | `Retry-After` arrives in the obsolete RFC 850 (`Saturday, 26-Sep-26 00:00:30 GMT`) or asctime (`Sat Sep 26 00:00:30 2026`) form, dated in the future | **Owner decision, recorded 2026-09-26; behaviour unchanged.** RFC 9110 §5.6.7 says a recipient MUST accept both obsolete forms; `parseRetryAfter` accepts only delta-seconds and IMF-fixdate (CC-RATE-17) and backs off on anything else. CC-RATE-17 pinned the refusal only with 1994 dates, where honoring would have meant a zero wait; the new test pins it with dates 30 s ahead of the clock, where honoring would mean a real wait, so a change of policy has to change a test. **Recommendation: keep the refusal.** Meta sends delta-seconds; `Date.parse` reads asctime in the host's LOCAL time (measured: a +03:00 host reads the 30 s-ahead asctime as 2 h 59 m 30 s in the past, i.e. a zero wait); and backoff is the safe default for an unreadable hint. If the owner wants RFC conformance instead, the correct change is an explicit UTC parse of both forms (two anchored regexes and `Date.UTC`, with RFC 850's two-digit year windowed per RFC 9110 §5.6.7's 50-year rule), never a hand-off to `Date.parse`. Test: `a FUTURE-dated RFC 850 or asctime Retry-After still takes backoff, not its wait`. Hand mutant (IMF regex widened to accept both obsolete forms) killed 1/1. | post-M5 · unit |
| CC-PUB-58 | A media URL whose scheme is not followed by exactly `//`, or that carries a backslash before its query: `https:/cdn/a.jpg`, `https:cdn/a.jpg`, `https:///cdn/a.jpg`, `https:\\cdn\a.jpg`, `https://cdn\a.jpg`, `https://cdn/photos\a.jpg?v=1` | **Found by audit 2026-09-26.** `isHttpsUrl` in `src/api/media-spec.ts` judged `new URL(value)`, and for the special `https` scheme the WHATWG parser repairs the authority delimiter: one slash, no slash or three slashes after `https:` all come out as `https://cdn/…`, and a backslash anywhere before the query counts as `/`. Every caller forwards the RAW string to Graph (the byte-for-byte pin), and an RFC 3986 reader of `https:/cdn/a.jpg` finds no host at all (or, with backslashes, a different host and path), so the verdict was earned by a different URL than the one sent — the CC-PUB-57 defect by another route. The old test pinned `https:/cdn.example.com/a.jpg` as accepted; that pin is reversed. **Fixed:** before parsing, the raw value must have exactly `//` after its FIRST colon (`/^[^:]*:\/\/(?!\/)/`, so a `://` inside the query cannot stand in) and no backslash before `?`/`#` (`/^[^?#]*\\/`); otherwise it is refused through `isHttpsUrl`, `assertHttpsUrl` and `httpsUrlSchema` with the ordinary https-only wording. Neither check names the scheme, so the exact `protocol === 'https:'` compare (CC-PUB-31) stays load-bearing. Still accepted by decision: an upper-case scheme or host (case-insensitive under every URL grammar), a backslash in the query or fragment (kept verbatim by the parser), a `://` inside the query, and edge spaces (CC-PUB-57). Host normalisations that do not change the structure (`ex%61mple.com`, IPv4 shorthand, dot segments) are not refused: an RFC 3986 reader resolves the same host and path. Closed by `a URL the parser has to re-slash before it reads a host is not the URL that gets forwarded (CC-PUB-58)` in `test/api/media-spec.test.ts` and `a media URL the parser had to re-slash is refused, not forwarded raw (CC-PUB-58)` in `test/tools/publishing.test.ts` (image, carousel child, reel video and cover, story; nothing is created). Hand mutants (drop either check or both, invert either, `\|\|`→`&&`, return true, `[^:]*`→`.*`, drop or flip the lookahead, one slash, drop either `^`, `[^?#]*`→`.*`, drop `?` or `#` from the class) are killed, 16/16. | post-M5 · unit |
| CC-PUB-59 | `resume_container_id` is given together with media inputs (`imageUrls`, `videoUrl`, `coverUrl`, `caption`, `userTags`, …) on `instagram_post_image` / `_reel` / `_story` | **Owner decision (audit 2026-09-26), no behaviour change.** Resume publishes the existing container and ignores every media input, as the `resume_container_id` description already says ("When set, the media inputs are ignored."). The media inputs are not validated in that mode and the confirm prompt shows only `resume_container_id`, so a caller that passes a new caption believing it will apply gets the container's original caption with no signal. Resume also does not check that the container is of the tool's kind (a story tool can publish a feed image container). **Recommendation:** keep resume ignoring them (the container is immutable), but add a `details.warnings` entry naming the ignored fields in both preview and apply, so the operator sees it before confirming. | post-M5 · unit |
| CC-PUB-60 | The write-gate confirm prompt for a publish shows counts, not content: `media_kind`, `image_count`, caption `characters` / `hashtags` / `mentions` and warnings, but never the caption text or any URL | **Owner decision (audit 2026-09-26), no behaviour change.** Omitting URLs is deliberate (pre-signed URLs carry credentials, CC-PUB-8). Omitting the caption means the human approving a PUBLIC post never sees what it says, which weakens the consent the gate exists to obtain; the agent could publish a caption the operator never read. **Recommendation:** show the caption, sanitised and truncated, inside the existing fenced details blob (within `CONFIRM_DETAILS_MAX`), and the URL hosts only (no path or query), so the operator can tell whose CDN is being fetched without leaking signatures. Pinned surface — a policy change, not a fix. | post-M5 · unit |
| CC-PUB-61 | A `userTags` entry without `x` / `y` on a feed image | **Owner decision (audit 2026-09-26), no behaviour change.** `userTagSchema` keeps `x` and `y` optional (0–1 when given), and the repo docs do not say they are required. Meta documents `x`/`y` as required for image user tags, while reels (and stories) take `username` only, so an x/y-less tag on a feed image is likely refused by Meta at container creation, after the gate. **Recommendation:** confirm with a live probe; if Meta refuses, require `x` and `y` for feed images (single image and carousel image children) while keeping them optional for reels. | post-M5 · live |
| CC-PUB-62 | A padded media URL (`' https://cdn/a.jpg '`) | **Reviewed by audit 2026-09-26, no change.** Accepted and forwarded byte for byte by existing decision (CC-PUB-57 and `a media URL is forwarded byte for byte, never rewritten on the way out`). The parser trims the spaces for the verdict while Graph receives them; the leading-space case is the only remaining parser rewrite that the verdict tolerates. **Recommendation:** keep, unless a live probe shows Meta refusing a padded `image_url`; then refuse edge spaces as part of the CC-PUB-58 shape check rather than trimming (never rewrite). | post-M5 · unit |
| CC-PUB-63 | A hashtag in a script with combining marks (`#हिन्दी`, `#สวัสดี`) against `HASHTAG_RE = /#[\p{L}\p{N}_]+/gu`, which lacks `\p{M}` | **Reviewed by audit 2026-09-26, not a bug.** The regex stops at the first combining mark, so the matched TOKEN is truncated, but the code only COUNTS matches, and each hashtag still yields exactly one match (`'#हिन्दी #भारत'` → 2, `'#สวัสดี #ไทย'` → 2). Only a mark directly after `#` (`#́abc`, not a real hashtag) counts differently. Adding `\p{M}` would be an equivalent mutant for every observable result; no change. Revisit if hashtag TEXT is ever surfaced. | post-M5 · unit |
| CC-DATA-116 | `discover_business` reads a profile with more media than the nested `media.limit(N)` returns | **Found by audit 2026-09-26 (carried from wave 17).** `readMediaEdge` in `src/api/discovery.ts` kept only `media.data` and dropped the whole `paging` object. A capped media list therefore read as the account's complete output, and nothing could fetch the rest. Meta's business_discovery reference says the field-expanded `media` edge carries `before`/`after` cursors when there are several pages, and **no** `next`/`previous`. The shared end rule (no `next` means finished, CC-DATA-115) would therefore call every page the last one here. The new `readMediaPaging` reads `paging.cursors.after` first. Only when that is absent does it fall back to `nextPageCursor`, which covers a `next`-only page and the documented single-page answer (no cursor and no `next` means complete, `truncated: false`). The tool publishes `mediaPaging: { after?, truncated }` alongside `media`, never without it. A usable cursor gives `truncated: true`, `after`, and a note (`MEDIA_MORE_NOTE`) saying more **may** exist and that an empty list on the next read means the end. A cursor that is present but unusable (`null`, `''`, a non-string, or outside `^[A-Za-z0-9_+/=-]{1,2048}$`) gives `truncated: true` with no `after` and the CC-DATA-11 unusable-cursor note. A new `mediaAfter` input resumes the edge as `media.after(<cursor>).limit(N){…}`. A field expression has no escaping, so the cursor is pinned to that charset in the tool schema and re-checked in the api layer, the same way as `username`; the refusal (`validation`) does not echo it. With no cursor the field spec is byte-identical to before. Closed by `discoverBusiness reports the nested media edge truncated with a resume cursor instead of dropping its paging (CC-DATA-116)`, `discoverBusiness resumes the nested media edge with media.after(cursor) (CC-DATA-116)`, `discoverBusiness refuses a mediaAfter that would rewrite the field expression, without echoing it (CC-DATA-116)`, `real McpServer: discover_business publishes the media edge paging and resumes from it (CC-DATA-116)` and `discover_business declares exactly username + mediaLimit + mediaAfter with their real bounds`. Hand mutants: 26/26 killed (1 by the type checker). `[verify — needs a live call: business_discovery on a handle with > N posts, confirm the cursor syntax media.after(<cursor>).limit(N) is honoured and that the last page's cursors shape (after present or absent) matches the reference]` | post-M5 · unit + live |
| CC-DATA-117 | A `paging.next` URL whose `after` is odd: named twice, `%`-encoded, or on a host that is not Graph's | **Found by audit 2026-09-26 (carried from wave 17).** Verified first. The `next` URL is never requested: `afterFromNextUrl` reads only its `after`, and that value goes out as an ordinary query parameter to the pinned Graph host and the same edge path through `core/host`, which encodes it. A foreign host, an encoded `&access_token=`, or a `limit` in the link therefore changes nothing about where the request goes or what else it asks for. These are not bugs, and a test now pins them. The one real ambiguity was a duplicated `after`. `searchParams.get` silently followed the first copy, but which copy a server honours is its own choice (first-wins and last-wins both exist), so the walk could resume from a position the link never meant. `afterFromNextUrl` now uses `getAll` and returns `null` unless there is exactly one value. That is the CC-DATA-11 "present but unusable" stop (`truncated: true` and the note), never the end of the listing. It applies to every cursor walk and to `discover_business` (CC-DATA-116). Closed by `listMedia refuses a \`next\` URL that names \`after\` twice, and never follows a foreign host (CC-DATA-117)`. Hand mutants: 4/4 killed. | post-M5 · unit |
| CC-COM-18 | `reply_to_comment` / `create_comment` with a whitespace-only `message` (`' '`, `' \n\t '`) | **Found by audit 2026-09-26.** CC-COM-6 has always said "zod rejects empty/whitespace client-side", but both inputs declared only `z.string().min(1)`. So `"   "` passed the schema and the write gate, and with `apply: true` it reached Graph as a public write. Both now share `messageField()`, which adds `.regex(/\S/, 'must contain a non-whitespace character')`. That demands one non-whitespace character and, unlike a `.trim()`, leaves the posted text exactly as written. The published input schema gains `pattern: '\\S'` for both. Closed by `reply_to_comment and create_comment refuse a whitespace-only message (CC-COM-18)` (through a real McpServer: both tools refuse, a padded real message is posted untrimmed, and only those two calls reach Graph), plus the updated published-contract snapshot. Hand mutants: 5/5 killed. | post-M5 · unit |
| CC-COM-19 | `get_comment` answers with a `media` context whose `media_type` or `permalink` has the wrong type (`null`, a number) | **Found by audit 2026-09-26.** `commentDetailOutput.media` types `media_type` and `permalink` as optional strings, and the SDK validates structured content. `commentDetailToRecord` cast every scalar of the comment itself (CC-COM-16), but for the nested `media` it checked only the id. So one `media_type: null` failed the whole read with MCP error -32602. The media object is now copied (the api object is not mutated), and `media_type` and `permalink` are dropped when they are not strings, the same way as the comment's own scalars. Extra passthrough keys stay. Closed by `real McpServer: get_comment drops a null media_type and a numeric permalink instead of failing (CC-COM-19)`. Hand mutants: 5/5 killed. | post-M5 · unit |
| CC-AUTH-77 | Path B: `debug_token` reports `is_valid: true` together with a `data_access_expires_at` that is already in the past | **Found by audit 2026-09-26.** CC-AUTH-12 says `doctor` and `token_status` report the data-access window separately. Neither did so for a window that had closed. `doctor` never read `dataAccessExpiresAtSec` at all, so a valid token whose window had closed printed only OK token lines. `token_status` published the past `dataAccessExpiresAt` with no warning. In both cases every Instagram data read fails with a permission error while the diagnostics look healthy. The window is now read in one place, `summarizeDataAccessExpiry` in `src/api/account.ts`, which both callers share. It returns `none` for an omitted field or `0` (CC-DATA-113), `unknown` with the value named for anything that is not a recordable instant (CC-AUTH-45, CC-AUTH-71), `expired` with a remedy when the window closed at or before now, and `open` otherwise. `token_status` keeps the date and appends the expired sentence to `warning`, next to any token-expiry warning. `doctor` prints `OK Data access expiry: open until <iso>.`, `WARN … expired — …`, `INFO … unknown — …`, or nothing. A closed window is a WARN, not a FAIL, because the reachability GET is the authority on whether data reads work and fails on its own when they do not. Tests: `summarizeDataAccessExpiry classifies the data-access window (CC-AUTH-77)`, `token_status warns when the data-access window has closed on a valid token (CC-AUTH-77)`, `Path B: doctor reports the data-access window, which expires independently (CC-AUTH-77)`. **Owner decision, no behaviour change:** CC-AUTH-12 also asks for a warning as the window nears its end. No threshold exists for that. `IG_REFRESH_AFTER_DAYS` (default 45, sized for the 60-day token) would warn for half of a 90-day window, and it would flip the pinned `token_status still renders a representable data-access expiry with no warning` (30 days left). Recommendation: warn within a fixed 7 days of the window closing, rather than reusing `IG_REFRESH_AFTER_DAYS`. Also for the owner: whether a closed window should FAIL `doctor` even when reachability passes. Recommendation: keep WARN. | post-M5 · unit |
| CC-DATA-121 | A string the token-shape backstop scans holds a long `[A-Za-z0-9_-]` run that begins `IG_` and repeats `IG<alnum>` (`IG_IGaIGaIGa…`): a model-supplied tool argument with no length cap (a caption before its client-side guard, a cursor, a confirm-prompt field), a log field, a tool result, or Graph error text | **Found by audit 2026-09-26, security (DoS).** The `IG` token shape spelled its `IG_` exemption as a nested lookbehind, `(?<!(?<![A-Za-z0-9_-])IG_[A-Za-z0-9_-]*)`, checked at every `IG<alnum>` candidate. On a run rooted at `IG_`, every candidate is exempt and none is consumed, so each one walked the run back to its start. The cost was quadratic: 16 ms at 8 000 characters, 2.7 s at 64 000, 15 s at 200 000. It ran synchronously inside the redactor that every log line, confirm prompt, journal entry and tool result passes through, and inside `stripTokens` in `core/errors.ts`, which runs over the whole Graph error text (bodies up to 16 MiB) BEFORE the 1 000-code-point cap. One such string stalled the whole stdio server. **Fixed 2026-09-26** in both copies (`src/core/redact.ts` `TOKEN_SHAPE_PATTERNS`, `src/core/errors.ts` `TOKEN_SHAPES`). The IG shape now splits the text into maximal `[A-Za-z0-9_-]+` runs, leaves a run that `startsWith('IG_')` whole, and applies `/IG[A-Za-z0-9][A-Za-z0-9_-]{19,}/g` inside every other run. This is linear: 200 000 characters now take about 5 ms. It is equal to the lookbehind on every input. A candidate's third character is alphanumeric, so it never starts an `IG_` run itself, and the greedy body cannot leave its run, so "the maximal run begins `IG_`" is exactly what the lookbehind asked. Order and markers are unchanged, CC-PROC-72 / CC-PROC-186 / CC-DATA-109 behaviour is unchanged, and the drift test stays green. Closed by `property: the IG exemption masks exactly what the lookbehind it replaced did (CC-DATA-121)` (2 000 fast-check runs against the old regex), `a long IG_-rooted run of IG candidates is redacted in linear time (CC-DATA-121)` (200 000 characters under 1 s, exempt run kept whole, non-exempt run masked), and `a run is exempt only when it begins IG_, not IG- or IG (CC-DATA-121)` in `test/core/redact.test.ts`. Also closed by `the IG exemption strips exactly what the lookbehind it replaced did (CC-DATA-121)` and `a long IG_-rooted run in a Graph message is stripped in linear time (CC-DATA-121)` in `test/core/errors.test.ts`. Hand mutants: redact.ts 28/29 killed and errors.ts 22/22 killed. The one survivor, dropping `g` from the inner IG shape, is equivalent: a match runs greedily to the end of its run, so a run holds at most one. It is documented in both equivalent-mutant notes. | post-M5 · unit |
| CC-DATA-122 | A boxed primitive (`new String(token)`, `new Number(7)`, `new Boolean(false)`) nested anywhere in a value the redactor clones (a log field object, a journal entry, a tool result built by code) | **Found by audit 2026-09-26, security.** `redactValue` in `src/core/redact.ts` walked a boxed `String` as an ordinary object, so the clone was its index keys, `{"0":"E","1":"A",…}`, one character per value. Neither the registry nor any token shape can match one character, so the secret reached the sink in pieces, trivially reassembled. This is CC-AUTH-47's defect one level down: the logger's guard only wraps a string passed as the whole `fields` argument. `new Number` and `new Boolean` became `{}`, losing data that `JSON.stringify` would have written. **Fixed 2026-09-26:** `redactValue` unwraps a boxed `String` and redacts the primitive, and returns a boxed `Number` / `Boolean` as its primitive, mirroring `JSON.stringify`. Wire data (`JSON.parse`) never holds a boxed primitive, so this affects only values built in code. Closed by `a boxed string nested in a value is unwrapped and masked, not split into characters (CC-DATA-122)` and `a boxed number or boolean is unwrapped to its primitive, as JSON.stringify writes it (CC-DATA-122)` in `test/core/redact.test.ts`. Hand mutants: 6/6 killed (String branch removed, String unwrapped without redaction, Number/Boolean branch removed, each half of the `instanceof` disjunction dropped, wrapper returned unwrapped). | post-M5 · unit |
| CC-DATA-123 | A value the redactor clones carries an INHERITED `toJSON`: a `Date`, `URL` or `Buffer` nested in a log field or a code-built result | **Assessed 2026-09-26, no behaviour change.** `redactValue` copies own enumerable properties only, and it drops an own function `toJSON` on purpose (CC-AUTH-48). A `Date` therefore clones to `{}` and a `URL` to `{}`. A `Buffer` clones to its index keys, the same char-split shape as CC-DATA-122 but for bytes. `JSON.stringify` would have written the ISO string, the href, or `{type:"Buffer",data:[…]}`. No shipped call site passes such a value today: the journal's `ts` is already an ISO string, and wire data holds none. The loss is silent data loss, not a leak, except that Buffer bytes of a secret would be emitted as numbers no token shape sees. **Owner decision:** (a) leave it (the current contract is "plain JSON data only"); or (b) recommended: call an inherited `toJSON` (one that is not an own property) and redact its RESULT, so a `Date` / `URL` survive as strings and a `Buffer` is masked as a unit. That keeps CC-AUTH-48 intact, because the call happens before redaction rather than on the clone after it. | owner |
| CC-DATA-124 | A secret sits under a camelCase key: `accessToken`, `appSecret`, `clientSecret`, `appsecretProof` (e.g. an options object or an `err.cause` logged whole) | **Assessed 2026-09-26, no behaviour change.** `SECRET_KEY_PATTERN` matches only the snake_case wire spellings (`access_token`, `app_secret`, `client_secret`, `appsecret_proof`) and `authorization`. A camelCase key's value is masked only if it is registered or token-shaped. Every value this server holds is registered at load, mint and refresh, and the 32-hex app secret is the one value that is neither shape-visible nor, before `registerSecret` runs, registered. This is the same vocabulary class as the pinned hyphenated-key finding (`app-secret`), so it is not re-litigated here. **Owner decision:** (a) leave it (the registry is the primary line); or (b) recommended: widen the key pattern to case- and separator-insensitive forms (`access[_-]?token`, `app[_-]?secret`, `client[_-]?secret`, `appsecret[_-]?proof`). This is cheap, and it closes the class, the hyphenated case included, for code-built objects. | owner |
| CC-DATA-125 | Untrusted text spells a near-miss of the fence delimiters: a case variant (`[/untrusted]`), extra inner whitespace, or a zero-width character inside the tag | **Assessed 2026-09-26, no behaviour change.** `fence()` in `src/mcp/result.ts` defangs only the exact-case delimiters. A near-miss passes through as written. The text-block escaper then renders a zero-width character as `​` in `content`, but `structuredContent` keeps it raw. The exact-match defang itself (`[ /UNTRUSTED]`) still reads to a model as a closing tag. Models are not parsers, so a variant can close the fence in the model's reading even though it cannot in code. **Owner decision:** (a) leave it (the fence is advisory, and the tool descriptions already say fenced text is data); or (b) recommended: detect the delimiters case-insensitively, tolerating whitespace and `\p{Cf}` inside the brackets, and defang by replacing the brackets (e.g. `⟦/UNTRUSTED⟧`) rather than inserting a space. | owner |
| CC-DATA-126 | A Graph string is made only of code points that render as nothing but are not `\p{Cf}`: U+FE0F (variation selector), U+3164 / U+115F (Hangul fillers), U+2800 (braille blank), or a lone surrogate | **Assessed 2026-09-26, no behaviour change.** `visibleText` in `src/core/untrusted.ts` treats every non-`Cc`/`Cf`/`Z*` code point as visible, so these strings count as non-empty. A Graph error message made only of them is kept instead of falling back to the generic text, and the same gap applies to the wave-18 open decision on a zero-width-only comment message (not implemented here). Nothing leaks, but the model is shown an apparently blank message. **Owner decision:** (a) leave it; or (b) recommended: fold this into the wave-18 zero-width decision and extend `visibleText` with an explicit blank-renderer set (`\p{Mn}` variation selectors, U+115F, U+1160, U+3164, U+FFA0, U+2800, and unpaired surrogates). | owner |
| CC-DATA-127 | `quoteUntrusted` / `quoteUntrustedString` bound text by `max` code points, but escaping happens after the cut | **Assessed 2026-09-26, no behaviour change.** The cut counts code points before escaping, and a control or format character then renders as `\u{…}` (8 characters for `\u{200b}`, 9 for a supplementary tag character). The emitted text can therefore be about 8–9× `max`: a 1 000-code-point message of zero-width spaces comes out at 8 026 characters (measured). The cut never splits an escape, because escaping follows it, and the ellipsis marker is appended after escaping, so it is never cut either. The bound is on content, not on bytes. **Owner decision:** (a) leave it (recommended: the bound exists to keep a hostile message from flooding the context, and 9× a 1 000 cap is still small); or (b) document the bound as "`max` code points of source text" in `core/untrusted.ts`, or cap after escaping at a code-point boundary that never splits a `\u{…}`. | owner |
| CC-PROC-208 | A client config passes a secret to the server entry as an argument (`"args": ["--access-token=EAA…"]`, or a bare pasted token), or `doctor` / `refresh` get a token-shaped stray argument | **Found by audit 2026-09-26, security.** `assertUsage` in `src/index.ts` echoed the refused token into stderr, and MCP hosts capture the server's stderr into their log files. The unknown-subcommand refusal echoed the first token whole: CC-PROC-71 cut only the `doctor` / `refresh` stray token at `=`, so `instagram-mcp-ai --access-token=<token>` wrote the token into the host log. Neither refusal applied the token-shape redactor, so a bare pasted token (`EAA…`, `IG…`, 64-hex) was echoed verbatim in both places. **Fixed 2026-09-26:** both refusals now name the token through one helper, `argvName`. It cuts the token at its first `=` and then runs it through `createRedactor()`, so an inline value is never shown and a token-shaped name is shown as `[REDACTED]`. Exit code (2), the usage text, the empty stdout and the refusal wording are unchanged. Closed by `a refused argv token never echoes an inline value or a token-shaped value (CC-PROC-208)` in `test/index.test.ts`. The test pins the whole stderr and an empty stdout for `--access-token=<secret>` → `'--access-token'`, `=<secret>` → `''`, `--a=<secret>=x` → `'--a'` (first `=`, not last), a bare `EAA…` → `'[REDACTED]'`, `x<EAA…>=v` → `'x[REDACTED]'`, and `doctor <EAA…>` → `doctor: unknown argument '[REDACTED]'; …`. It also asserts that neither the secret nor the token appears in stderr. Hand mutants: 7/7 killed (no `=` cut, no redactor, subcommand refusal reverted to the raw token, stray refusal reverted to the raw token, `slice(0, eq + 1)`, `lastIndexOf`, `eq !== -1`). | post-M5 · unit |
| CC-DATA-128 | `describeWireValue` is handed a value that `JSON.stringify` rejects and `String()` rejects too: a circular object with no prototype (`Object.create(null)`), or a revoked `Proxy` | **Found by audit 2026-09-26.** The function is documented as total, because every caller (the timestamp guards and the doctor lines) is documented never to throw. However, its fallback for a value `JSON.stringify` throws on was a bare `String(value)`. `String()` throws `TypeError` on a null-prototype object (it has no `toString` / `Symbol.toPrimitive`) and on a revoked proxy, so the renderer threw from inside the guard that called it. Neither value can come off `JSON.parse`, so this is not reachable from wire data today. It is a contract bug, and the kind the docstring says it is handled for. **Fixed 2026-09-26:** the `String()` fallback has its own `try`, and a value that neither renderer accepts renders as `[unrenderable] (type object)`. Closed by `describeWireValue does not throw when String() throws too (CC-DATA-128)` in `test/core/time.test.ts`, which covers both values. Hand mutants: 2/2 killed (fallback literal changed, inner catch removed). | post-M5 · unit |
| CC-PROC-209 | `Clock.sleep(ms)` with `ms` above 2^31−1 (about 24.8 days) | **Assessed 2026-09-26, no behaviour change.** Node clamps a `setTimeout` delay over 2^31−1 to 1 ms (with a `TimeoutOverflowWarning`), so such a sleep would return almost at once. No caller can reach it today, because every delay is bounded: `Retry-After` is capped at 60 s, the throttle is 1 s, the lock poll is 20 ms, and the login capture window is 5 min. **Owner decision:** (a) leave it (recommended — latent only, and the cap on `Retry-After` is the real guard), or (b) clamp or chunk inside `Clock.sleep` so that a future unbounded caller cannot turn a long wait into a hot loop. | owner |
| CC-PROC-210 | `bin/instagram-mcp-ai.cjs` is run on a Node old enough that it cannot parse dynamic `import()` (Node ≤ 10) | **Assessed 2026-09-26, no behaviour change.** The launcher is CommonJS so that its Node-version gate can run before the ESM entry loads. On a runtime whose parser does not know `import()`, however, the whole file is a `SyntaxError` and the friendly "requires Node …" message never prints. Only end-of-life runtimes are affected, and on every Node that can parse the file the gate works as designed. **Owner decision:** (a) leave it (recommended — the `engines` field and the install-time warning already cover these runtimes), or (b) load the entry through `new Function('p', 'return import(p)')` or a `require` of a tiny ESM shim, so the gate message reaches even a Node 10 user. | owner |
| CC-DATA-118 | A model asked "does the account have live stories?" calls `instagram_list_media` and reads an empty or story-free page | **Found by audit 2026-09-26.** Meta's IG User Media reference (Limitations, read 2026-09-26) says the edge does not return story media and points to `GET /<IG_USER_ID>/stories`; it also caps the edge at the 10K most recent media. The `list_media` description listed "stories" among the kinds it returns and said "counts on stories" may be absent, so a model answered "no active stories" off a page that could never hold one. The description now lists feed posts, reels and albums, states that stories are not included because they live on a separate edge this tool does not read, and drops the story wording from the absent-fields sentence. `docs/tools.md` names the stories edge and the 10K cap; the README tools table is regenerated. Tests: the list_media description tests in `test/tools/media.test.ts` (published-contract pin and the named CC-DATA-118 assertion, including a negative match on "reels, stories" / "counts on stories"); they failed before the fix. Hand mutants restoring "stories" in the kind list, restoring "counts on stories", dropping the exclusion sentence and inverting it are all killed (4/4). Whether to add a stories tool is an owner decision (CC-DATA-119). | post-M5 · unit + docs |
| CC-DATA-119 | An operator wants the account's live (non-expired) stories | **Owner decision, no behaviour change.** No tool reads `GET /{ig-id}/stories`, and `list_media` cannot return stories (CC-DATA-118). Options: (a) add a read-only `instagram_list_stories` in the `media` package over `fetchPagedEdge` with `MEDIA_FIELDS` (stories carry no like/comment counts, so those fields are already optional in the output schema), or (b) keep it out of scope and leave the description's disclosure as the answer. **Recommendation: (a)** — it is a small, read-only addition on existing paging code, it is already listed as a gap in `docs/reviews/product-dx-review.md`, and it makes CC-DATA-115's `list_stories` mention true. | owner |
| CC-DATA-120 | `instagram_get_media` on a `CAROUSEL_ALBUM` whose inline `children` is missing or empty, and the `/children` fallback call fails (throttle, 5xx after retries, permission) | **Owner decision, no behaviour change.** The handler awaits `getMediaChildren` without a catch, so the fallback's error replaces the whole result: the album detail that was already read successfully is discarded and the caller sees only the child-edge failure. Options: (a) keep today's behaviour (all-or-nothing), or (b) return the detail with `children` omitted and a `note` naming the fallback error kind. **Recommendation: (b)** — the fallback is best-effort enrichment (CC-DATA-66/67), and "children omitted" is already the documented shape when neither source lists an item; a throttle on the secondary call should not cost the primary read. Do not auto-retry beyond the transport's existing policy. | owner |

## 9. Live-probe protocol (cases marked `live`)

Live cases run against a **dedicated junk professional account** during the phase
that owns them, following the QA review's protocol: stories-first smoke tests
(stories self-expire in 24 h — no cleanup problem), feed posts kept to a minimum
(published feed media **cannot be deleted via the API** — only `comment_enabled`
can be toggled), findings recorded back into this file replacing the `[verify]`
markers with `[verified <date>]` + observed behavior.

**The protocol is executable.** It is not a checklist to work through by hand:
[`scripts/live-probe.mjs`](../scripts/live-probe.mjs) implements it as **26 probes
across 7 lanes**, each declaring in an `answers:` field which corner case it
discharges. Run it, then transcribe its findings into this file — that is the whole
Lane E workflow.

```bash
npm run build                              # the probe imports the built core seam
node scripts/live-probe.mjs                # read-only: lanes `read` + `discovery`
node scripts/live-probe.mjs --allow-writes --image-url https://…/probe.jpg
node scripts/live-probe.mjs --only comment-length-ladder    # one probe, or one lane
```

**Three separate consent flags, escalating, none implied by default.** The ordering
is the safety design, not ergonomics:

| Flag | Unlocks | Why it is its own flag |
|---|---|---|
| *(none)* | `read`, `discovery` | Nothing is created. Safe on any account. |
| `--allow-writes` | `container`, `story`, `comment` | Stories expire in 24 h and comments are deletable — recoverable. |
| `--allow-feed-post` | `feed` | A published feed post **cannot be deleted via the API**. Permanent, forever, on that account. |
| `--allow-token-refresh` | `auth` | Rotates the live credential and **rewrites the XDG env file**. If the old token dies, concurrent instances break. |

Both `--allow-feed-post` and `--allow-token-refresh` imply `--allow-writes`; neither
is implied by it. A gated lane is reported as skipped with its reason rather than
silently omitted, so a run's report always states what it did *not* do.

Each of the five still-open cases below is discharged by a named probe:

| Case | Probe | Lane |
|---|---|---|
| CC-AUTH-14 | `refresh-old-token-fate` | `auth` (3rd flag) |
| CC-PUB-4 | `double-publish` | `story` |
| CC-PUB-11 | `caption-at-cap`, `caption-over-cap` | `container` |
| CC-COM-6 | `comment-length-ladder` | `comment` |
| CC-INS-4 | `insights-timezone` | `read` (no flag) |

The two docs-closed cases (CC-PUB-6, CC-COM-5) also have confirmation-only probes
(`mixed-carousel-container`, `hide-own-comment`) — they are not expected to change
the verdict, only to catch the documentation being wrong.

Note what the lane ordering buys: everything answerable read-only runs *first*, so a
run that dies halfway has already banked CC-INS-4 and the whole PCA/hashtag lane
without having written anything. CC-COM-6's ladder brackets an undocumented cap by
bisection and stops on a spam block (CC-COM-4) rather than reporting the block as a
length answer.

### Open `[verify]` register — triaged 2026-07-30 against official Meta docs

**Closed from public documentation** (no live call needed; see the rows above for
the citations):

- **CC-PUB-6** — mixed image/video carousels are explicitly allowed, max 10
  children. Only the minimum-of-2 is undocumented and is ours to enforce.
- **CC-COM-5** — hide rules are documented: an owner's own comments on their own
  media always display despite `hide=true`, and live-video comments are
  unsupported.

**Still open — each now names the call that discharges it:**

| Case | The call that settles it |
|---|---|
| CC-AUTH-14 | Refresh, then replay `GET /me?fields=user_id` with the **old** token |
| CC-PUB-4 | `POST /{ig-id}/media_publish` twice with one `creation_id` |
| CC-PUB-11 | `POST /{ig-id}/media` with a 2,200-code-point caption containing non-BMP emoji |
| CC-COM-6 | `POST /{comment-id}/replies` bisecting the message length |
| CC-INS-4 | `GET /{ig-id}/insights?metric=reach&period=day&metric_type=time_series` across a UTC midnight boundary |

Plus the M1 hashtag/PCA probe from [auth.md](auth.md) §5. Note that the hashtag
**budget** side of that probe no longer needs guessing:
`GET /{ig-user-id}/recently_searched_hashtags` reports the real 7-day usage
server-side (see [operations.md](operations.md) §1).

## 10. Open owner decisions — consolidated register

Twenty-four of the cases above end in an explicit **owner decision**: the behaviour is
measured, pinned by test, and deliberately left alone because changing it costs
something the code cannot weigh — a published tool surface, a documented CLI
contract, or bytes on the wire. Scattered across 150 rows they are easy to lose, so
they are gathered here with a one-line statement of the call and the recommendation
I would make if asked. **Nothing in this table is a bug report.** Every row is
already the current, tested behaviour; the register exists so the choice stays a
choice.

| Case | The decision to make | Recommendation |
|---|---|---|
| CC-AUTH-15 | Path-B's `appsecret_proof` is static for the token's lifetime — adopt the time-bound form (`appsecret_proof` + `appsecret_time`) or accept indefinite replay of an observed proof? | **Adopt it.** One extra parameter, bounds the replay window, and Graph already supports it. The cheapest security win left in the repo. |
| CC-AUTH-17 | `cli/login.ts` calls the only **unversioned** Graph URL in the project while `core/refresh.ts` goes through `buildUrl` and gets `/v25.0/`. Which form is right for each endpoint? | **Settle it with the live probe (T-E1), then make both deliberate.** Do not unify on a guess — an unversioned endpoint that Meta documents as unversioned is correct, and pinning it to `v25.0` would be the regression. |
| CC-AUTH-18 | `envVarFor('Second-Studio')` yields `IG_PROFILE_SECOND-STUDIO_APP_SECRET`, which a `.env` file accepts and `export` refuses. Rename the scheme or keep it? | **Keep it.** Renaming breaks every existing named profile for a case the operator creates by choosing the profile name. Worth a sentence in the profiles docs, not a migration. |
| CC-AUTH-23 | A dead token that arrives with no recognised `code` falls through to the HTTP-status fallback, becomes `upstream`, and is retried three times on GETs. Consult `error.type` as a tie-breaker? | **Yes** — read `type === 'OAuthException'` immediately before the status fallback and map it to `auth`. It narrows nothing that works today and stops a dead token from looking like a flaky server. **APPLIED 2026-09-19** exactly there: rule 3 in `deriveKind`, after the code ladder and before the status fallback, on an exact case-sensitive match; six tests in `test/core/errors.test.ts`, plus the not-retried pin one layer up in `test/core/http.test.ts`. |
| CC-PUB-23 | An `.mp4` handed to `imageUrl` or `coverUrl` draws no warning, because the one sentence available names the wrong problem. Add a second, video-aware message? | **Add it**, when the surface next moves. Silence is the right answer only until there is a sentence that is true. |
| CC-PUB-24 | `imageUrlFormatWarning` takes `lastIndexOf('.')` over the whole path, so a dot in a directory segment yields junk. Read the last path segment first? | **Yes** — two lines, strictly more accurate, and it costs nothing today. The reason to wait is that a stricter reader invites "no warning means it is a JPEG", which the docstring refuses; the fix must not change that wording. **APPLIED 2026-09-19**: the file name is read before its last dot, and the docstring still refuses to let silence mean JPEG. |
| CC-PUB-25 | Four things at once: (a) applied payloads carry no `warnings` key, (b) two same-extension offenders produce two identical lines, (c) the prose still says "feed images" on story and reel paths, (d) videos need their own hint (CC-PUB-23). | **Do (c) now, (a) and (d) together at the next surface change, and leave (b).** The wrong word is free to fix; a new output field is not; de-duplicating (b) loses the count, which is the only information the second line carries. **(c) APPLIED 2026-09-19** — the hint names images (feed, story, and reel cover). (a), (b) and (d) stand as decided. |
| CC-DATA-16 | The last page overflowing `IG_MAX_ITEMS` returns `truncated: true` with no cursor and no note. Does that stop deserve a note of its own? | **Yes, eventually** — and unlike CC-DATA-11's path the note here has real advice to give ("raise `maxItems`"). Pinned as `note === undefined` so the two stops cannot quietly merge in the meantime. |
| CC-DATA-17 | Two raw `TypeError`s escape `src/core/host.ts` without becoming an `InstagramError` (a non-string `path`; an allowlisted host with surrounding whitespace). Widen the CC-DATA-14 fix? | **Yes** — same fix shape, and neither is reachable from an in-repo call site, so the change is safe. Note this **fails closed**: what is wrong is the shape of the failure, not the safety of it. **APPLIED 2026-09-19**: both conversions are guarded inside `buildUrl`, which now throws nothing but `InstagramError({ kind: 'validation' })`. |
| CC-DATA-30 | `IG_PRETTY_JSON` is honoured by 3 of the 28 tools while three env tables promise it without qualification. Thread `{ pretty: ctx.settings.prettyJson }` through the remaining 25 `json()` call sites so the documented promise becomes true, or narrow the documentation to the three account readers that actually obey it? | **Thread it through.** The usual argument for compactness — pretty-printing a listing roughly doubles its token cost for indentation a model gains nothing from (`test/tools/media.test.ts`) — is an argument about the *default*, and the default is `false` and stays `false` either way. The knob is opt-in; an operator who turns it on has already accepted the cost, and they are almost always a human reading stdio traffic, for whom partial support is worse than none because it looks like a broken config loader rather than a design choice. The cheaper alternative is honest but leaves a knob whose name means one thing and whose behaviour means another. **Implemented 2026-09-25 (CC-DATA-112).** |
| CC-CFG-11 | A relative `configDir` resolves against the process CWD, so a caller of `writeCredentials` deposits a `0600` credential file wherever it was run (there is no `login --config-dir` flag — corrected 2026-09-23). Reject relative paths, resolve them against the config home, or keep? | **Resolve against the config home and say so in `--help`.** Rejecting breaks an operator who means it; resolving silently retargets a path they spelled out — which is why the fix has to be announced, not just applied. |
| CC-CFG-12 | Env-file write durability: no `fsync`, an orphaned `.tmp` after a failed rename, a symlinked target replaced rather than followed, raw `node:fs` errors reaching the operator. | **Fix the error surface, skip the rest.** Wrapping `ENOSPC`/`EACCES` in an `InstagramError` that names the path is pure gain; an `fsync` on every `login` buys durability against a power loss in a millisecond window that costs a real disk barrier. None of the four can corrupt or disclose a credential on a successful write. **APPLIED 2026-09-19**, error surface only: one `storeFailure` wrapper names the store, the `code` and the remedy and keeps the raw error as `cause`; the other three are left as decided. **REVISED 2026-09-23:** `fsync` applied after all (CC-CFG-54) — a zero-length store loses every profile, and `login`/`refresh` are too rare for the barrier to matter; the orphaned `.tmp` was closed by CC-CFG-48; the symlinked store stays as decided. **REVISED 2026-09-24:** the symlinked store is followed after all (CC-CFG-68) — replacing the link silently cut a dotfiles-managed store off from every later token. |
| CC-CFG-13 | Booleans are case-insensitive and enums are not, and a mistyped `IG_*` name is a silent default rather than a startup error. | **APPLIED 2026-09-19**, both halves as recommended: enums fold case and yield the canonical spelling; unrecognised `IG_*` names are listed in one `warn` record at startup, assembled in `src/index.ts` from the half each of `core/settings.ts`, `core/config.ts` and `mcp/registry.ts` publishes and pins. Names only, never values. |
| CC-CFG-14 | `isLoopbackHost` accepts 1–3 digit octets, so `127.0.0.01` passes and `127.0.0.0001` does not — and the comment justifying the refusal argued an octal reading that applies equally to the accepted spellings. | **Comment half APPLIED 2026-09-02**; the check is unchanged. The comment in `test/core/settings.test.ts` now states the shape rule it actually implements. What remains open is whether to tighten the shape to canonical octets — recommendation: **do not**, everything that passes is inside `127.0.0.0/8`, which is what the bind decision turns on. |
| CC-PROC-9 | `instagram_set_comments_enabled` is a write defined in `src/tools/comments.ts` and tagged `media`, so **no** `IG_PACKAGES_DENY` incantation isolates just this write. Move the tag? | **Move it to `comments`** at the next surface change — it is where an operator looks for it — and until then keep the mapping pinned by test so the drift stays deliberate. `IG_PACKAGES_READONLY=media` is the only read-only control that neutralises it today. |
| CC-PROC-16 | `GRAPH_ID_PATTERN` refuses an id over 64 characters or containing `.` or `:`, with no env escape hatch — only a new release. | **Leave it, and watch it.** The bound is already ~2× the longest id observed, but the id space is Meta's; if this ever fires on a real id it is a worse failure than the injection it guards against, which is exactly why it is recorded as `live`. |
| CC-PROC-21 | Key-name masking reaches `structuredContent` only — by the time `redactResult` runs, `json()` has stringified the payload and the keys are gone from the text block. | **Leave it.** Value-based and shape-based masking still cover both blocks; closing it means re-parsing content blocks as JSON to re-serialise them, which is a worse trade than the residual. The docstrings state the asymmetry rather than glossing it. |
| CC-PROC-25 | All seven writes travel in the **query string** — caption, `user_tags`, `message`, `image_url`, alongside `access_token` — while the documented `body` sink of `IgRequestOptions` is dead code. | **Move the writes onto the body and validate live.** Doing neither is the bad outcome: a long caption percent-encodes 3–4× and fails as an opaque 414 that names no parameter. If the move is refused, the field must be marked reserved so it stops implying a path that has never met real Graph behaviour. |
| CC-PROC-29 | `doctor` decides ANSI colour from the real `process.env` and `process.stdout.isTTY`, not from its injected `env` seam. | **Leave it.** Colour is a property of the attached terminal, not of the configuration being diagnosed; widening the seam makes `env` mean two things, and narrowing `NO_COLOR` to the seam breaks the convention. A one-line change either way, the day the owner says so. |
| CC-PUB-35 | Should the publishing input schemas stop de-duplicating structurally identical URL fields, so that every field's own `description` survives a strict draft-07 client? Two mechanisms are available: pass `$refStrategy: 'none'` wherever the schema is rendered, or give each URL field its own zod instance so no twin exists to point at. Either one changes the published bytes for four fields across three tools. | **Give each field its own instance.** It is a local, readable change confined to the specs, it needs no knowledge of the renderer's options, and it makes the published schema say what the spec author wrote — which is the whole contract of a `describe()` call. `$refStrategy: 'none'` would also work but hides the reason in a rendering flag far from the field it rescues. Whichever is chosen, the fix must land together with the `deepEqual` pin update in `test/tools/publishing.test.ts`, and the recursive comment `$ref`s must be left alone. |
| CC-DATA-32 | Should `instagram_discover_business` declare `required: ['id']`, and should `instagram_get_account_insights.paging` declare its actual shape instead of publishing an open empty object? Adding `required` tightens a published output contract, which is a breaking change for any client that already generated types from it. | **Add `required: ['id']` to the business output; leave `paging` open for now.** The `id` case is settled by precedent — `instagram_get_account` already makes exactly that promise for exactly that reason, and a `discover_business` result without an `id` is not a result. `paging` is different: it is Meta's cursor envelope, its shape is theirs to change, and declaring it would be pinning a foreign contract the server does not control. Fence it in prose rather than in the schema. |
| CC-PUB-36 | Should the eight published URL inputs advertise `pattern: '^https://'` (by chaining `.regex()` alongside the existing `.refine()`), accepting that the published rule is deliberately weaker than the enforced one and that two different messages then exist for the same field? | **Yes, add the regex — and give it the same message.** The pattern's job is to steer the model before the call, not to be the enforcement; the refine stays as the real gate. Chain the regex with an explicit `message` equal to `'must be a well-formed https:// URL'` so the regex-first failure reads identically to the refine's, which keeps all ten existing whole-clause pins green and prevents a second vocabulary from growing. Land it together with the `deepEqual` contract updates in `test/tools/publishing.test.ts`, and decide it alongside [[CC-PUB-35]] — both change the same eight fields' published bytes, so they should move in one commit. |
| CC-PROC-19 | Should `src/core/errors.ts` and `src/core/redact.ts` share one frozen token-shape list instead of holding two byte-identical copies guarded by a behavioural drift test? | **Yes — extract a Layer-0 `src/core/token-shapes.ts`.** The drift guard at `test/core/errors.test.ts:659` compares the two copies only over the fixed `TOKEN_CORPUS`, so it is corpus-bound by construction and any drift with no fixture witness passes in silence. That blind spot was real, not theoretical: five rewrites of the `IG` and `EAA` patterns survived the full 22-file set until 2026-09-22 (see [[CC-CFG-26]] for the sibling class), two of which — dropping `-` or digits from the IG body class — ship the tail of a live token to the model in the clear. The corpus is now armed, but a shared list removes the class rather than fencing it; `errors.ts` must be able to import it without pulling in `createRedactor`. |
| CC-CFG-26 | Should `DEFAULT_REDIRECT_URI` carry a direct literal pin, and should the older help-row expectation that interpolates it be deduplicated against the new literal one? | **Yes to the literal pin; the dedupe is cosmetic.** Mutating the constant's path component today kills by hanging the capture test and cascading 75 cancellations rather than by any assertion reading the path — which is both a poor diagnostic and the exact shape [[CC-PROC-89]] warns about. One `assert.equal(DEFAULT_REDIRECT_URI, 'http://127.0.0.1:8723/callback')` turns that cascade into a one-line failure. The duplicate help-row expectation is now harmless, since the new literal row holds the same text independently. |

### Three decisions settled in passing

These were settled in passing, recorded only in the session journal, and are keyed
by module rather than by case number. They are listed here so the register is
complete; each earns a `CC-` row the day it is acted on — as the first of them
since has.

- **`src/mcp/write-mode.ts` journals the `already_published` and `in_progress`
  arms as applied writes.** `withWriteGate` records whenever `result.isError !==
  true`, so a container that was already published — a call that publishes
  nothing — appends a journal record indistinguishable from a real publish, with
  the *container* id in `targetId` rather than a media id. **Recommendation:**
  carry the returned `status` into the record. The journal is local, append-only
  and owner-only, so a new field costs nothing, and without it the audit trail
  cannot answer "did this actually post?". **Applied 2026-09-19 — now
  CC-PROC-73 in §8.** `perform` reports the outcome
  beside the target id, `recordWrite` writes it into the line, and the key stays
  absent for a write whose action already names its only outcome. Pinned at both
  levels 2026-09-22; the row carries the detail.
- **The caption limit counts code points.** Validation measures `[...caption].length`,
  which is right for non-BMP emoji under a UTF-16 reading and wrong if Instagram
  counts grapheme clusters or UTF-8 bytes. **Recommendation:** keep code points and
  let CC-PUB-11's live probe settle it — a limit that is wrong in the safe
  direction rejects a caption Instagram would have taken, which is a visible,
  correctable failure rather than a silent truncation.
- **`igIdOf` falls back to `me`.** With no `accountId` on the profile — absent, or
  blank and normalised away by `clean()` — every write targets whichever account
  the token belongs to. **Recommendation:** leave it; `me` is what a single-account
  install means. It is recorded because on a multi-profile install the fallback is
  silent, and the apply-preview is the only place an operator would notice.
